Presiding Officer, 
C.G.I.T-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 30 OF 202 
PARTIES: Pramod Kanu, 
Son of Late Dahu Kanu 


[भाग ॥---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 506l 


Vs 

Management of Khas Kajora Colliery, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, learned Advocate 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 0/04/2025 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (/4 of 947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-2202/64/202-IR(CM-ID) dated 4/2/202l has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Khas Kajora Colliery of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their workmen for 
adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“Whether the action of the Management of Khas Kajora Colliery, Kajora Area, M/s. E.C.Ltd. not providing 
employment to Sri Promod Kanu son of Late Dahu Kanu, Ex-CCM Driver, U.M.No. 557030 and 
compensation to the wife of deceased employee is justified or not? If not, what relief the dependent of Late 
Dahu Kanu is entitled to?” 


॥ On receiving Order No. 7,-2202/64/202-॥२((५-॥) dated 4/2/202] from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 30 of 202 was registered on 
4.2.202/0.07.2022 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing 
them to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims. 


2. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed written statement on behalf of the dependent 
son of the deceased employee on 08/08/2022. Management of ECL filed their written statement on !7/0/2023. In 
gist, fact of the union’s case is that Dahu Kanu, an employee of ECL bearing UM No. 55030 was posted at Khas 
Kajora Colliery as CCM Driver. He died in harness on 48/05/996. Under clause 9.3.2 of NCWA-VI one dependent 
of the deceased employee is entitled to get employment. Accordingly wife of the deceased employee applied for 
providing employment to her and submitted necessary documents. Management of the company did not provide 
employment to the wife of the deceased employee. Discouraging female dependent from claiming employment, 
management of ECL suggested to the wife of the deceased to apply for employment to the dependent son, Pramod 
Kanu. Wife of Late Dahu Kanu thereafter submitted another application for providing employment to her son, 
Pramod Kanu. Management of ECL processed the employment proposal, held screening test at the colliery level and 
Area level. Pramod Kanu was thereafter referred for his medical examination which was to be held by the Initial 
Medical Examination Board. The IME Board declared the age of Pramod Kanu as seventeen to twenty two years 
which was calculated as nineteen years and six months at the time of Initial Medical Examination. Pramod Kanu also 
submitted several documents including his Birth Registration Certificate, wherein his Date of Birth is recorded as 
0I/07/982 which implies that his age was sixteen years on the date of death of Dahu Kanu. He produced his School 
Leaving Certificate/Transfer Certificate in which his Date of Birth is mentioned as 0/07/982. Janma Kundali 
produced revealed the Date of Birth as 0i/07/982. In the Service Record Excerpt of Dahu Kanu age of his son, Dahu 
Kanu was recorded as two years in the year 987. On request of Pramod Kanu for holding his medical examination 
again, the Apex Medical Board on examining Pramod Kanu assessed his age between twenty to twenty five years as 
on 2/03/2007 i.e. fourteen years and eight months on the date of death of employee. According to the union age of 
the person was sixteen years on the date of death of his father as mentioned above and according to report of the 
Medical Board his age was fourteen years and eight months i.e. near to fifteen years. It is further stated that on the 
date of submitting application for employment, age of Pramod Kanu was above fifteen years and according to the 
provision of clause 9.5.0 of NCWA-VI if age of the dependent son is twelve years on the date of death of employee, 
his name would be maintained in live roster for providing employment and wife of the deceased employee is entitled 
to get Monetary Compensation. It is contended by the union that according to the provision of NCWA Coal India 
Limited is legally bound to provide employment to one dependent of the deceased employee and the claim for 
employment is not based upon compassionate ground. It is disclosed that the union raised Industrial Dispute before 
the ALC (C), Raniganj on 2/07/20i0 which ended in failure and FOC report was sent to the Secretary, Ministry of 
Labour and Employment vide letter No. I/05/20l0-ALCR but unfortunately neither the dispute was referred for 
adjudication nor did the Ministry deny such reference. Concerned union thereafter approached the Ministry afresh for 
referring the Industrial Dispute to the Tribunal. In the Written Statement reference has been made to similar situation 


5062 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


where in a case of Late Sitaram Singh, Hon’ble High Court passed the order and employment was provided to the 
dependent son, though his age was less than fifteen years at the time of death of the employee. Union prayed for 
providing employment to Pramod Kanu as dependent son of Late Dahu Kanu and Monetary Compensation to the wife 
of the deceased employee from the date of his death with all consequential benefits. 


3. Management contested the case by filing written statement through Agent, Khas Kajora Colliery, ECL. It is 
admitted that Dahu Kanu was a permanent employee of Khas Kajora Colliery and expired on !8/05/998. According 
to the management, Chandra Kala Devi, wife of Late Dahu Kanu at first applied for providing employment to her in 
place of her deceased husband by filing application on 25/0/2000. Her claim was processed. Management of Khas 
Kajora Colliery forwarded a proposal on 8/04/2000 to the Competent Authority for providing Monetary 
Compensation to Chandra Kala Devi in lieu of employment under death scheme. In their internal letter dated 
23/08/2000 issued from the Area to the management of the colliery, it was specified that ordinarily no approval would 
be given for belated claim nor would the same be processed. The case file was resubmitted at Area level on 
2/09/2000 which was returned on 9/05/2003 with a communication that as directed by Headquarters, the colliery 
was requested to process the case of Late Dahu Kanu, Ex-Machine Driver of Khas Kajora Colliery which was claimed 
by the dependent after six months from the date of death of deceased employee for employment after observing all 
formalities. It is further stated that Pramod Kanu claimed employment as son of the deceased employee by submitting 
application on 30/i/2003. A screening of Pramod Kanu was held on 6/02/2004 and relevant papers submitted by 
the son of the deceased were sent to the Area vide letter dated 6/8.03.2004. Initial Medical Examination of Pramod 
Kanu was held on 7/2/2004 where it was found that he was below fifteen years at the time of death of his father and 
the management of Kajora Area informed the Agent by letter dated 04/02/2005 that the claim for employment of 
Pramod Kanu could not be forwarded to Head Quarters. An application was submitted by Pramod Kanu for holding 
his medical examination once again. Initial Medical Examination of Pramod Kanu was done by the Area on 
7/2/2004 and his age was assessed as seventeen to twenty two years and he did not produce his Birth /Birth 
Registration Certificate before the Area Medical Board at that time. He arranged his Birth Registration Certificate 
subsequently which was issued on |8/09/2005 by the authority. On 09/03/2007, Pramod Kanu was referred for his re- 
medical examination by the Apex Medical Board for assessment of his age. On the basis of letter dated 30/05/2007 
issued by the Dy. Chief Personnel Manager of Kajora Area, it was communicated to the Agent, Khas Kajora Colliery 
that earlier Initial Medical Examination of Pramod Kanu was done by the Area Medical Board on [7/2/2004 and his 
age was assessed as seventeen to twenty two years i.e. nineteen years and six months as on [7/2/2004 and on 
referring to the Apex Medical Board for re-examination of his age, Pramod Kanu’s age was assessed by the Apex 
Medical Board as twenty to twenty five years i.e. twenty two years and six months as on [2/03/2007. According to 
the management age of Pramod Kanu was less than fifteen years at the time of death of Late Dahu Kanu and 
according to the rule of the company, son of a deceased employee had to be above fifteen years of age for being 
eligible for entering his name in the live roster for employment. According to the management, Pramod Kanu was 
found ineligible for employment against the death of his deceased father. Furthermore, dispute has been raised more 
than twenty one years after date of death of Late Dahu Kanu and has been referred to this Tribunal in the year 202], 
i.e. after more than twenty three years of death of the employee as such the Industrial Dispute raised is a stale one and 
same needs to be dismissed. Management referred to a decision of Hon’ble Supreme Court of India in a case of M/s. 
Eastern Coalfields Limited vs. Anil Badyakar and Others where the Hon’ble Could disapproved the employment 
on compassionate ground after passage of long years. It was observed that the dispute was a stale and the same was 
not considered for conciliation. 


4. In support of the case, Pramod Kanu filed an affidavit-in-chief and examined himself as Workman Witness- 
I. In his evidence witness deposed that his father died on 48/05/998 while he was in service of the company. He 
produced the following documents:- 


(i) copy of Service Identity Card of Dahu Kanu is produced as Exhibit ४-।. 

(ii) copy of service record of his father in two pages is collectively produced as Exhibit W-2. 
(iii) copy of death certificate of his father is produced as Exhibit W-3. 

(iv) copy of letter dated 07/02/2000 issued to his mother is produced as Exhibit W-4. 

(v) copy of reply submitted by his mother is produced as Exhibit W-S. 

(vi) copy of his application submitted for employment is produced as Exhibit W-6. 

(vii) copy of letter issued by the Dy. Personnel Manager is produced as Exhibit W-7. 

(viii) copy of reply submitted by him against letter dated 04/02/2005 is produced as Exhibit W-8. 
(ix) copy of his mother’s letter dated 9/[0/2005 is produced as Exhibit W-9. 


(x) copy of letter dated 4/0/2006 informing that his age could not be reassessed by Area Medical Board is 
produced as Exhibit W-0. 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 5063 


(xi) copy of letter dated 08/06/2007 regarding age assessment for the second time is produced as Exhibit W-]. 
(xii) copy of representation submitted by the workers’ union dated 27/0/2007 is produced as Exhibit W-2. 
(xiii) | copy of another representation dated [4/03/2008 of the union is produced as Exhibit W-3. 


(xiv) copy of internal letter dated 6/05/2009 whereby company regretted the proposal for re-medical of Pramod 
Kanu is produced as Exhibit ४४-4. 


(xv) copy of his Transfer Certificate issued by Utkramit Madhya Vidyalaya is produced as Exhibit ४७४७-5. 


(xvi) copy of order passed by Assistant Labour Commissioner (C) Raniganj at Durgapur which resulted in failure 
of Conciliation, which is collectively produced as Exhibit ४४-6. 


(xvii) copy of letter dated !4/08/20l9 issued by ALC(C), Raniganj at Durgapur for raising dispute afresh is 
produced as Exhibit ४४-7. 


In cross-examination workman witness deposed that his mother submitted application within two months from date of 
death of his father and he applied for employment in the year 2003. Witness stated that he was eighteen years of age 
in the year 2003. It may be gathered from evidence of WW-I that his father was murdered at his native place and his 
mother was mentally disturbed for which some delay might have occurred. It transpires from cross-examination of the 
witness that he appeared for Initial Medical Examination in the year 2004 and submitted another application in the 
year 2005 for his medical examination afresh. Apex Medial Board held re-medical examination on [2/03/2007 which 
assessed the age of Pramod Kanu as twenty to twenty five years. In the later part of cross-examination it is stated that 
his mother died on 6/0/202! during pendency of this dispute. 


5. Mr. Proloy Dasgupta is examined as Management Witness-I and filed his affidavit-in-chief. In the affidavit- 
in-chief witness deposed that initially wife of Late Dahu Kanu submitted application for providing employment to her 
but the witness was unable to produce copy of the application. It is deposed that application of the wife of Late Dahu 
Kanu was not considered on the ground that the application was filed after expiry of six months from date of death of 
her husband. Witness further stated that Pramod Kanu was less than fifteen years of age at the time of death of his 
father and his name could not be maintained in live roster which was communicated to him by the Agent vide letter 
dated 04/02/2005. According to the MW-I, the petitioner claiming employment was underage at the time of his 
father’s death due to which prayer for maintaining his name in the live roster of the company was not allowed. 
Management Witness-I produced the following documents in support of their case:- 


(i) copy of application submitted by Pramod Kanu on 28/ [/2003 is produced as Exhibit M-I. 
(ii) copy of service excerpt of Dahu Kanu is produced as Exhibit M-2. 
(iii) copy of letter dated 23/08/2000 is produced as Exhibit M-3. 


(iv) copy of letter dated 04/02/2005 of Deputy Chief Personnel Manager issued to the Agent, Khas Kajora 
Colliery is produced as Exhibit M-4. In the letter it was informed that Pramod Kanu was below fifteen years of age at 
the time of his father’s death. 


(v) copy of letter dated 9//2005 submitted by Pramod Kanu praying for assessing his age by Medical Board 
is produced as Exhibit M-5. 


(vi) copy of letter dated 30/05/2007 informing the findings of Apex Medical Board is produced as Exhibit M-6. 


In cross-examination, witness deposed that during pendency of application submitted by the wife of deceased, the son 
made a fresh application for employment for which employment proposal of the wife was discontinued. Witness 
admitted that no Monetary Compensation was granted to the wife of deceased employee despite her application was 
submitted claiming monetary compensation. Witness admitted Exhibit W-9 i.e. application submitted by the wife 
praying for payment of Monetary Compensation. In course of cross-examination Management Witness deposed that 
management considers the age assessed by the Medical Board and not the school certificate. Witness denied the 
suggestion that management committed illegality by not providing employment to the son of the deceased. 


6. Mr. Rakesh Kumar, union representative argued that Chandra Kala Devi, widow of the deceased employee 
submitted her application for her employment on !0/02/2000. In her letter she disclosed that after death of her 
husband she was in a state of mental shock and was not in normal position to take suitable decision at that time. She 
prayed for her employment after explaining the delay seeking help for providing employment. Management did not 
process her prayer for employment. During pendency of such application, Pramod Kanu, the minor son of the 
deceased employee on attaining majority submitted an application (Exhibit W-6) dated 26/[ [/2003 praying for his 
employment in place of his deceased father. Management regretted the prayer for employment on the ground that 
Pramod Kanu was less than fifteen years at the time of death of his father (Exhibit W-7). Mr. Kumar submitted that 
Pramod Kanu made a fresh application praying for his re-medical examination by the Apex Medical Board and stated 
that he was sixteen years of age as on |8/05/l998. Manager of Khas Kajora Colliery in letter dated 4/0/2006 
informed Pramod Kanu that Area Medical Board on 7/02/2004 assessed his age as seventeen to twenty two years 


5064 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


and the Birth Registration Certificate which was not produced before the Area Medical Board and was subsequently 
obtained on 8/09/2005 could not be considered. It is further stated in the letter that since age of Pramod Kanu was 
assessed by the Area Medical Board, the same could not be re-examined on the basis of documents not considered to 
be authentic. Mr. Kumar referring to Exhibit W-I, a letter issued by the Manager, Khas Kajora Colliery dated 
08/06/2007 submitted that Pramod Kanu was referred for reassessment of age by the Apex Medical Board and his age 
was assessed as twenty to twenty five years i.e. twenty two years and six months as on |2/03/2007. It is argued that if 
the age assessed by the Medical Board is considered to be correct then average age of Pramod Kanu would be twenty 
two years and six months as on |2/03/2007. Therefore, applying the provision of clause 9.5.0 (iii) of NCWA-VI 
applicable to the workman and the management from 0/07/996 to 30/06/200l, which provides that in a case of 
death if no employment is offered and male dependent is twelve years or more in age, he will be kept in live roster 
and will be provided employment commensurating with his skill and qualification when he attains eighteen years of 
age. Mr. Kumar argued that ECL is bound by NCWA and was responsible to maintain name of the dependent son of 
Late Dahu Kanu in live roster and provide employment to him on his attaining majority. It is argued that in the instant 
case management has acted in an arbitrary manner by not providing employment to the dependent son and not paying 
Monetary Compensation to the widow of the deceased during her lifetime. 


7. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management of ECL argued that the Industrial Dispute is liable to be 
dismissed on two counts. Firstly, the application for employment was made after inordinate delay of fifteen years and 
secondly the age of the dependent son should not be less than fifteen years for the purpose of maintaining his name in 
live roster. Since the age of Pramod Kanu was thirteen years and eight months at the time of death of his father on 
8/05/998 management had no option but to disapprove the prayer for employment due as petitioner son 
was below age. 


8. I have considered the pleading, evidence adduced and documents produced. Admittedly Dahu Kanu was a 
permanent employment of Khas Kajora Colliery, ECL and died on 8/05/998 while he was in service of the 
company. Chandra Kala Devi, his wife at first submitted application on !0/02/2000 claiming employment. In reply to 
management’s letter No. KKC/P&IR/C-6/46/50 dated 07/02/2000 where after two years from death of employee, 
management asked wife of the deceased that no claim paper for employment had been filed and she filed her 
application only on 25/0/2000 after one and half years as such prayer would not be entertained as per circular and 
guideline of the company, wife of the deceased in letter dated 0/02/2000, explained the delay, disclosing that after 
death of her husband she was in a shock for long time and was not in normal position to take suitable decision. It 
appears from the materials on record that management did not consider the prayer for employment of the wife. 
However, on the basis of application dated 26//2003 by Pramod Kanu (Exhibit W-6) he was referred for his 
medical examination and by letter dated 09/02/2005 (Exhibit W-7), Pramod Kanu was informed by the management 
of Khas Kajora Colliery that on assessment of age by Initial Medical Examination on [7/2/2004 his age was assessed 
between seventeen years to twenty two years i.e. nineteen years six months as on |7/2/2004. It was further informed 
that his prayer could not be forwarded to Head Quarters as he was less than fifteen years of age. Written statement of 
the management reveals that on the basis of application for employment submitted by Pramod Kanu on 30/| ।/2003, 
screening at colliery level was held on !6/02/2004 and his medical examination was held on [7/2/2004. It is evident 
that the application of Pramod Kanu had been entertained and acted upon by the management. He was also referred to 
the Apex Medical Board on 09/03/2007 for his age assessment where his age was reassessed as twenty to twenty five 
years as on |2/03/2007. Management Witness relying upon the age assessed by the Apex Medical Board on 
2/03/2007 (Exhibit M-6) deposed that petitioner was underage at the time of his father’s death and the prayer for 
employment was not allowed. Witness admitted in cross-examination that management did not respond to the 
application of wife praying for employment and Monetary Compensation and no communication was made by the 
management disclosing reasons for not considering the prayers. I find from the evidence that management did not 
communicate their decision to Pramod Kanu regarding his claim for employment. At this juncture it would be 
appropriate to consider the provision of NCWA applicable to a case where death took place on 8/05/998. It appears 
that NCWA-VI which was signed on 23/2/2000 has been made applicable from 0/07/996 to 30/06/200I. In the 
present case management has assessed age of Pramod Kanu as twenty two years and six months as on [2/03/2007 and 
if said the assessment is considered for the purpose of deciding age of the dependent son then on the death of his 
father, he was thirteen years and eight months of age. According to the provision of clause 9.5.0(ii) name of a male 
dependent has to be maintained in live roster if he is above twelve years of age on the date of death of his father and 
he would be provided employment on attaining age of eighteen years. In the instant case the management has 
committed a clear violation of the provision of NCWA-VI where the management did not take into consideration the 
age of the dependent son for the purpose of maintaining his name in live roster for granting employment. As long as a 
son is a minor and does not have legal competence to decide as to within what time application for employment 
should be submitted, the argument of the management that application for employment was submitted after a long 
delay cannot be accepted. 


9. In the present case management faulted on their part by not providing Monetary Compensation to the wife of 
the deceased though application was filed within one year and eight months. It was the duty of the management to 
extend the benefits of Monetary Compensation and employment to the family of the deceased employee according to 
the terms of settlement. From the evidence of WW-I I find that wife of the deceased, Chandra Kala Devi has died on 


[भाग ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 5065 


6/0/202] as such no Monetary Compensation can be paid to her. Pramod Kanu, dependent son is found eligible and 
entitled to get employment in place of his father in terms of clause 9.5.0(iii) of NCWA-VI. Management of ECL is 
directed to take appropriate step to process the prayer of employment of Pramod Kanu, son of Late Dahu Kanu 
commensurating with his skill and qualification. Management shall complete the process for granting employment to 
the son within one month from date of communication of Award, if the process is not complete within the stipulated 
time, management shall be liable to provide compensation of Rs. 30,000/ per month to the dependent son till 
employment is provided to him. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute raised by the union is decided in favour of the petitioner on contest. Management is 
directed to provide employment to Pramod Kanu, son of Late Dahu Kanu within one month from the date of 
communication of Award, in default, management of ECL shall be liable to pay a compensation of Rs. 30,000/ per 
month to the dependent son till employment is provided to him. Let an Award be drawn up in light of my above 
findings and copies of the Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERJEE, Presiding Officer 


नई दिल्‍ली, 2 जुलाई, 2025

AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 ((4 of 947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-2002/24/2002-IR(C-D) dated 0/03/2003 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Swang Colliery of M/s. CCL and their workman through Area Secretary, N.C.O.E.A., 
Swang, Bokaro for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


“Whether the demand of the NCOEA from the management of CCL, Swang Colliery to give Shri 
Dasrath Singh, Driver Cat-V the benefits of VDA etc as per the N.C.D.C. rules is justified? If so to 
what relief is the workman concerned entitled and from what date?” 


2. On receiving order no. L-2002/24I/2002-IR(C-I) dated !0/03/2003 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 3] of 2003 was registered on 28.04.2003 and thereafter 
the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant 
documents in support of their claims and the witnesses. 


3. After issuance of regd. notice, Sri R.N. Ganguly, Ld. Advocate of workman appeared and filed letter of authority as 
also the written statement on behalf of the sponsoring union whereas Sri D.K. Verma, Ld. Advocate appeared from 
the side of the management. It further transpires that written statement and rejoinder has been filed by the 
management but when the case record was put up after a long gap, registered notices were again sent but the 


registered post was returned with remarks “अधूरा पता अतः वापस? and thereafter no step was taken from the side of 


the workman. Whereas Sri D.K. Verma, Ld. Advocate appeared from the side of the management but the workman 
never appeared before the Tribunal since 6.08.202I. 


4. On perusal of the entire case record it is transpires that the workman never appeared before this Tribunal for a 
period of 4 years which shows that the workman has lost his interest in this case and therefore, for the ends of justice, 
this case deserves to be dismissed for non prosecution. 


5. Hence, 
ORDERED 


that this case is hereby dismissed and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the above reference case. 
Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of India, New 
Delhi for information and notification. 


SACHINDRA KUMAR PANDEY, Presiding Officer

AWARD. 

In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (/4 of 947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-2002/6/200I-C-I dated 22/05/200] has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the employer i.e. 
management of Katras Area of M/s. BCCL and their workman through General Manager, National Coal Workers 
Congress, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 

“Whether the action of the management of East Katras Colliery M/s BCCL in dismissing Sri Dhiba 

Turi from the services of the Company w.e.f. 2.5.99 is justified? If not, to what relief is the concerned 

workman entitled?” 

2. On receiving order no. L-2002/6/200I-C-I dated 22/05/200! Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New 
Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 20 of 200! was registered on 20.06.200 and thereafter the 
notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant 
documents in support of their claims and the witnesses. 
3. After service of notice, Sri B.N. Singh, General Secretary of the Sponsoring Union appeared from the side of the 
workman and filed W/S and subsequently rejoinder also and Sri D.K. Verma, Advocate appeared from the side of the 
employer appeared and filed written statement from the management side. It further appears that management also led 
some evidence but workman failed to adduce any evidence, later on, a registered notice was sent on 30.09.2020 for 
appearance of the parties for further proceeding but none appeared from either side though Sri N. Nath, O.S Legal 
H.Q of management appeared on 0.06.2025. The workman did not appear and since the year 2020, the case record is 
running in dark which shows that the workman has lost his interest in this case and therefore, this Tribunal is of the 
opinion that the case deserves to be dismissed due to non prosecution. 
4. Hence, 
ORDERED 

that this case is hereby dismissed and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the above reference case.

Delhi for information and notification.

Their workman.

Appearances:

State : Jharkhand. Industry:-Coal 


Dated 0/06/2025 
AWARD. 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-2002/07/2004-IR(C-IT) dated 02/09/2004 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Sudamdih Incline Mine of M/s. BCCL and their workman through Joint Secretary, Dalit 
Mazdoor Sangh, Chandankyari, Bokaro for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


SCHEDULE 
“क्या भा को को लि, सुदामडीह इंक्लाइन माइन के प्रबंधतंत्र द्वारा कर्मकार श्री सत्य नारायण सहीस, माइनर लोडर को 
दिनांक 4/2/2002 & बर्खास्त किया जाना विधिवत, उचित एवं न्‍यायसंगत है? यदि नहीं तो कर्मकार किस राहत के 
पात्र हैं?” 


2. On receiving order no. L-200i2/07/2004- IR(C-D dated 02/09/2004 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 94 of 2004 was registered on 4.09.2004 and thereafter 
the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant 
documents in support of their claims and the witnesses. 


3. After issuance of regd. notice to the workman, the same returned with remarks “नहीं जानते @” and thereafter no 
step was taken on behalf of the workman. On 06.06.2025, Sri Dhiraj Kumar, Legal Inspector of management 
appeared. The case record shows that the registered notice that was sent to the workman returned with endorsement 
“नहीं जानते हे and thereafter no step was taken on behalf of the workman in this case. The case record shows that this 


case is of the year 2004 and since then no step has been taken from the side of the workman which shows that he has 
no interest in this case and therefore, this Tribunal is of the opinion that this case deserves to be dismissed for non 
prosecution. 


4. Hence, 
ORDERED

AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 ((4 of 947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-2002/67/2003- IR(C-I) dated 9/08/2003 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the employer 
i.e. management of Bhowra OCP of M/s. BCCL and their workman through Vice President, Dhanbad Colliery 


5076 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Karmachary Sangh, Dhanbad for adjudication by this Tribunal: 


“Whether the demand of the Dhanbad Colliery Karmachary Sangh from the management of BCCL, 
Bhowra OCP for promoting Sri Jagarnath Jha from Excavation Cat. B to Cat. A is justified? If so, to 
what relief is the workman entitled and from what date?” 


2. On receiving order no. L-2002/67/2003- IR(C-I) dated 9/08/2003 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New 
Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 86 of 2003 was registered on 06.!0.2003 and thereafter the 
notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant 
documents in support of their claims and the witnesses. 


3. Even after issuance of regd. notice, none appeared from either side though on 30.05.2025, Sri Dhiraj Kumar, 
Legal Inspector of management appeared. Again regd. notice was sent to the workman on 30.05.2025 that was 
delivered to him on 3.05.2025 and even after that he did not turn up. 


4. Today Sri Dhiraj Kumar, Legal Inspector of management appeared. 


5. The case record shows that after issuance of two regd. notices, the workman never appeared before the Tribunal 
since the year 2003 which makes it clear that the workman has no interest in this case and therefore, this Tribunal is 
of the opinion that this case deserves to be dismissed for non prosecution. 


6. Hence, 
ORDERED 


that this case is hereby dismissed and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the above reference case.

Government hereby publishes the Award (Reference.LC/R/46/202l) of the Central Government Industrial 


5078 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEc. 3(ii)]

Workman 
Versus 
l. The Depot Manager 
Food Corporation of India 
Kalling Nagar, Gudhiyari, 
PO- Gudhiyari, Raipur (C.G.)-4944 
2. M/s. Satyawati Services 
Contractor at FCI, Kalling Nagar 
Gudhiyari, PO: Gudhiyari, Raipur (C.G.)-4944 
Management 
AWARD 
(Passed on this 0" day of June-2025.) 


As per letter dated 30/09/202 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under section-!0 of I.D. Act, 947 as per reference number L-220/4/202I/IR(CM-I) 
dt. 30/09/202. The dispute under reference related to :- 


“Whether the demand of union of payment of bonus to 88 workers by M/s. Satyawati Services a contractor 
and CM, FCI, Raipur for the year 2076-79 and 2049-20 is legal and justified ? If not, what relief the 88 
workmen, represented through Chhattisgarh Hamal Mazdoor Sangh Rajnandgaon are entitled to ?” 


Notices were sent to the parties after registering a case on the basis of reference. The workman union 
appeared and filed its statement of claim. None appeared for the management of FCI and contractor inspite of service, 
hence case is proceeding ex-parte against them vide order dated 0.0.2024. 


The case of the workman union, as taken by them in their statement of claim is mainly that these workmen, 
mentioned in the Annexure to the Schedule have worked with Food Corporation of India through contractor M/s. 
Satyawati Services in the year 20I8-l9 & 209-20 but were not paid bonus. The union raised a dispute before the 
Assistant Labour Commissioner. After failure of conciliation, reference was sent. The workman union has prayed that 
the workmen be held entitled to get bonus from Food Corporation of India being the principal employer/ the 
contractor. 


None from the side of Food Corporation of India and Contractor appeared. In evidence, the workman union 
has filed affidavit of its General Secretary and affidavit of one worker Basanti Sagar. The union has also filed RTI 
documents with respect to their representation before the Regional Labour Commissioner and conciliation 
proceedings. 


I have heard argument of Mr. R.C. Shrivastava, learned Counsel for the workman union and have gone 
through the record. 


From the uncontroverted affidavits, it is proved that the 88 workmen as per list have worked with Food 


[भागवा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 5079 


Corporation of India as contract workers supplied by the contractor M/s. Satyawati Services. 


Section 2] of the Contract Labour (Regulation & Abolition) Act 970, deals with the responsibility of the 
principal employer (Food Corporation of India in this case), is being reproduced as follows :- 


“20. Liability of principal employer in certain cases.- 


(3) If any amenity required to be provided under Section 76, Section 77, Section 76 or Section 79 for the 
benefit of the contract labour employed in an estab lishment is not provided by the contractor within the 
time prescribed therefore, such amenity shall be provided by the principal employer within such time as 
may be prescribed. 


(2) All expenses incurred by the principal employer in providing the amenity may be recovered by the 
principal employer from the contractor either by deduction from any amount payable to the contractor 
under any contract or as a debt payable by the contractor. 


24. Responsibility for payment of wages.- 


(I) A contractor shall be responsible for payment of wages to each worker employed by him as contract 
labour and such wages shall be paid before the expiry of such period as may be prescribed. 


(2) Every principal employer shall nominate a representative duly authorised by him to be present at the 
time of disbursement of wages by the contractor and it shall be the duty of such representative to certify 
the amounts paid as wages in such manner as may be prescribed. 


(3) It shall be the duty of the contractor to ensure the disbursement of wages in the presence of the 
authorised representative of the principal employer. 


(4) In case the contractor fails to make payment of wages within the prescribed period or makes short 
payment, then the principal employer shall be liable to make payment of wages in full or the unpaid 
balance due, as the case may be, to the contract labour employed by the contractor and recover the 
amount so paid from the contractor either by deduction from any amount payable to the contractor any 
contract as a debt payable by the contractor.” 


From the above referred provision, it is established that the responsibility of payment of wages is of the 
contractor and if he fails to discharge his this responsibility, the principal employer is under obligation to discharge it. 


Now the question arises, as whether bonus is part of wages or not ? 
Section 2(h) of the Contract Labour (Regulation & Abolition) Act 970, defines wages as follows :- 


(h) "wages" shall have the meaning assigned to it in clause (vi) of Section 2 of the Payment of 
Wages Act, 7936 (4 of 7936); 


Section 2(vi) of Payment of Wages Act 936 defines wages as follows:- 


vi) “wages”? means all remuneration (whether by way of salary, allowances, or otherwise) 
expressed in terms of money or capable of being so expressed which would, if the terms of employment, 
express or implied, were fulfilled, be payable to a person employed in respect of his employment or of work 
done in such employment, and includes— 


(a) any remuneration payable under any award or settlement between the parties or order of 
a Court; 


(b) any remuneration to which the person employed is entitled in respect of overtime work or 
holidays or any leave period; 


(c) any additional remuneration payable under the terms of employment (whether called a bonus or 
by any other name); 


(d) any sum which by reason of the termination of employment of the person employed is payable 
under any law, contract or instrument which provides for the payment of such sum, whether with or without 
deductions, but does not provide for the time within which the payment is to be made; 


(e) any sum to which the person employed is entitled under any scheme framed under any law for 
the time being in force, 


but does not include— 


(3) any bonus (whether under a scheme of profit sharing or otherwise) which does not form part of 
the remuneration payable under the terms of employment or which is not payable under any award or 
settlement between the parties or order of a Court; 


5080 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


(2) the value of any house-accommodation, or of the supply of light, water, medical attendance or 
other amenity or of any service excluded from the computation of wages by a general or special order of 
appropriate Government; 


(3) any contribution paid by the employer to any pension or provident fund, and the interest which 
may have accrued thereon; 


(4) any travelling allowance or the value of any travelling concession; 


(5) any sum paid to the employed person to defray special expenses entailed on him by the nature of 
his employment; or 


(6) any gratuity payable on the termination of employment in cases other than those specified in 
sub-clause (d). 


The relevant provisions with respect to payment of bonus in the Payment of Bonus Act are being 
reproduced as follows :- 


8. Eligibility for bonus.—Every employee shall be entitled to be paid by his employer in an accounting year, 
bonus, in accordance with the provisions of this Act, provided he has worked in the establishment for not less 
than thirty working days in that year. 


9. Disqualification _for_bonus.—Notwithstanding anything contained in this Act, an employee shall be 


disqualified from receiving bonus under this Act, if he is dismissed from service for— 
(a) fraud; or 

(b) riotous or violent behaviour while on the premises of the establishment; or 

(c) theft, misappropriation or sabotage of any property of the establishment. 


409. Payment of minimum bonus.—Subject to the other provisions of this Act, every employer shall be bound 
to pay to every employee in respect of the accounting year commencing on any day in the year 7979 and in 
respect of every subsequent accounting year, a minimum bonus which shall be 8.33 per cent. of the salary of 
wage earned by the employee during the accounting year or one hundred rupees, whichever is higher, 
whether or not the employer has any allocable surplus in the accounting year: Provided that where an 
employee has not completed fifteen years of age at the beginning of the accounting year, the provisions of 
this section shall have effect in relation to such employees as if for the words “one hundred rupees”, the 
words “sixty rupees” were substituted. 


44. Payment of maximum bonus.— 


(I) Where in respect of any accounting year referred to in section 709, the allocable surplus exceeds the 
amount of minimum bonus payable to the employees under that section, the employer shall, in lieu of such 
minimum bonus, be bound to pay to every employee in respect of that accounting year bonus which shall be 
an amount in proportion to the salary or wage earned by the employee during the accounting year subject to 
a maximum of twenty per cent. of such salary or wage. 


(2) In computing the allocable surplus under this section, the amount set on or the amount set off under the 
provisions of section ज> shall be taken into account in accordance with the provisions of that section. 


Since, the workman union has not file the terms of contract between the principal employer and contractor, 
has not also filed the terms of engagement between the contractor and its workers, the Food Corporation of India 
cannot be held responsible to pay bonus being a principal employer. Rather, it is the contractor M/s. Satyawati 
Services who are held responsible to pay bonus for the year 20l8-l9 & 209-20 to the workers mentioned in the 
reference. 


On basis of above discussion and findings, the reference is answered as follows :- 
AWARD 


The contractor M/s. Satyawati Services is held responsible to pay bonus for the years 208-9 & 209-20 to 
its contract workers mentioned in the list with interest @ of !0% per annum from the date which the amount of bonus 
became due till the date of payment. 


No order as to cost. 
DATE: 0/06/2025 
P.K. SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer 


[भाग ॥---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 508 


नई दिल्‍ली, 3 जुलाई, 2025

सरकार महासचिव, क्षेत्रीय सुदूर संवेदन केंद्र-ती, नागपुर; श्री चौ. सत्यनारायण, प्रभारी अभियंता, राष्ट्रीय सुदूर संवेदन

AWARD 
(Dated: 7" June, 2025) 


In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section () and sub-section 2(A) of section 0 of 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947) (“the Act” in short), the Central Government has referred the industrial 
dispute between the employers, in relation to the management of Regional Remote Sensing Centre, Nagpur through 
its General Manager, Contactor M/s Ch Subba Rayudu Hyderabad and their union, General Secretary, Nagpur 
General Labour Union, Nagpur, for adjudication, as per letter No. 24(57)/2023-IR dated 03.04.2024, with the 
following schedule:- 


“Whether the action of the management of Regional Remote Sensing Centre, Nagpur through its 
General Manager and its Contractor, M/s Ch. Subba Rayudu, Hyderabad not considering charter of 
demands (Annex-A) raised by the General Secretary, Nagpur General Labour Union is legal and 
justified in the eye of law or not? If not, to what relief the worker entitled to.” 


2. Case is called out. Both parties are absent despite service of notices. No statement of claim and written 
statement have been filed by the parties respectively till today. Petitioner has not filed any evidence to prove his case. 
Petitioner is not coming to the Court since long back. It appears that he is not interested to contest the case further 
more. Claim of the petitioner is not proved. So, it is closed. 


Hence, it is ordered. 
ORDER 


The action of the management of Regional Remote Sensing Centre, Nagpur through its General 
Manager and its Contractor, M/s Ch. Subba Rayudu, Hyderabad not considering charter of demands 
(Annex-A) raised by the General Secretary, Nagpur General Labour Union is legal and justified in the eye of 
law. The union is not entitled to any relief. 


Justice (Retd.) SHIV SHANKER PRASAD, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 3 जुलाई, 2025

AWARD 
(Dated: 7" June, 2025) 


In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section () and sub-section 2(A) of section 0 of 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947) (“the Act” in short), the Central Government has referred the industrial 
dispute between the employers, in relation to the management of Akola Range, Akola, and their workmen Shri Mohd. 
Iqbal Mohd. Bilal, Sachin D. Patrodiya, Krishna N. Nitnawre & Smt. Sushila Pardhi, for adjudication, as per letter 
No. L-420/73/208 (IR(DU) dated 04.2.208, with the following schedule:- 


“Whether the action of the management of Income Tax, Akola through its Joint Commissioner of 
Income Tax and Income Tax, Nagpur through its Chief Commissioner of Income Tax in terminating 
the service of Sh. Mohd. Iqbal Mohd. Bilal, Sachin D. Patrodiya, Krishna N. Nitnawre & Smt. Sushila 
Pardhi w.e.f. November, 206 who were working on the post of daily wagers w.e.f. 999 is legal and 
justified? If not, what relief the workmen are entitled to?” 


2 Case is called out. Both parties are absent. Both parties are not responding and attending the Court since 
23.02.202. Although statement of claim and written statement have been filed by the parties respectively. Petitioners 
have not filed any evidence to prove their case. Petitioners are not coming to the Court since long back. It appears that 
they are not interested to contest the case further more. Claim of the petitioners are not proved. So, it is closed. 


Hence, it is ordered. 
ORDER 


The action of the management of Income Tax, Akola through its Joint Commissioner of Income Tax 
and Income Tax, Nagpur through its Chief Commissioner of Income Tax in terminating the service of 
Sh. Mohd. Iqbal Mohd. Bilal, Sachin D. Patrodiya, Krishna N. Nitnawre & Smt. Sushila Pardhi 
w.e.f. November, 206 who were working on the post of daily wagers w.e.f. 999 is legal and justified. The 
workmen are not entitled to any relief. 


Justice (Retd.) SHIV SHANKER PRASAD, Presiding Officer

AWARD 


Sri Babulal Basfore the aggrieved workman has appeared in person and produced his photo identity card. 
Mr. Harish Gupta, Ld. Advocate appeared for the Management of CWC, Guwahati, Assam. None appeared for M/S 
BIPICIRI, Guwahati, the Contractor/firm. Case is specially fixed up today for filing evidence-in-chief as last chance 
and for disposal during the special campaign. 


In exercise of power vested under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub-section (2A) of Section l0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947), the Government of India through the Deputy Chief Labour Commissioner 
(Central), Guwahati, in pursuance of notification No. S.0.936 (E), dated 0"" June,209 issued order No. G/R. 
8(0)/202-CGIT dated 0i-02-202] and dated 02-07-202] referring the scheduled dispute before this Tribunal for 
adjudication. 


On receiving Order No. G/R. 8(0I)/202-CGIT dated 0i-02-202l and 02-07-202l two separate cases 
bearing Reference case No. 04 of 202] and Reference case No. II of 202l were registered on 02-02-202] and 02- 
07-202! respectively. Orders were passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to 
appear and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims and a list of 
witnesses. 


It is pertinent to mention that the Ministry of Labour and Employment, Government of India through the 
Office of the Deputy Chief Labour Commissioner (Central), Guwahati, vide its Order No. G/R. 8(0)/202-CGIT 
dated 0-02-202] and dated 02-07-202] referred the same dispute twice and the same were registered as Reference 


[भाग ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 5085 


Case No. 04 of 202! and Reference Case [०.] of 202. Both the cases were being proceeding separately. Thereafter, 
the matter was brought to the notice of this Tribunal and at the instance of both the parties the reference cases have 
been amalgamated vide order dated 5-i-202l passed in Ref. Case 7२०.।| of 202! for disposing them together, 
schedule in both cases being same. 


(l) “Whether the action of the Central Water Commission (CWC), Guwahati and their Contractor M/s 
BIPICIRI, Guwahati in terminating the services of Sh. Babulal Basfore, Ex-Casual Labour (workman) of 
CWC, New Goshala, Maligaon, Guwahati is legal and justified without compliance of Sec.25F of 
ID. Act, 947 ? 


(2) Whether the action of the CWC in changing the service condition of the workman from casual labour to 
contractual labour in the year 20l9 after putting continuous service since 20l0 is legal and justified in 
violation of Sec. 9A of the I. D. Act,947? 


(3) Whether the demand of the workman/casual labour for payment of wages @ /30" of the pay at minimum of 
the pay scale as applicable to the regular employees of CWC is legal and justified? If not, what relief he is 
entitled to and from which date? 


On receiving the notice, the Management and the workman appeared through their respective Counsels. The 
Contractor firm was absent and vide order dated 04-03-202! case was fixed for ex-parte hearing against the 
Contractor firm. The Management and the workman submitted Written Statement. The case is now fixed up for 
evidence and hearing. At this stage Sri Babulal Basfore submitted that he does not want to pursue the case any further 
and the same may be disposed of. 


Consider the submission. It appears that the dismissed workman is not inclined to proceed with the case. 
Same is accordingly dismissed for non-prosecution. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


That the Industrial dispute in both the cases is dismissed for non-prosecution. Let a No Dispute Award be 
drawn up on the basis of my above findings. Same be communicated to the Ministry of Labour and Employment, 
Government of India, New Delhi for information and Notification.

5086 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


ANNEXURE 


IN THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL-CUM-LABOUR COURT-II, 
CHANDIGARH 


(Presided over by Mr. Kamal Kant). 
Special Campaign for Settlement 
ID No.2/206 
Registered on:-2.04.20l6 
Abid Hassan S/o Sh. Yashin, R/o VPO Darpur, Tehsil Chhachhrauli, Distt. Yamunanagar. 


wannnnn- Applicant 
Versus 
l. The Assistant Superintendent of Post Offices, Yamunanagar, Distt. Yamunanagar. 
2. The Senior Superintendent of Post Offices, Ambala Division, Ambala. 
----Respondents 
Present:- Sh. R. P. Rana, AR for Applicant. 
Mr. Alankrit Bhardwaj, AR for respondent no.| & 2. 
Award : 07.06.2025 

l. File taken up today. The applicant Abid Hasan has filed the present claim petition under Section 2A of the 


Industrial Disputes Act 947 (hereinafter called the ID Act) to claim reinstatement in service along with arrears of pay 
and allowances with interest. 


2. The brief facts of the case are that the applicant was engaged as on outsider GDS (Gramin Dak Sevak), by 
the Post Master Sub Post Office Darpur on 8.05.203 and rendered a service of one year till 6.05.204 and he was 
retrenched by the respondent without compliance of Section 25F of the ID Act as he had already completed more than 
240 days during the period 6.05.203 to 6.05.204. The applicant filed an application before the Conciliation cum 
Assistant Labour Commissioner (Central), Karnal in February 20I5, however the conciliation proceedings failed and 
has filed the present application before this Tribunal to seek relief under S 2(A) read with S 25 F,S 25 G and S25 H of 
the ID Act. It is prayed that the status of the applicant be restored and he be granted benefit of service along with 
arrear of pay and allowances along with interest. 


3. Respondent Nos. | & 2 filed written statement alleging therein that the post of GDSMD/C Darpur had fallen 
vacant due to death of Sh. Mohinder Singh GDSMD/C on 30.08.202, who was also holding the charge of GDS BPM 
Darpur due to resignation of the incumbent of the post. Therefore, the work of the GDS BPM was managed by Sh. 
Surjit Singh, GDSMD/C Kot and the work of GDSMD/C Darpur was managed by engaging on Sh. Jaspreet Singh 
outsider. Accordingly, proposal for filling up the post of GDS MD/C Darpur on compassionate grounds was 
forwarded to higher authorities. Till the approval of appointment case on compassionate grounds the work was 
managed by deployment of various outsiders keeping in view the limitations of 89 days at a stretch and 50 days ina 
calendar year. The applicant was engaged to work as outsider as GDS MD/C Darpur to manage the work w.e.f 
6.05.20i3 and as such he worked for the dates as noted below in the year 203 and 20]4. It is further added that 
Branch Postmaster Darpur had engaged the applicant as outsider GDS after approval from ASPOs Yamuna Nagar 
who is appointing authority of the GDS:- 

6-05-203 to 5-06-203, 

7-06-203 to 5-07-203, 

6-07-203 to 0-08-203, 

2-08-203 to 3-08-203 


0-09-203 to 30-09-203 
In the year 204 
0l-0i-204 to 3-0I-204, 
0-02-204 to 28-02-204, 
0-03-204 to 28-03-204 
Total 25 days. 


[भाग ॥---खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 5087 


The applicant had not worked after 28.03.204. As the applicant has not worked continuously for 240 days and 
the applicant is not engaged as GDS as such question of giving notice to the applicant does not arise. As regards 
implementation of the provisions of ID Act, it is stated that the Department of Posts is not an industry. Therefore 
provisions of ID Act are not applicable to Department of Posts and its employees. Moreover GDS employees are not 
regular employee working for 8 hours. They are part time employees working for 3 to 5 hours and governed by a 
separate set of Rules called GDS (Conduct and Engagement) Rules, 20l! (hereinafter called as GDS Rules). The 
provisions of the ID Act are not applicable to the Department of Posts and Its employees. Furthermore, the applicant 
was not GDS but was an outsider GDS. It is submitted that the applicant is not entitled for the relief as claimed and 
the application be dismissed with costs. 


Evidence of parties: 


4. In order to prove his case, applicant himself appeared and filed his affidavit Ex.WWI/A along with 
documents Ex.WI to W4 and closed the evidence on behalf of applicant on 3i.0!.2025. Thereafter, respondents have 
examined Sh. Arun Goel, Superintendent of Post Offices, Ambala Division, Ambala, who tendered his affidavit as 
MWI/A along with documents +. ५] & Ex.M2. AR for respondent no.! & 2 closed the evidence on 47.02.2025 and 
the case was fixed for arguments. 


Submissions of Applicant:- 


2. Learned AR of applicant argued orally and contended that the applicant is entitled for the benefits as he has 
claimed in his application and reiterated the claim as stated by him in the application. 


Submissions of respondents:- 


6. Learned AR for respondents contended that the applicant had not worked continuously for 240 days and the 
applicant was not engaged as GDS as such question of giving notice to the applicant does not arise. Moreover GDS 
are governed by a separate set of Rules called GDS Rules. The provisions of the ID Act are not applicable to the 
Department of Posts and its employees. Moreover, the applicant was an outsider GDS and was not even governed by 
GDS Rules. AR for respondents contended that the applicant is not entitled for the relief as claimed and the 
application be dismissed. 


Findings:- 
7. Ihave heard the arguments advanced by AR for both the parties. 


8. It is admitted fact that the applicant Abid Hasan was appointed as an outsider GDS by the Department in 
place of regular incumbent Sh. Mohinder Singh. Outsider GDS are appointed as a stop gap arrangement until regular 
incumbent come on the post. In fact, as per procedure when a GDS appointed by the department is unable to perform 
his duty then as a stop gap arrangement, a temporary arrangement is made until regular incumbent come on that post. 
The substitute appointment is sporadic depending on the period during which the regular is not appointed whatsoever 
reason. Thus, it is clear and proved that applicant rendered his services not an employee of management but as 
nominee of GDS as an outsider. It is also pertinent to mention that as GDS (outsider) is not entitled for any 
termination notice as per GDS Rules. The learned counsel for the applicant could not place any rule before me that 
any notice of termination under the GDS Rules was required before retrenchment by the management of the applicant. 
Moreover, the applicant has also failed to prove that he was appointed on regular basis as GDS, rather applicant was 
appointed as an outsider GDS as stop gap arrangement. He was working as GDS outsider, which are not governed 
under Section 25F of the ID Act. There was no requirement for issuing termination notice as per GDS Rules. 


9. The present application has been filed by the applicant under Section 2A of ID Act, claiming that he was 
retrenched by the management on 6.05.20l4 and he has also approached Conciliation-cum-Assistant Labour 
Commissioner, Karnal during 20l5 and proceedings have failed. Undoubtedly, the applicant covered under ID Act 
can file application under Section 2A of the ID Act against his discharge, dismissal or termination of service after 
failure of the proceedings within 45 days. However, the applicant has not attached any documents that proceedings 
have failed before the conciliation commissioner. Thus, he has directly approach this Court under Section 2A of the 
ID Act, which is not maintainable. Moreover, even if it is presumed that the applicant was covered by the ID Act, 
then it was incumbent upon him to prove that he had worked 240 days in the preceding year from the date of his 
termination, i.e. before 28.03.20l4. However, when the days are counted from !6.05.20I3 to 28.03.20I4, these are 
less than 240 days. On this score also, the application is not maintainable under the ID Act. 


0. Hence, this application under Section 2A is not maintainable and the same is dismissed. File after completion 
be consigned in the record room. Let copy of this award be sent to the Appropriate Government as required under 
Section |7 of the Act for publication. 


KAMAL KANT, Presiding Officer

AWARD 
(Dated: 25" June, 2025) 


In exercise of the powers conferred by clause (d) of sub-section () and sub-section 2(A) of section 0 of 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947) (“the Act” in short), the Central Government has referred the industrial 
dispute between the employers, in relation to the management of N.T.P.C. Mouda, and their workman Shri Sachin 
Ramaji Bawne, for adjudication, as per letter No. N-8(35)2020-ID/IR dated 27.0.2020, with the following 
schedule:- 


“Whether the action of (l) The Managing Director/General Manager, M/s Utility Powertech Limited, 
(UPL) Mouda Site Office: Qtr. No. A-3, Block No. 7, Utkarsh Nagar, N.T.P.C. Ltd. MSTPP, Mouda 
Ramtek Road, P.O. Mouda, Distt-Nagpur (2) The Manager, M/s Sanjay Enterprises, Contractor, 
N.T.P.C. Mouda, Tah. Mouda, Distt-Nagpur in terminating the service of Shri Sachin Ramaji Bawne 
w.e.f. 02/07/207 are just fair & legal? If not, to what relief the workman is entitled to?” 


2 Case is called out. Learned Counsel Shri. A.K. Neware is present on behalf of respondent no. 2 before the 
Court. But none is present on behalf of petitioner as well as respondent no. | and 3. 


From perusal of record, it is apparent that petitioner is not coming to the Court since |7.09.202]. Petitioner 
has not filed his statement of claim till today. Written statements have also not been filed by the respondents till 
today. Petitioner has not filed any evidence to prove his case. Petitioner is not coming to the Court since long back. It 
appears that he is not interested to contest the case further more. Claim of the petitioner is not proved. So, it is closed. 


Hence, it is ordered. 
ORDER 


The action of () The Managing Director/General Manager, M/s Utility Powertech Limited, (UPL) 
Mouda Site Office: Qtr. No. A-3, Block No. 7, Utkarsh Nagar, N.T.P.C. Ltd. MSTPP, Mouda Ramtek Road, 
P.O. Mouda, Distt-Nagpur (2) The Manager, M/s Sanjay Enterprises, Contractor, N.T.P.C. Mouda, Tah.

का.आ. 4290.--केन्द्रीय सरकार, कर्मचारी राज्य बीमा अधिनियम, 948 (4948 का 34) की धारा 94क के 


साथ पठित धारा 88 द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए, मैसर्स गैस अथॉरिटी ऑफ़ इंडिया लिमिटेड के कारखानों और 


स्थापनों के नियमित कर्मचारियों को उक्त अधिनियम के प्रवर्तन से छूट प्रदान करती है और छूट राजपत्र में इस अधिसूचना 
के प्रकाशन की तारीख से एक वर्ष की अवधि के लिए प्रभावी होगी। 


2. उक्त छूट निम्नलिखित शर्तों के अध्यधीन है, अर्थात्‌:- 
(4) 0 कारखाना और स्थापन, छूट प्राप्त कर्मचारियों के नाम और पदनाम विनिर्दिष्ट करते हुए, कर्मचारियों का एक 
रजिस्टर अनुरक्षित करेगी; 


(2) कर्मचारी, उक्त अधिनियम के अधीन ऐसी प्रसुविधाएं प्राप्त करते रहेंगे, जिन्हें पाने के लिए वे इस अधिसूचना द्वारा 
अनुदत्त छूट के प्रवर्तन होने की तारीख से पूर्व संदत्त अभिदाय के आधार पर हकदार हो जाते हैं; 


(3) छूट प्राप्त अवधि के लिए कोई अभिदाय, यदि पहले ही संदत्त किए जा चुके हैं, तो वे प्रतिदेय नहीं होंगे; 


(4) उक्त कारखाने और स्थापन का नियोजक, उस अवधि की बाबत जिसके दौरान वह कारखाना उक्त अधिनियम के 
प्रवर्तन के अध्यधीन था(जिसे इसमें इसके पश्चात उक्त अवधि कहा गया है), ऐसी विवरणियां, ऐसे प्ररूपों में और ऐसी 
विशिष्टियों को अंतर्विष्ट करते हुए जो कर्मचारी राज्य बीमा (साधारण) विनियम, 7950 के अधीन उक्त अवधि की बाबत 
उससे शोध्य थीं, प्रस्तुत करेगा; 


(5) निमम द्वारा उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 45 की उपधारा (4) के अधीन नियुक्त किया गया कोई सामाजिक सुरक्षा 
अधिकारी या इस प्रयोजन के लिए इस निमित्त निगम का प्राधिकृत कोई अन्य पदधारी-- 


(i) उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 44 की उपधारा () के अधीन, उक्त अवधि के लिए प्रस्तुत किसी विवरणी में 
अंतर्विष्ट विशिष्टयों को सत्यापित करेगा; या 


500 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


(ii) यह अभिनिश्चयन करने के लिए कि क्या कर्मचारी राज्य बीमा (साधारण) विनियम, (950 sre उक्त अवधि 
के लिए यथाअपेक्षित रजिस्टर और अभिलेख अनुरक्षित किए गये थे या नहीं; या 


(iii) यह अभिनिश्चयन करने के लिए कि क्या कर्मचारी, नियोजक द्वारा उपबंधित उन प्रसुविधाओं को, जिसके 
फलस्वरूप इस अधिसूचना के अधीन छूट अनुदत्त की जा रही है, नकद में और वस्तु रूप में पाने का हकदार 
होना जारी रहेगा या नहीं; या 


(iv) यह अभिनिश्चयन करने के लिए कि क्‍या उस अवधि के दौरान, जब उक्त कारखाने और स्थापन के संबंध में 
अधिनियम के ऐसे उपबंध प्रवृत्त थे, ऐसे heel उपबंधों का अनुपालन किया गया था या नहीं, निम्नलिखित 
कार्य करने के लिए सशक्त होगा-- 


(क) प्रधान या अव्यवहित नियोजक से अपेक्षा करना कि वह उसे ऐसी जानकारी दे जिसे इस अधिनियम के प्रयोजन के 
लिए वह आवश्यक समझे; या 


(ख) ऐसे प्रधान या aerated नियोजक द्वारा के अधिभोगाधीन, किसी कारखाने, स्थापन, कार्यालय या अन्य परिसर में 
किसी भी युक्तियुक्त समय पर प्रवेश करना और उसके प्रभारी से यह अपेक्षा करना वह कार्मिक के नियोजन और मजदूरी के 
संदाय से संबंधित ऐसे लेखे, बहियां अन्य दस्तावेज, ऐसे निरीक्षक या अन्य पदधारी के समक्ष प्रस्तुत करें और उनकी परीक्षा 


अनुज्ञात करें या उसे ऐसी जानकारी दें जिसे वह आवश्यक समझे; या 


(ग) प्रधान या अव्यवहित नियोजक की, उसके अभिकर्ता या सेवक की, या ऐसे किसी व्यक्ति की, जो ऐसे कारखाने, 
स्थापन, कार्यालय या अन्य परिसर में पाया जाए, या कोई व्यक्ति जिसे उक्त निरीक्षक या अन्य पदधारी के पास विश्वास 
करने का युक्तियुक्त कारण है कि वह कर्मचारी है, की परीक्षा करना; या 


(घ) ऐसे कारखाने, स्थापन, कार्यालय या अन्य परिसर में अनुरक्षित किसी रजिस्टर, लेखा, बही या अन्य दस्तावेज की 
प्रतियां बनाना या उद्धरण लेना; या 


(ड) ऐसी अन्य शक्तियों का प्रयोग करना जो विनिर्दिष्ट की जाएं | 


(6) अपविनिधान या निगमीकरण की दशा में, अनुदत्त छूट रद्द हो जाएगी और नया अस्तित्व छूट के लिए समुचित 
सरकार को आवेदन कर सकेगी | 


[सं. TAt-3804/03/202- एस. एस-]] 


धीरेंद्र मोहन खरे, अवर सचिव 


New Delhi, the 3rd July, 2025 


S.O. 290.—In exercise of the powers conferred by section 88 read with section 9lA of the Employees’ 
State Insurance Act, 948 (34 of 948), the Central Government hereby exempts the regular employees of factories 
and establishments of M/s Gas Authority of India Limited from the operation of the said Act and the exemption 
shall be effective for a period of one year from the date of publication of this notification in the Official Gazette. 


2. The exemption is subject to the following conditions, namely:- 


(.) the factories and establishments shall maintain a register of the employees specifying the names and 
designations of the exempted employees’; 


(2) the employees shall continue to receive such benefits under the said Act to which they would have been 
entitled to on the basis of the contribution paid prior to the date from which exemption granted by this 
notification operates; 


(3) the contribution for the exempted period, if already paid, shall not be refundable; 


(4) the employer of the said factory and establishment shall submit in respect of the period during which that 
factory was subject to the operation of the said Act (hereinafter referred as the said period), such returns in 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाहढ़ 2, 947 5i0I 


such forms and containing such particulars as were due from it in respect of the said period under the 
Employees’ State Insurance (General) Regulations, |950; 


(5) a Social Security Officer appointed by the Corporation under sub-section (.) of section 45 of the said Act 
or other official of the Corporation authorised in this behalf by it, shall, for the purpose of — 


(i) verifying the particulars contained in any return submitted under sub-section (L) of section 44 of 
the said Act for the said period; or 


(ii) ascertaining whether registers and records were maintained as required by the Employees’ State 
Insurance (General) Regulations, !950 for the said period; or 


(iii) ascertaining whether the employees continue to be entitled to benefits provided by the 
employer in cash and kind being benefits in consideration of which exemption is being granted 
under this notification; or 


(iv) ascertaining whether any of the provisions of the Act had been complied with during the period 
when such provisions were in force in relation to the said factory and establishment to be 
empowered to — 


(a) require the principal or immediate employer to him such information as he may consider 
necessary for the purpose of this Act; or 


(b) at any reasonable time enter any factory, establishment, office or other premises occupied 
by such principal or immediate employer at any reasonable time and require any person found 
in charge thereof to produce to such inspector or other official and allow him to examine 
accounts, books and other documents relating to the employment of personal and payment of 
wages or to furnish to him such information as he may consider necessary; or 


(c) examine the principal or immediate employer, his agent or servant, or any person found in 
such factory, establishment, office or other premises or any person whom the said inspector or 
other official has reasonable cause to believe to have been an employee ; or 


(d) make copies of or take extracts from any register, account 


book or other document maintained in such factory, establishment, office or other 
premises; or 


(e) exercise such other powers as may be specified; 


(6) in case of disinvestment or corporatisation, the exemption granted shall stand cancelled and the new 
entity may apply to the appropriate Government for exemption.
