S.O. 29l.—In pursuance of Section 7 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. No. 7/202) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal- 
cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of ECL 
and their workmen received by the Central Government on 0/07/2025. 


[No. L-2202/35/202— IR (CM-ID)] 
MANIKANDAN. N, Dy. Director 


502 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


ANNEXURE 
BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM- LABOUR COURT, 
ASANSOL. 
PRESENT: Shri Ananda Kumar Mukherjee, 


Presiding Officer, 
C.G.LT-cum-L.C., Asansol. 
REFERENCE CASE NO. 7 OF 202] 


PARTIES: Prabir Bouri 
Vs. 

Management of | and 2 Incline, Jhanjra Area of ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Basudev Choudhury, Advocate. 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 8.07.2024 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, l947, the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No. L-2202/35/202-IR(CM-II) dated 08.09.202l has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of | and 2 Incline Under Jhanjra Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workman for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“ Whether the action of the Management of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Ltd. in relation to its / & 2 Incline under 
Jhanjra Area in imposing a punishment of dismissal on Shri Prabir Bouri, Cat-I Mazdoor (Man No. 2469॥,, 
vide ref. No. GM/JNR/PERS/20I3/l648 dated /9-03-2073 is just and legal? if not, to what relief the 
workman is entitled to? ” 


| On receiving Order No. L-2202/35/202-IR(CM-ID dated 08.09.202 from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 47 of 202 was registered on 
20.09.202 and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear 
and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims and a list of their 
witnesses. 


2. The workman filed his written statement on 24.07.2023. The fact of the case as disclosed in his written 
statement is that Prabir Bouri, the workman was a permanent employee at Jhanjra Project Colliery under Jhanjra Area 
of Eastern Coalfields Limited (hereinafter referred to as ECL) bearing U.M. No. 74780l. He was appointed on 
9.09.985 and had remained absent from duty from !8.04.20I2 due to his illness. He informed the management 
about the reason of his absence. Despite such information management illegally issued a Charge Sheet to him bearing 
No. AGT/JNR/I&2/P/CS/2/789 dated 30.04.20!2. A Domestic Enquiry was held against him on 24.07.202. The 
workman participated in the Enquiry Proceeding and disclosed the reason of his absence and also submitted Medical 
Certificate in support of his illness. No 2" Show Cause Notice was issued by the management nor any copy of 
Enquiry Proceeding was supplied to him before dismissing him from service, violating the principles of natural 
justice. It is contended that a major punishment has been imposed against the workman, disproportionate to the 
alleged charge. He has been made jobless and forced to a starving condition. The dismissed workman prayed for 
setting aside the order of dismissal, seeking reinstatement in service and payment of back wages. 


3. The management of ECL contested the case by filing their written statement on 23.0.2023. It is stated that 
Prabir Bouri, General Mazdoor, Category — I, was absenting from his duty at Jhanjra Project Colliery since 
8.04.20i2 without any information. A Charge Sheet was issued to Prabir Bouri for misconduct vide Charge Sheet 
No. AGT/JNR/I&2/P/CS/2/789 dated 30.04.20I2 under Clause 26.23 of Certified Standing Order for his habitual 
absence from duty without cause and under Clause 26.29 of Certified Standing Order for unauthorized absence for 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 503 


more than ten days. The Charge Sheet was received by the delinquent by putting his signature. The reply submitted 
against the Charge Sheet was not satisfactory and a Domestic Enquiry was initiated. Enquiry was held against Prabir 
Bouri on 24.07.202, following the principle of principles of natural justice and the charges were well established 
against him. Management issued a 2"' Show Cause Notice to the charged employee bearing Ref. No. 
GM/JNR/PERS/203/I355 dated 6/9.0.203. Copy of Enquiry Proceeding and Enquiry Report were supplied to 
him with the 2"! Show Cause Notice and the workman submitted his written reply. The reply of the workman was not 
found satisfactory by the Disciplinary Authority as his past record in service was poor. The management having no 
other alternative, dismissed the workman from service of the company vide Office Order bearing Ref. No. 
GM/JNR/PERS/203/648 dated 9.03.203. It is claimed that the Industrial Dispute is liable to be dismissed. 


4. The dismissed workman has examined himself as Workman Witness — । and filed his affidavit-in-chief, 
wherein he has averred that he informed the management the reason of his absence but the management illegally and 
arbitrarily issued Charge Sheet against him. A Domestic Enquiry was held in respect of the charge and he participated 
in the enquiry. In the Enquiry Proceeding the workman disclosed that he was suffering from illness but management 
did not consider the document. No 27 Show Cause Notice was served upon him and the management illegally 
terminated him from service violating the principles of natural justice. During the evidence the workman witness 
produced a copy of his order of dismissal dated 9.03.203 as Exhibit W-I. 


5. During cross-examination workman witness denied the suggestion that he worked for thirty-two days in the 
year 2009, sixteen days in the year 20l0 and forty-one days in the year 20l. He further denied that due to his 
unauthorized absence his annual increments were stopped or that he did not remain absent for his illness. The witness 
also disowned the signature appearing on the reply to the 2"' Show Cause Notice and on the copy of the Charge Sheet. 
The witness however admitted the signature appearing in the Enquiry Report, and the same has been marked as 
Exhibit M-]. 


6. Mr. Alaric Oneal Lyndem, Manager (Personnel), Jhanjra Project Colliery has been examined as Management 
Witness — |. In the affidavit-in-chief the witness stated that शिक्षा Bouri was chargesheeted on 30.04.20l2 under 
Clause 26.29 of Certified Standing Order for being absent from duty beyond ten days without sanctioned leave or 
sufficient cause and under Clause 26.23 for habitual low attendance or absence from duty without sufficient cause. It 
is stated that the Charge Sheet was received by the workman by putting his signature on the office copy of the same. 
As his reply was not found satisfactory and for his antecedents of habitual absence a Domestic Enquiry was held. It is 
also stated that the charge against Prabir Bouri was proved and on the basis of the findings of the Enquiry Officer 
management issued 2077 Show Cause Notice to the workman along with his Enquiry Proceeding. The delinquent 
employee submitted reply to the 277 Show Cause Notice but it was not found satisfactory and he was dismissed from 
service by order dated 9.03.20l3. In course of his evidence the management witness produced the following 
documents: 


(i) Signature of Prabir Bouri on the Enquiry Proceeding is marked as Exhibit M-]. 
(ii) Copy of the Charge Sheet dated 30.04.202 has been produced as Exhibit M-2. 
(iii) Copy of the reply to the Charge Sheet, as Exhibit M-3. 
(iv) Copy of the Notice of enquiry dated 2.07.202, as Exhibit M-4. 
(v) Copy of the Enquiry Proceeding dated 24.07.20I2 in three pages has been collectively marked as 
Exhibit-5. 
(vi) Prabir Bouri has put his signature on all the pages of Enquiry Proceeding. The signature on page no. 


l and 2 of the Enquiry Proceeding has been marked as Exhibit M-5/l and M-5/2 and signature on 
page no. 3 has already been marked as Exhibit M-]. 


(vii) Copy of the Enquiry Report dated 24.07.202, as Exhibit M-6. 
(Vili) Copy of the 2" Show Cause Notice dated 6/9.0.20I3 has been produced as Exhibit M-7. 
(ix) Copy of the reply to the 27 Show Cause Notice, as Exhibit M-8. 
(x) Copy of the order of dismissal dated 9.03.203, as Exhibit M-9. 
7. In cross-examination the witness denied that the punishment of dismissal of Prabir Bouri from service was 


improper or it was illegal. 


8. The point for consideration before this Tribunal is whether the punishment of dismissal imposed against 
Prabir Bouri is just and legal. If not, whether he is entitled to any relief? 


9. Mr. Basudev Choudhury, learned advocate for the workman argued that Prabir Bouri was serving the 
company as a permanent employee since 985. He has rendered satisfactory service all through, but the management 
of ECL in an arbitrary manner issued a Charge Sheet against the workman for his absence from duty for a period of 
only twelve days and imposed a disproportionate punishment without service of Charge Sheet, 2"! Show Cause Notice 


504 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


and Enquiry Report. It is further argued that the workman participated in the Enquiry Proceeding and produced his 
medical certificate in support of his absence from 8.04.202 to 8.07.202 but the Enquiry Officer did not consider 
the Certificate, covering the period of his absence on the ground that he did not receive medical treatment from 
colliery Hospital. Learned advocate argued that the Enquiry Proceeding was held in violation of principles of natural 
justice without providing any opportunity to the workman and by way of rejecting his defence without any plausible 
cause. Learned advocate asserted that the order of dismissal passed against the workman is illegal, improper, arbitrary 
and is liable to be set aside. It is urged that the workman merits reinstatement in service and payment of back wages 
till his reinstatement. 


0. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate for the management argued that copy of Charge Sheet, Enquiry Report and 
2™ Show Cause Notice were served upon the charged workman who received the same by putting his signatures. In 
his cross-examination the workman witness admitted his signatures appearing in the Enquiry Proceeding, which goes 
to establish that he was well informed about the Charge Sheet against him as well as the Enquiry Proceeding 
conducted by the Enquiry Officer. It is argued that the workman is a habitual absentee and attended his duty for 
thirty-two days the year 2009, sixteen days in the year 20!0 and forty-one days in the year 20Il. Due to his previous 
absence the workman was earlier punished by stoppage of one increment in the year 996, stoppage of two 
increments in the year |997 and final warning in the year 999. Once again, he was punished by stoppage of three 
increments in the year 2000 and stoppage of one increment in the year 200l. He was demoted to the post of Cleaning 
Mazdoor, Category — I in the year 2005. Again, he was demoted to the initial Basic of Category — I as a last chance in 
the year 2006. Stoppage of one increment in the year 200 and thereafter stoppage of three increments in the year 
20]. It is submitted by the learned advocate that the workman did not rectify his conduct and continued to remain 
absent without informing the management of the company and thereby caused disruption in service, being absolutely 
unpredictable in his attendance. Learned advocate took me through the Charge Sheet dated 30.04.20I2 (Exhibit M-2) 
which bears the signature of the workman. The workman submitted a reply to the Charge Sheet on 20.07.202 ice. 
after two months and twenty days after the issuance of Charge Sheet, wherein he prayed for allowing him to join duty 
on the ground that he was under medical treatment and was then found fit to join his duty. Learned advocate drew my 
attention to the Notice of enquiry, a copy of which was also served upon the workman. Copy of the Enquiry 
Proceeding and Enquiry Report have been produced as Exhibit M-5 and Exhibit M-6. Regarding issuance of 2"! Show 
Cause Notice, the learned advocate pointed out that a 2" Show Cause Notice dated 6/9.0I.20I3 was issued to 
Prabir Bouri, which has been produced as Exhibit M-7. Though the workman has denied having received the 2" 
Show Cause Notice, the learned advocate for management referred to the reply submitted by Prabir Bouri dated 
3.0.203 against 2"! Show Cause Notice, where he has reiterated his earlier statement that he was under medical 
treatment at Khandra BPHC from 8.04.202 to 8.07.202. It is argued that the reply to the 270 Show Cause Notice 
goes to prove that the 2" Show Cause Notice was served upon the workman and he had been provided with 
reasonable opportunity to meet the charge levelled against him under Clause 26.23 and 26.29 of the Certified 
Standing Order. It is vehemently argued that there is no illegality on the part of the management in issuing the Charge 
Sheet against a habitual absentee who had no responsibility towards his work. It is urged that the Industrial Dispute 
raised by the workman eight years after his dismissal has no merit and is liable to be dismissed. 


ll. I have considered the rival contentions of the parties in the backdrop of facts and circumstances of the case. 
The evidence on record is rife to establish that the workman had actually remained absent from duty for more than 
three months from 8.04.202 to 8.07.20I2 and he submitted his application, seeking permission to join his duty 
only from 20.07.20I2 (Exhibit M-3). Nowhere in the application he disclosed the nature of the ailment or the place 
where he received the medical treatment. Only after the Enquiry Proceeding was started, he made an endeavour to 
defend the case raising a plea that he was absent from duty due to illness. The Enquiry Officer in his Report (Exhibit 
M-6) has stated that the workman did not report to the company’s dispensary and also failed to produce the papers 
related to his medical treatment. Besides, the charge of unauthorized absence for more than ten days under Clause 
26.29 of the Certified Standing Order, the Enquiry Officer has found the workman a habitual absentee who 
performed work for thirty-two days in the year 2009, sixteen days in the year 20I0 and forty-one days in the year 
20I] and till 8.04.20I2 he performed work for only fourteen days. These facts have not been controverted by the 
workman. Simple denial of such facts in course of cross-examination does not disprove the charge of misconduct 
under Clause 26.23 of the Certified Standing Order. It is evident the that previously the workman had been subjected 
to minor punishment on nine occasions from December !996 to August 20l!. The workman did not mend his conduct 
nor did he care to attend his work after so many occasions of warning and punishments meted out to him. The 
workman submitted his reply to the 27 Show Cause Notice which has been produced as Exhibit M-8. The controlling 
authority finally issued the Office Order dated 9.03.203, dismissing him from his service in exercise of power 
conferred to him under Clause 27 of the Certified Standing Order applicable to the workman and the establishment. 


42, In my considered view the management having provided reasonable opportunity to the workman has 
initiated a Departmental Proceeding against him for his misconduct and after following the principles of natural 
justice the Enquiry Officer found the workman guilty of the charge. The workman failed to satisfy the Disciplinary 
Authority by submitting his explanation to the 277 Show Cause Notice and the General Manager of Jhanjra Area, 
ECL, being the competent authority dismissed him from service. I find no illegality, impropriety or irregularity in the 
proceeding and the final outcome. The workman having faced nine minor punishments in earlier occasions, failed to 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 505 


rectify his conduct. Therefore, there is no extenuating circumstance for him to avoid the punishment of dismissal from 
service. I do not find any illegality in the Enquiry Proceeding nor the order of dismissal passed against Prabir Bouri. 
Therefore, the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest against the workman. I find no reason to interfere with the 
order of dismissal passed against Prabir Bouri. An award be drawn up in light of my above findings. Let copies of the 
Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India, New Delhi for information and 
Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERIEE, Presiding Officer 
नई दिल्‍ली, 3 जुलाई, 2025

Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. No. 8/202) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal- 
cum-Labour Court, Asansol as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of ECL 
and their workmen received by the Central Government on 0/07/2025.

MANIKANDAN. N, Dy. Director

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVT. INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL -CUM- LABOUR COURT,

REFERENCE CASE NO. 8 OF 202] 


PARTIES: Kalu Charan Suin 
Vs 

Management of Naba Kajora Colliery, ECL 
REPRESENTATIVES: 
For the Union/Workman: Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress 
For the Management of ECL: Mr. P. K. Das, Advocate. 
INDUSTRY: Coal. 
STATE: West Bengal. 
Dated: 3/0/2025 


AWARD 


In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of Sub-section (l) and Sub-section (2A) of Section 0 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (/4 of 947), the Government of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 


506 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


No. L-2202/44/202-IR(CM-ID) dated 29/09/202l has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer, that is the Management of Naba Kajora Colliery, Kajora Area of Eastern Coalfields Limited and their 
workmen for adjudication by this Tribunal. 


“Whether the action of the Management of M/s. Eastern Coalfields Ltd. in relation to its Kajora Area in 
imposing a punishment of dismissal on Shri Kalucharan Swain, Dresser of Naba Kajora Colliery under 
Kajora Area (U.M. No. 725526) w.e.f. /9-05-2020 is just and legal? If not, to what relief the workman is 
entitled to?” 


| On receiving Order No. L-2202/44/202-IR(CM-II) dated 29/09/202] from the Government of India 
Ministry of Labour, New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, a Reference case No. 8 of 202 was registered on 
0I/0/202] and an order was passed for issuing notice to the parties through registered post, directing them to appear 
and submit their written statements along with relevant documents in support of their claims and a list of witnesses. 


2. Union of Koyala Mazdoor Congress filed written statement on 2[/09/2022 on behalf of Kalu Charan Suin 
through Mr. Rakesh Kumar, President, Koyala Mazdoor Congress. Mr. P. K. Das, learned advocate appeared for ECL 
and filed written statement on same date i.e. 2/09/2022. Fact of the case disclosed in the written statement of the 
union is that Kalu Charan Suin was posted as Underground Dresser at Naba Kajora Colliery under ECL, having UM 
No. 25828. Due to his illness he was unable to attend duty from !6/09/209 till his recovery. When he reported to 
join his duty, management of the colliery did not allow him to join and issued a charge sheet bearing No. 
ECL/NKC/Mgr./2020-2/572 dated 2/2/209 for his unauthorized absence without information and also for his 
habitual absence in the preceding years 20l6, 20l7 and 20I8 which constituted gross misconduct as per Certified 
Standing Orders applicable to the company. Workman replied to the charge sheet but explanation was not found 
satisfactory. He also submitted papers regarding his medical treatment. Management of the colliery appointed Enquiry 
Officer and a domestic enquiry was conducted. Workman participated in the enquiry and submitted his explanation 
for alleged unauthorized absence and also produced treatment papers related to his illness. Enquiry Officer conducted 
domestic enquiry and submitted his report, finding workman guilty of charge. After completion of enquiry, a second 
show cause Notice was issued to the workman to which he replied. On recommendation of the colliery management, 
General Manager of the Area being the Competent Authority dismissed the workman from service by issuing a letter 
No. ECL/KA/APM/C-6/2020/0/87 dated 9/05/2020. Dismissed workman thereafter submitted his mercy appeal 
before the higher authority on 3/07/2020. Mercy appeal was not considered by the management. It is inter alia 
contended that the workman was absent from duty only for two months and twenty six days i.e. from 6/09/209 to 
2/2/20l9. Therefore, punishment of dismissal from service, which is an extreme punishment is harsh and 
disproportionate to the alleged misconduct. According to the union, punishment imposed in departmental proceeding 
should be proportionate to the misconduct. It is prayed that management of ECL should set aside order of dismissal 
and Kalu Charan Suin should be reinstated in service with full back wages. 


3 Management of ECL contested the Industrial Dispute by filing written statement. It has been asserted that 
Kalu Charan Suin was appointed as Dresser (UG) on 24/0/996 and was working at Naba Kajora Colliery. He was a 
habitual absentee for which he was reprimanded several times. It is inter alia contended that as per record workman 
was absent from duty without any intimation to the employer from !6/09/20I9 and continued to remain absent 
without intimation or authorization of leave. Management ultimately issued a charge sheet against the workman on 
2/2/209. A domestic enquiry was ordered and he participated in the enquiry proceeding held on 2/03/2020. 
Enquiry Officer conducted enquiry by following the principles of Natural Justice, by giving full opportunity to the 
charged workman to defend himself. Charge of unauthorized absence was proved beyond doubt. On the basis of 
finding of the Enquiry officer, General Manager, Kajora Area dismissed the workman by letter dated 9/05/2020. It is 
the case of the management that workman attended work only for forty six days in the year 206, thirty five days in 
the year 207 and one hundred forty two days in the year 20l8. Workman was charged for habitual absence. Further 
contention of the management is that the charged workman submitted mercy petition before the management after his 
dismissal. On previous occasions workman suffered impunities by way of warning and stoppage of three increments 
with cumulative effect in the year 207, demotion to lower cadre in the year 206, stoppage of three increments with 
cumulative effect in the year 20I5 and on several occasions, he was warned for his unauthorized absence. According 
to the management workman did not improve his conduct after several opportunities extended to him. Under such 
circumstances, it is urged that Industrial Dispute has no merit and workman has been dismissed from service for 
proper reasons and in a proper manner. 


4. Moot point for consideration before this Tribunal is “Whether the action of the Management of M/s. Eastern 
Coalfields Ltd. in imposing a punishment of dismissal against Shri Kalu Charan Suin, Dresser of Naba Kajora 
Colliery under Kajora Area (U.M. No. 25828) w.e.f. 9-05-2020 is just and legal? If not, to what relief the workman 
is entitled to?” 


5. Union examined Kalu Charan Suin as Workman Witness-I. He filed his affidavit-in-chief reiterating the case 
disclosed in written statement of the union. In course of his evidence, he produced the following documents:- 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढह़ 2, 947 507 


(i) Copy of Identity Card is produced as Exhibit ४७४-। 
(ii) Copy of charge sheet dated 2/2/209 is produced as Exhibit W-2 


(iii) Copy of order of dismissal issued by the Agent, Naba Kajora Colliery dated 8/06/2020 is produced as 
Exhibit W-3. 


(iv) Copy of mercy appeal dated 3/07/2020 addressed to the Director (Personnel), ECL is produced as 
Exhibit W-4. 


In cross-examination, workman admitted that he attended duty only for forty six days in the year 20I6, thirty five 
days in the year 20l7 and one hundred forty two days in the year 20l8. Workman tried to reason out his absence by 
deposing in cross-examination that he had reported for duty after short absence but the management did not allow him 
to join on the pretext that his medical papers were sent to the Area office and ultimately charge sheet was issued for 
his unauthorized absence. Witness, however stated that he did not make formal compliant before the Agent disclosing 
that he was not allowed to join duty on several occasions. In his later cross-examination, he admitted that he attended 
the enquiry proceeding. He further deposed that a second show cause Notice was issued to him. Witness also denied 
suggestion put to him that he was not suffering from illness at the time of his absence or that the company dismissed 
him from service for adequate reasons but did not produce any medical papers. 


6. Mr. Ramjee Tripathi for the management of ECL filed an affidavit-in-chief and was examined as 
Management Witness-I. Witness stated that workman received charge sheet and submitted his reply but the 
explanation was not found satisfactory for which a domestic enquiry was held in which he was found guilty of the 
charge of unauthorized absence and habitual absence. A second show cause Notice was issued to the workman. 
General Manager, Kajora Area after careful consideration of the charge sheet and entire enquiry proceeding, 
dismissed the workman from service on 9/05/2020. In course of his evidence, witness produced the following 
documents: - 


(i) Copy of charge sheet dated |2/ 2/209 is produced as Exhibit -। 
(ii) Copy of reply submitted by the workman against charge sheet is produced as Exhibit M-2. 


(iii) Copy of office order dated 04/03/2020 by which Ashis Mohan, Asstt. Manager (P) was appointed as the 
Enquiry Officer and H. K. Srivastav, Safety Officer as Management Representative is produced as Exhibit M-3. 


(iv) Copy of Notice of Enquiry dated !6/03/2020 is produced as Exhibit M-4. 

(v) Copy of Enquiry proceeding dated 2/03/2020 is produced as Exhibit M-5. 

(vi) Copy of second show cause Notice dated 04/04/2020 is produced as Exhibit M-6. 

(vii) Copy of reply dated 8/04/2020 against the second show cause Notice is produced as Exhibit M-7. 


(viii) | Copy of order of dismissal passed by the General Manager, Kajora Area dated 3/9.05.2020 is produced as 
Exhibit M-8. 


In course of his cross-examination, witness stated that the period of absence of the workman was two months and 
twenty six days. Management witness categorically stated that workman did not submit document relating to medical 
treatment along with his reply. Witness also denied suggestion that punishment imposed was disproportionate to the 
charges established against him. 


7. Mr. Rakesh Kumar arguing the case on behalf of the dismissed workman submitted that workman was absent 
for two months and twenty six days till charge sheet was issued on |2/l2/209 (Exhibit W-2/Exhibit ४-). It is 
submitted that workman participated in the enquiry proceeding and produced papers relating to his medical treatment 
issued from Khandra Health Centre. Union representative in his argument tried to focus on the fact that work of 
Dresser (UG) is hazardous in nature and management should have considered the precarious conditions in which the 
workman served before dismissing him at the drop of a hat only for his unauthorized absence for two months and 
twenty six days, which was for his illness. It is urged that punishment awarded is disproportionate to the misconduct 
and the workman should be reinstated in service. Union fairly submitted that though prayer has been made for back 
wages, justice would be served if workman is reinstated in service simpliciter. 


8. Management argued that workman not only remained absent in unauthorized manner without any intimation 
to the management but was also a habitual absentee and his performance in previous three years was extremely 
unsatisfactory. Several attempts were made to rectify him by imposing lighter punishment like stoppage of 
increments, demotion to lower post and issuing warnings but he did not serve even for a continuous period of one 
hundred ninety days which is the attendance requirement for continuous service in a year in Underground duty. Ld. 
Advocate argued that enquiry proceeding was held in a proper manner and opportunity was granted to the charged 
employee to defend his case and demolish charges levelled against him but both the charges i.e. habitual absence and 
unauthorized absence were established against the charged employee. A second show cause Notice was issued to the 
workman (Exhibit M-6). Workman replied to the second show cause Notice but there was no extenuating 


508 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


circumstance to take lighter view. The General Manager who is the Competent Authority ultimately directed the 
workman to be removed from duty (Exhibit M-8). In furtherance of such letter of dismissal, Agent communicated the 
same to the workman by office order dated 7/8.06.2020 (Exhibit W-3). Mr. Das contended that there is no merit in 
the Industrial Dispute and sought for dismissal of the same. 


9. Considered the rival contentions of the union and learned advocate for the management of ECL in light of 
pleading and evidence on record. In the instant case, Kalu Charan Suin was charge sheeted on !2/!2/209 wherein he 
was charged for his unauthorized absence for a period from 6/09/20I9 to 2/I2/209 without information and leave 
and also for his habitual absence from duty. Workman was directed to submit reply within seventy two hours. 
Workman submitted reply to the charge sheet which is produced as Exhibit M-2. In his reply it is submitted that he 
was under medical treatment at Khandra Rural Hospital and again he fell ill from 5/2/209 to 25/02/2020 and was 
unable to join duty. Nowhere in the reply workman stated that after recovery from illness and before issuance of 
charge sheet on 2/I2/209, he had reported to join his duty or was disallowed to join. It is therefore clear that the 
submission made in the written statement that he was disallowed to join duty is untrue and not reliable due to lack of 
evidence and want of consistency. No document relating to illness or medical treatment of the workman has been 
filed along with his reply. Management issued office order dated 04/03/2020 (Exhibit M-3) appointing Shri Ashis 
Mohan, Asstt. Manager (P), Madhabpur colliery to conduct the domestic enquiry. Notice of Enquiry was issued on 
6/03/2020 fixing 2/03/2020 as date for holding domestic enquiry. A copy of Notice of Enquiry is produced as 
Exhibit M-4. It is gathered from the enquiry proceeding (Exhibit M-5) that a domestic enquiry was held against the 
charged employee in respect of charges levelled under clause 26.29 and 26.23 of Certified Standing Orders applicable 
to the company. Content of the charge sheet was explained to the charged employee who understood the charges 
levelled against him. Workman was given opportunity to take assistance of co-workers or representatives of the Trade 
Union but he declined. Management Representatives were examined who clearly gave out that past performance of 
the workman and his attendance from the year 206 to 20I9 appears to be abysmal. It appears that he was warned on 
previous occasions for his unauthorized absence and three increments with cumulative effect were stopped by letter 
dated 2/03/20I5. He was demoted to lower post without any pay protection by letter dated 04/08/20l6 and was 
warned and three increments were stopped with cumulative effect by letter dated 29/ [/207., Workman did not cross- 
examine witnesses and admitted his guilt. It is stated that he suffered from viral fever, liver disorder and pain from 
7/09/209 to 06/2/209 and subsequently from 5/2/209 to 25/02/2020. There is clear admission that he did not 
inform the management nor did he avail any leave. He stated that he submitted medical certificate regarding his 
illness from Khandra (Ukhra) BPHC but in fact no document was submitted before the Enquiry officer nor before the 
Competent Authority of the company. Enquiry Officer held the workman guilty of charges in respect of clause 26.29 
and 26.23 of certified standing orders applicable to the company. A second show cause Notice dated 04/04/2020 was 
issued to the workman (Exhibit M-6) and a copy of enquiry proceeding was enclosed therewith for his perusal. 
Workman replied to the second show cause Notice once again admitting the charge (Exhibit M-7). Competent 
Authority on considering the entire material and reply to the second show cause Notice, dismissed the workman from 
service with effect from 08/05/2020 which was intimated to him in due course of time by way of order of dismissal 
issued by the Agent, Naba Kajora colliery, ECL. 


0. It emerges from the above facts and circumstances that there was no cogent reason to treat the charged 
employee in lenient manner. There was no mitigating circumstance nor any reliable document related to his medical 
treatment, claimed to have been undergone by the workman during the period of his absence. Management of ECL 
has carried out the enquiry proceeding in a proper manner providing reasonable opportunity to the workman to defend 
himself. Principle of Natural Justice has been duly complied in holding the domestic enquiry. A mercy petition has 
been filed by the workman but his claim has to be balanced with past conduct which shows that management 
provided several opportunities to the workman to rectify the conduct but he has not been able to fulfil the basic 
requirements. Under such circumstance the punishment of dismissal of the workman from service cannot be 
considered to be harsh or disproportionate to the nature of misconduct. I do not find any incongruity in the order of 
dismissal to interfere with the same. Therefore, the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest. Workman is found not 
entitled to the relief of reinstatement. 


Hence, 
ORDERED 


that the Industrial Dispute is dismissed on contest. Kalu Charan Suin, dismissed workman is not entitled to any relief 
of reinstatement in service or to back wages. Let an Award be drawn up in light of my above discussion. Let copies 
of Award be communicated to the Ministry for information and Notification. 


ANANDA KUMAR MUKHERIEE, Presiding Officer

AWARD 


The Government of India, Ministry of Labour by its order No. L- 2202/48/20I4-IR(CM-II) dated 8.8.204 
referred the following dispute under section 0()(d) of the I.D. Act, 947 for adjudication to this Tribunal between 
the management of M/s. Singareni Collieries Company Ltd., and their workman. The reference is, 


SCHEDULE 


“Whether the action of the General Manager, M/s. Singareni Collieries Company Ltd., Ramagundam-I Area, 
Godavrikhani, Karimnagar Distt., in terminating the services of Sri Akula Odelu, Ex. Coal Filler, GDK-I 
Inc., RG-I Area with effect from 29..998 is justified or not? If not, to what relief the applicant is entitled 
for? 


The reference is numbered in this Tribunal as I.D. No. 32/20I5 and notices were issued to the parties 
concerned. 


2. The averments made is the claim statement are as follows:- 


It is submitted in the representation of the Petitioner Union that, the workman Sri Akula Odelu was initially 
appointed on 3!.7.99] as a Badli Filler and by virtue of hard work and sincerity he was confirmed as Coal Filler 
w.e.f. .9.995. He was regular to his duties and performing his duties upto the satisfaction of all his superiors. 
While the matters stood thus, he was issued with a charge sheet dated 25.9. [998 alleging that the workman remained 


50 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


absent during the year 4997 which amounts to misconduct under company’s Standing Order No.25.25. 
Subsequently, one enquiry was conducted on 5.0.!998 and the Workman was not given any opportunity to defend 
his case. Enquiry officer proceeded with a presumption of guilt on the part of the concerned Workman. The reasons 
pleaded by the concerned workman were not considered by the enquiry officer and enquiry proceedings were treated 
as mere formality and after obtaining the signatures of the concerned Workman enquiry was closed though there were 
circular instructions to conduct the enquiry in the language known to the charge sheeted employee, enquiry 
proceedings were drafted in English and the concerned workman's signatures were obtained without even explaining 
its contents. Thereafter, a show cause notice was issued to which the Workman has submitted his reply. However, 
without consideration of the submissions made by the concerned Workman, he was dismissed from service w.e.f. 
29..998 vide proceeding No.P.RG.I/32/98/6059 dated 24..998. It is stated that the Workman has 
categorically stated about his inability to perform his duties regularly during the year 4997, which was only on 
account of his ill-health and other family problems. But without considering any of his submissions, the Workman 
was dismissed from service. It is also stated that the action of the Respondent’s management in dismissing the 
Workman from service is wholly illegal, arbitrary, violative of the principles of natural justice. The Workman has 
rendered 6 years of continuous service in the Respondent’s management. The Workman approached the Respondent 
to consider his case sympathetically, but the Respondent management did not pay any heed to it. Therefore, the 
Workman was constrained to approach this Tribunal to declare the impugned order issued by the Respondent is illegal 
and arbitrary and to set aside the same and consequently to direct the Respondent to reinstate the Workman into 
service duly granting all other attendant benefits such as continuity of service, and back wages etc.. 


3. Respondent filed counter denying the averments of the Petitioner/ Union as under: 


It is submitted that the present petition has been filed by the Workman Sri Akula Odelu, to direct the Respondent to 
reinstate the Workman into service with continuity of service and other attendant benefits and with full back wages, 
by setting aside the dismissal order dated 24..998. It is submitted that the Workman was dismissed from the 
services of the Company on proved charge of absenteeism w.e.f. 29..998 vide proceeding No.P.RG. |/32/986/6059 
dated 24.i.998. It is apparently clear that there is an abnormal delay of about 47 years in raising the dispute by 
the Workman; therefore, the petition is liable to be dismissed on the ground of delay and latches. It is prayed that 
this Hon'ble Tribunal may be pleased to decide the delay in raising the dispute as a preliminary issue. It is submitted 
that the delinquent workman was dismissed on proved charge after conducting a detailed domestic enquiry duly 
following the principles of natural justice. It is submitted that the Workman Sri Akula Odelu, Ex.Coal Filler, Gdk. | 
& 3 Incline of the Respondent Company, was initially appointed in the Company on 3].7.]99] as Badli Filler and 
later he was appointed as Coal Filler with effect from |.9.995. While so, in the year 997 he had put in just 0I8 
musters during the calendar year !997 and remained absent to duties unauthorisedly on all other days, for 347 days in 
the year, which constituted misconduct under Company's Standing Orders No.25.25. Hence, he was issued with a 
charge sheet bearing No. Gdk.l/6-G/98/2733, dated 25.9.998 under Company's Standing Orders No. 25.25 for the 
misconduct committed by him, which reads as follows: 


"25.25: Habitual late attendance or habitual absence from duty without sufficient cause." 


The Workman acknowledged the receipt of the charge sheet and submitted his written explanation admitting his 
absenteeism due to ill-health. His explanation was examined and found to be not satisfactory, hence an enquiry was 
ordered. An enquiry notice was issued advising the Workman to attend enquiry along with witnesses, if any, to defend 
his case. It is submitted that the enquiry was conducted on 5.0.998 adhering to the principles of natural justice. The 
Workman attended and fully participated in the enquiry and he was given full and fair opportunity to defend his case. 
Before commencing the enquiry proceedings, the Enquiry Officer had explained the enquiry procedure. The 
Workman having understood the procedure and at his free will took part in the enquiry proceedings. The Enquiry 
Officer had offered the opportunity of availing the services of defence assistant and workman took assistance of his 
co-worker Sri Odela Shankar, B.F., GDK. 6 Incline. The Presenting Officer and the management witnesses deposed 
their evidence in the presence of the Workman which was duly recorded by the Enquiry Officer and explained to the 
charge sheeted workman in Telugu. Further, the documentary evidence was produced by the management in the 
presence of the Workman to substantiate the charge leveled against him in the enquiry and explained in Telugu by the 
Enquiry Officer. The Workman did not cross examine the management witnesses when the opportunity was afforded 
to him and voluntarily pleaded guilty of charge leveled against him. In his deposition during the course of enquiry, 
the Workman submitted that he could not be regular to duties during the year 997 due to personal problems and ill- 
health and accepted that he remained absent from duty on all the dates covered in the charge sheet. The Petitioner 
contended that he remained absent from duty due to Renal Colic with back ache and produced a sick certificate which 
was found not genuine. Inspite of giving opportunity, he failed to establish the causes cited by him for his 
unauthorized absence from duty by submitting relevant documentary evidence. The Enquiry Officer on the basis of 
evidence adduced in the enquiry and after appreciating all the recorded evidence, submitted his report in which the 
Workman was held to be guilty of the charge levelled against him under Company's Standing Order No.25.25. It is 
submitted that the Workman was supplied copy of the Enquiry report and proceedings vide letter dated 28.0.998 
wherein he was advised to submit his representation if any, against the findings of the enquiry officer within seven 
days of receipt of the letter. The Workman acknowledged receipt of the letter along with its enclosures and 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 Sl] 


submitted his representation dated 8..!998. Since the charge framed and proved in the enquiry was grave and 
serious in nature warranting punishment of dismissal and as there was no extenuating circumstance to take a lenient 
view, the Workman was dismissed from Company's Services with effect from 29..998. Further, he remained 
absent from duty without sanctioned leave, sick or sufficient cause and did not bother to communicate to the unit 
authorities about his inability to attend to duties. He did not at least inform or communicate the reasons of his 
absence to the mine authorities at any point of time, which clearly establish the fact that he was not interested in his 
job. Therefore, the management was compelled to dismiss the Workman, from the service of the Company with 
effect from 29..998 on proved charge. It is to submit that in the Respondent Company, employes about 67,578 
persons. Naturally among such quantum of employees, few will surely go on authorised leave or report sick and in 
their places management provides employment to Badlis and it includes workmen, executives and supervisors. 
Production results will depend upon the overall attendance and performance of each and every individual. They are 
interlinked and inseparable. In this regard if anyone remains absent without prior leave or without any justified cause 
the work to be performed gets affected. Such unauthorised absence creates sudden void which at time is very 
difficult to fill up and there will be no proper planning and already planned schedules get suddenly disturbed without 
prior notice. That is the reason why the Respondent’s company is compelled to take severe action against the 
unauthorised absentees. It is submitted that the Workman should have been more careful and conscious of his 
responsibilities towards his family members and towards his job. For the fault and mistakes of the Workman he 
cannot hold the Respondent company responsible. It is the Workman himself who has to be blamed for this situation 
which is the result of his negligent behaviour and careless attitude towards his job. Therefore, prayed to dismiss the 
claim petition as devoid of merits. 


4. Workman didn't file any documents in support of his claim in evidence. Whereas Respondent has filed 
many documents of enquiry proceeding through list dated 6.3.2023. Despite sufficient opportunity Workman did 
not adduce any arguments and requested to treat the claim statement as his arguments. However, Respondent has 
filed written arguments. 


55 On the basis of rival pleadings of both the parties, following issues emerge for determination:- 
I Whether the departmental enquiry held against the Workman is legal and valid? 
Il. Whether the claim petition filed by the Workman is bad due to delay and latches? 


Ii. Whether the action of the General Manager, M/s. Singareni Collieries Company Ltd., Ramagundam-I Area, 
Godavrikhani, Karimnagar Distt., in terminating the services of Sri Akula Odelu, Ex. Coal Filler, GDK-l 
Inc., RG-I Area with effect from 29..998 is justified or not? 


IV. To what relief the Workman is entitled? 
Findings:- 
6. Issue No.I: - The Petitioner workman has conceded the legality and validity of departmental enquiry 


therefore, the domestic enquiry conducted by the Respondent is held legal and valid by the Tribunal vide order dated 
0.4.2023. 


Thus, Issue No.| is decided accordingly. 


7 Issue No.II:- This issue pertains to the question whether the industrial dispute raised by the Workman is 
barred by delay and laches. In this context the Learned Counsel for Respondent submitted that the Workman was 
dismissed from the service of the company on the proved charges of absenteeism w.e.f. 29.l.998 vide proceeding 
No.P.RG. /32/98/6059 dated 24..998. Therefore, it is apparently clear that there is an abnormal delay of ]7 years 
in raising the dispute by the Workman. Therefore, the petition is liable to be dismissed on the ground of delay and 
latches. Further, it is contended that as per provision contained under section 2A(2) the applicant feeling aggrieved 
by the order of employer terminating the services of the individual Workman for discharging, dismissing or 
retrenching he will make an application to the Labour Court or Tribunal before the expiry of 3 years from the date of 
discharge or dismissal, retrenchment or otherwise termination of services. Whereas in the present case the Workman 
has raised the industrial dispute with an inordinate delay of 7 years from the date of termination from service i.e., 
24..998. Therefore, in view of the above the claim petition is barred by delay and latches. 


8 Perused the record. Undisputedly, Workman in the present case has been terminated from service vide 
order dated 24..998 with effect from 29.!.998 passed by Disciplinary Authority of the Respondent. Whereas 
the industrial dispute has been raised in the year 205 against the said termination order when it has been referred for 
adjudication vide letter dated 5.5.20l5 of Government of India Ministry of Labour, New Delhi. Thus, there is an 
inordinate delay of about 47 years in raising the industrial dispute in the present matter. However, Workman has not 
furnished any explanation regarding inordinate delay in raising the dispute against his termination order from the 
service by the Respondent. 


52 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


In this context, reference of few decisions of Hon’ble Supreme Court are relevant as discussed hereunder:- 


In K RReddy Vs. Industrial Tribunal-II, Hyderabad, Hon’ble Court held:- 


“The Supreme Court extensively considered the scope of relevant provisions and precedent decisions and held that 


there was inordinate, unexplained delay in referring the dispute.’ 


, 


In the case of Assistant Engineer, CAD, Kota and Dhan Kumwar, CA No.6473, 2006 III LLJ, the Hon'ble Apex 
Court have held: 


“workman raising the dispute eight years after termination of service —therefore relief by Labour Court should not 
have been granted to workman.” 


In Haryana State Co-operative Land Development Bank and Neelam, 2005 I LLJ, the Apex Court held: 


“Though no time limit prescribed for raising industrial dispute, but stale claim, could not be entertained - 


approaching Labour Court after delay of more than 7 years. Held:- justified refusal of relief in this case.’ 


, 


In the case of Sri Prabhakar Vs. Joint Director Sericulture Department in Civil Appeal decision dated 7.9.5 
Hon'ble Apex Court have held:- 


“40) On the basis of aforesaid discussion, we summarise the legal position as under: 


“An industrial dispute has to be referred by the appropriate Government for adjudication and the workman 
cannot approach the Labour Court or Industrial Tribunal directly, except in those cases which are covered 
by Section 2A of the Act. Reference is made under Section /9 of the Act in those cases where the appropriate 
Government forms an opinion that ‘any industrial dispute exists or is apprehended’. The words ‘industrial 
dispute exists' are of paramount importance unless there is an existence of an industrial dispute (or the dispute 
is apprehended or it is apprehended such a dispute may arise in near future), no reference is to be made. Thus, 
existence or apprehension of an industrial dispute is a sine qua non for making the reference. No doubt, at the 
time of taking a decision whether a reference is to be made or not, the appropriate Government is not to go into 
the merits of the dispute. Making of reference is only an administrative function. At the same time, on the basis 
of material on record, satisfaction of the existence of the industrial dispute or the apprehension of an industrial 
dispute is necessary. Such existence/apprehension of industrial dispute, thus, becomes a condition precedent, 
though it will be only subjective satisfaction based on material on record. Since, we are not concerned with the 
satisfaction dealing with cases where there is apprehended industrial dispute, discussion that follows would 
confine to existence of an industrial dispute. Dispute or difference arises when one party make a demand and 
other party rejects the same. It is held by this Court in number of cases that before raising the industrial dispute 
making of demand is a necessary pre-condition. In such a scenario, if the services of a workman are terminated 
and he does not make the demand and/or raise the issue alleging wrongful termination immediately thereafter 
or within reasonable time and raises the same after considerable lapse of period, whether it can be said that 
industrial dispute still exist. Since there is no period of limitation, it gives right to the workman to raise the 
dispute even belatedly. However, if the dispute is raised after a long period, it has to be seen as to whether such 
a dispute still exists? Thus, notwithstanding the fact that law of limitation does not apply, it is to be shown by 
the workman that there is a dispute in praesenti. For this purpose, he has to demonstrate that even if 
considerable period has lapsed and there are laches and delays, such delay has not resulted into making the 
industrial dispute seized to exist. Therefore, if the workman is able to give satisfactory explanation for these 
laches and delays and demonstrate that the circumstances discloses that issue is still alive, delay would not 
come in his way because of the reason that law of limitation has no application. On the other hand, if because 
of such delay dispute no longer remains alive and is to be treated as “dead”, then it would be non-existent 
dispute which cannot be referred. Take, for example, a case where the workman issues notice after his 
termination, questioning the termination and demanding reinstatement. He is able to show that there were 
discussions from time to time and the parties were trying to sort out the matter amicably. Or he is able to show 
that there were assurances by the Management to the effect that he would be taken back in service and because 
of these reasons, he did not immediately raise the dispute by approaching the labour authorities seeking 
reference or did not invoke the remedy under Section 2A of the Act. In such a scenario, it can be treated that the 
dispute was live and existing as the workman never abandoned his right. However, in this very example, even if 
the notice of demand was sent but it did not evoke any positive response or there was specific rejection by the 
Management of his demand contained in the notice and thereafter he sleeps over the matter for number of 
years, it can be treated that he accepted the factum of his termination and rejection thereof by the Management 
and acquiesced into the said rejection. Take another example. A workman approaches the Civil Court by filing 
a suit against his termination which was pending for number of years and was ultimately dismissed on the 
ground that Civil Court did not have jurisdiction to enforce the contract of personal service and does not grant 
any reinstatement. At that stage, when the suit is dismissed or he withdraws that suit and then involves the 
machinery under the Act, it can lead to the conclusion that dispute is still alive as the workman had not 
accepted the termination but was agitating the same; albeit in a wrong forum. In contrast, in those cases where 
there was no agitation by the workman against his termination and the dispute is raised belatedly and the delay 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 53 


or laches remain unexplained, it would be presumed that he had waived his right or acquiesced into the act of 
termination and, therefore, at the time when the dispute is raised it had become stale and was not an ‘existing 
dispute’. In such circumstances, the appropriate Government can refuse to make reference. In the alternative, 
the Labour Court/Industrial Court can also hold that there is no “industrial dispute” within the meaning 
of Section 2(k) of the Act and, therefore, no relief can be granted. 


447) We may hasten to clarify that in those cases where the Court finds that dispute still existed, though raised 
belatedly, it is always permissible for the Court to take the aspect of delay into consideration and mould the 
relief. In such cases, it is still open for the Court to either grant reinstatement without back wages or lesser 
back wages or grant compensation instead of reinstatement. We are of the opinion that the law on this issue has 
to be applied in the aforesaid perspective in such matters.” 


Moreover, in the case of Chief Engineer (Construction) Vs. Keshav Rao, 2005 JCR 96, Hon’ble Supreme Court 
have held that, the delay of a one year and five months in issuing a notice appears to us to be significant. Here, in the 
present matter workman has not issued any notice or raised dispute against his termination in any forum for a long 
period of about fourteen years and his claim against dismissal has become stale due to inordinate delay and latches. 


9. Therefore, in view of the fore gone discussion and law laid down by the Hon'ble Apex Court in the instant 
case industrial dispute raised by Workman against his termination order dated 24.i.998 with effect from 
29..998 after a lapse of |7 years from the date of his termination without furnishing any reasonable explanation 
or notice, has become stale and not maintainable. Therefore, the petition filed by the Workman is not maintainable 
due to inordinate delay and latches and such a dispute has become stale and not maintainable. 


This issue is decided against the Workman. 


0. Issue No.III:- This issue pertains to question whether the action of Respondent management in terminating the 
services of the Petitioner Workman Sri Akula Odelu, Ex. Coal Filler, GDK-l incline, RG-l area with effect from 
29..998 is justified or not. In this context, Respondent contended that the delinquent Workman was dismissed 
from the services of the company on proved charges of absenteeism vide proceeding No.P.RG. I/32/98/6059 dated 
24..998 w.e.f. 29.I.998. The delinquent Workman was dismissed on proved charge after conducting 
departmental enquiry duly following the principles of natural justice. Further, it is submitted that the Workman 
Sri Akula Odelu, Coal filler was initially appointed in the company on 3.7.99] as badli filler and later he was 
appointed as coal filler w.e.f. .9.!995. Further, it is submitted that while he was working at GDK-|! incline, RG-| 
Area, he had put in just 0l8 musters during the calendar year 997 and remained absent unauthorisedly on all other 
dates which constituted misconduct under Company’s Standing Order No. 25.25. Hence, he was issued with the 
chargesheet dated 25.9.]998 under Company’s Standing Order No.25.25 for habitual absence from duty without 
sufficient cause. Further, it is submitted that Workman acknowledged the receipt of the chargesheet and submitted 
his explanation wherein he has submitted that he has put in less musters during the year 997 purely due to ill-health, 
viz., suffering from U.T.I.O and he remained absent from duty in the year [997 and requested to consider his 
sickness, keep aside the charge sheet and assured to be careful in future and discharge his duties regularly. His 
explanation was examined and found to be not satisfactory hence an enquiry was ordered. 


Il. Further, it is submitted that enquiry was conducted on 5.0.998 adhering to the principles of natural 
justice. Petitioner workman attended and fully participated in the enquiry and he was given full and fair opportunity 
to defend his case. Before commencing the enquiry proceedings the Enquiry Officer had explained the enquiry 
procedure to workman and he was offered the opportunity of availing the services of defence assistant but the 
Workman did not avail the same. The management witnesses deposed their evidence in the presence of the Workman 
which was duly recorded by the Enquiry Officer and explained to the charge sheeted Workman in Telugu. Further, 
documentary evidence produced by the management in the presence of the Workman to substantiate the charge 
levelled against him in the enquiry and explained in Telugu by the Enquiry Officer. Workman did not cross examine 
the management witnesses despite opportunity was accorded to him. The Workman has examined himself in defence 
and in his statement he has stated that he admits the charge levelled against him under Company’s Standing Order 
No.25.25. Further, he voluntarily admit that he remained absent from duty without leave, sick, permission or 
information, on the dates as mentioned in the chargesheet and he remained absent unauthorisedly due to his ill-health. 
Witness was cross examined by the Respondent and in his cross examination witness states that regarding sickness he 
has not informed the Respondent management and he has not submitted any medical certificate in the office. Further, 
witness states that he has no medical slips, treatment prescriptions or diagnosis bills etc., in support of his statement 
that he fell sick during the absence period. Further, witness admits that he was suffering from muscles pain and leg 
pains as per prescriptions. Thus, from the above statement of the Workman recorded during the enquiry it manifests 
that the Workman has admitted the charge levelled against him and he failed to furnish any explanation regarding his 
unauthorised absence from duty. The Enquiry Officer on the basis of evidence adduced in the enquiry and after 
appreciating all the record of evidence, submitted his report in which the Workman was held to be guilty of the charge 
levelled against him under Company’s Standing Order No.25.25._ Workman acknowledged the receipt of the enquiry 
report by letter dated 28.0.998 and submitted his representation in detail dated I8..998 stating that he got no 
other source of earning and assured to be regular to duties. Moreover, workman did not dispute the enquiry 


5i4 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


proceedings and findings of the Enquiry Officer. The Disciplinary Authority after going through the entire enquiry 
proceedings, explanation letter dated 8.l!.998 of the Workman and after evaluating all the evidence on record 
confirmed the findings of the Enquiry Officer since the charge framed and proved in enquiry was grave and serious in 
nature warranting punishment of dismissal and as also there was no extenuating circumstances to take a lenient view, 
the Workman was dismissed from service with effect from 29.ll.998 vide letter dated 24.!.998. Further, 
Respondent submitted that the Workman was a chronic and habitual absentee and didn't put in at least 90 
attendances as expected from an underground Workman in any of the year. Further, it is submitted that he remained 
absent from duty without sanctioned leave, sufficient cause and did not bother to communicate to the unit authorities 
about his inability to attend to duties. 


2. In view of the submission made by the Respondent, perused the record of enquiry proceeding. The record 
of enquiry proceeding goes to reveal that the delinquent Workman was chargesheeted vide chargesheet dated 
25.9.998 under Company’s Standing Order No.25.25 for habitual absence from duty without sufficient cause. The 
workman was chargesheeted for absence from duty without sanctioned leave or sufficient cause during the year 997. 
The charge sheet was served upon workman. The delinquent Workman submitted his explanation to the charge sheet 
and enquiry was conducted and during the enquiry in support of charge two witnesses were examined by the 
Respondent, i.e., Sri K.V. Subba Rao, Oftg. Office Supdt., and Sri R.C. Malakondaiah, Paysheet Clerk. Sri R.C. 
Malakondaiah has stated that during the year 4997 January to December the delinquent Workman remained absent 
from duty without any sanctioned leave or sufficient cause. Despite opportunity to cross examine Workman did not 
cross examine the Management witnesses. Thus, statement of the management witnesses remained uncontraverted 
regarding the allegation of the charges against the Workman that he remained absent from duties without sanctioned 
leave or sufficient cause during the year 997. Further, delinquent Workman has also examined himself in evidence 
as witness and in his statement workman Sri Akula Odelu has stated, admitting the charge levelled against him that, 
he had remained absent from duty on the dates and period mentioned in the charge sheet and he was not granted any 
leave for these days. Further, he stated that he has not given any intimation to the company about his absence from 
duty and he has no record to show about his sickness. Further, the CSE was cross examined. Thus, on going 
through the evidence recorded during the enquiry it is established that the Workman remained absent from duty 
unauthorisedly without sanction of leave during the year क्‍9970 on the dates as mentioned in the chargesheet. 
Moreover, he was served with the copy of the documents and enquiry report and he submitted his explanation and 
did not dispute the findings of the enquiry proceedings and legality of the order of Disciplinary Authority. Thus, 
keeping in view of the facts and circumstances and evidence available on record and in view of the seriousness of the 
charge levelled against the delinquent Workman, Disciplinary Authority has passed the order to dismiss the 
Workman from service of the company with effect from 29.!.998. Therefore, in view of the above the order of 
dismissal of the Workman from service does not suffer from any procedural infirmity or illegality or perversity. 


3. Although, Petitioner workman has taken the plea that due to his sickness and other family problems he 
could not be regular to his duties during the year !997, thus, he also admitted the absent period during the year 997 
as alleged in the chargesheet. As per chargesheet he had put in only I8 days of attendance during the year 4997 and 
this fact has been admitted by the Workman in his statement recorded during the enquiry. But he has not submitted 
any document in evidence in support of his plea that he was regularly ill during the absence period and he has gone 
under treatment for the illness. Further, he failed to furnish any explanation that why he did not intimate about his 
absence to the Respondent management and also why he did not report sickness at the Respondent’s hospital. 
Therefore, for the want of any evidence in support of the plea, argument of the Petitioner workman of illness is not 
tenable. He has taken the plea that he could not attend his duty during the year !997 due to ill-health. Whereas the 
chargesheet dated 25.9.998 was issued to the Workman for conducting the enquiry for unauthorized absence from 
duty during the year 997. Further, the record of enquiry proceeding goes to reveal that the Workman was served 
with chargesheet and documents and he participated in the enquiry at every stage of proceedings. He was also 
accorded the opportunity to cross examine witness but he did not avail it. Therefore, the plea of the Petitioner 
workman that the termination order has been passed by Respondent in violation of principles of natural justice is not 
tenable. Thus, the said plea of Workman is not tenable. 


4. In this context, the reference of decisions of Hon’ble Supreme Court are relevant wherein Hon’ble 
Supreme Court have held that, the misconduct of habitual absence from duty unauthorisedly by the employee is a 
serious and grave nature. 


In State of U.P. V. Ashok Kumar Singh 996 () SCC 302, wherein the Apex Court had held:- 


“Having notices the fact that the first Respondent has absented himself from duty without level on several occasions, 
we are unable to appreciate the High Court's observation that ‘his absence from duty would not amount to such a 
grave charge. Even otherwise on the facts of this case, there was no justification for the High Court to interfere with 
the punishment holding that 'the punishment does not commensurate with the gravity of the charge’ especially when 
the High Court concurred with the findings of the Tribunal on facts. No case for interference with the punishment is 
made out.” 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 55 


In the case of North Eastern Karnataka R.T. Corpn. v. Ashappa decided on 2 May, 2006 wherein, the Apex 
Court had held:- 


“Remaining absent for a long time, in our opinion, cannot be said to be a minor misconduct. The Appellant 
runs a fleet of buses. It is a statutory organization. It has to provide public utility services. For running the 
buses, the service of the conductor is imperative. No employer running a fleet of buses can allow an employee 
to remain absent for a long time. The Respondent had been given opportunities to resume his duties. Despite 
such notices, he remained absent. He was found not only to have remained absent for a period of more than 
three years, his leave records were seen and it was found that he remained unauthorisedly absent on several 
occasions. In this view of the matter, it cannot be said that the misconduct committed by the Respondent herein 
has to be treated lightly. 


In Delhi Transport Corporation v. Sardar Singh [(2004) 7 SCC 574], the Apex Court held-: 


"II. Conclusions regarding negligence and lack of interest can be arrived at by looking into the period of 
absence, more particularly, when same is unauthorised. Burden is on the employee who claims that there was 
no negligence and/or lack of interest to establish it by placing relevant materials. Clause (ii) of para 4 of the 
Standing Orders shows the seriousness attached to habitual absence. In clause (i) thereof, there is requirement 
of prior permission. Only exception made is in case of sudden illness. There also conditions are stipulated, non- 
observance of which renders the absence unauthorised." 


State of U.P. v. Sheo Shanker Lal Srivastava and Others [(2006) 3 SCC 276], 


it was opined that, “the Industrial Courts or the High Courts would not normally interfere with the quantum of 
punishment imposed upon by the Respondent stating: "It is now well-settled that principles of law that the High 
Court or the Tribunal in exercise of its power of judicial review would not normally interfere with the quantum 
of punishment. Doctrine of proportionality can be invoked only under certain situations. It is now well-settled 
that the High Court shall be very slow in interfering with the quantum of punishment unless it is found to be 
shocking to one's conscience." 


The Hon’ble Apex Court in the case of Management Coal India Ltd. v. Mukul Kumar Choudhary Civil Appeal 
5762-5763 of 2009 decided on 24.08.2009 laid down the test of proportionality of punishment and held:- 


“One of the test to be applied while dealing with the question of punishment would be: would any reasonable 
employer have imposed such punishment in like circumstances? Obviously, a reasonable employer is expected 
to take into consideration measure, magnitude and degree of misconduct and all other relevant circumstances 
and exclude irrelevant matters before imposing punishment.” 


In the case of Maharashtra State Road Transport Corporation Vs. Dilip Uttam Jayabhay, the 2022 LLR page 
26, wherein the Hon’ble Apex Court held:- 


“once the enquiry finding is held to be fair and proper, industrial Tribunal or Labour Court lacks jurisdiction to 
interfere with the quantum of punishment unless the same is shockingly disproportionate to the gravity of conduct.” 


Therefore, in view of the fore gone discussion and a law laid down by the Hon'ble Apex Court I am of the considered 
opinion that the dismissal order passed by the Respondent management was proportionate and commensurate with the 
gravity of the charges hence deserves to be upheld. There is no illegality or impropriety or perversity in the 
impugned order. 


This issue is decided against the Workman and in favour of the Respondent. 


5. Issue No.IV:- In view of the finding given at Issue Nos. I , II and III the Workman is not entitled for any 
relief, hence the petition is liable to be dismissed. 


This issue is answered accordingly. 
AWARD 


In view of discussion and finding arrived at Issues No. I, II & II, the action of the General Manager, M/s. 
Singareni Collieries Company Ltd., Ramagundam-I Area, Godavrikhani, Karimnagar Distt., in terminating the 
services of Sri Akula Odelu, Ex. Coal Filler, GDK-I Inc., RG-I Area with effect from 29..998 is held justified. 
The workman is not entitled to any relief as prayed for. Therefore, the claim petition is dismissed as devoid of 
merits. Reference is answered accordingly. 


Award is passed accordingly. Transmit.

the 8™ day of June, 2025.

Government hereby publishes the Award (ID No. 28/204) of the Central Government Industrial Tribunal-cum- 
Labour Court NO.II, New Delhi as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute between the Management of 
Ministry of Health Sarvices, and their workmen, received by the Central Government on 03/07/2025.
