AWARD 


The Government of India, Ministry of Labour by its order No. L-2202/45/20i3-IR(CM.I]) dated 30. 5. 
203 referred the following dispute under section 0(l)(d) of the ILD. Act, 947 for adjudication to this Tribunal 
between the management of M/s. Singareni Collieries Company Limited and their workman. The reference is, 
“Whether the action of the General Manager, M/s. Singareni Collieries Com Ltd, Bhupalpally area, 
Warangal district, in awarding punishment of reversion to a lower stage by reducing four increments/ 
SPRAs in the present scale of pay with effect from |. l!. 2007 with cumulative effect in respect of Sri 
Pantangi Rajam, Trammer, KTK-! Inc., SCCL, Bhupalapally area, Warangal district(AP) is justified or not? 
If not, to what relief the applicant is entitled for?” 
The reference is numbered in this Tribunal as I.D. No.68/20l3 and notices were issued to the parties 


concerned. 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 549 
2: The averments made is the claim statement are as follows: 

It is submitted that, the concerned workman was appointed in the year 983 as Badli Filler and by virtue of his hard 
work and sincerity, he was confirmed as Coal Filler and later promoted as Trammer. While the workman was 
working a Trammer at KTK-I Incline. a charge sheet dt.27-06-2005 was issued alleging that having booked IN 
muster on 27-06-2005, he did not go to distribution point and instigated other workman not to go to distribution point 
which resulted in illegal strike. On receipt of the above charge sheet workman has submitted his reply on 30-06-2005 
not only denying the charge, but also explained all the factual aspects. However, without considering of any of the 
submissions made by the concerned workman, an enquiry was initiated in a pre-determined manner. During the 
enquiry, the concerned workman was not afforded any opportunity, much less valid in nature. It is submitted that, the 
enquiry officer, the presenting officer and all others know pretty well that, the concerned workman was an illiterate 
and more specifically he is not aware of English language. As per the circulars issued by the Management, the 
enquiry has to be conducted in the language known to the workman and has to be explained before commencement of 
enquiry. However, in the instant case, neither the procedure of enquiry was explained nor the proceedings of enquiry 
drafter in the language known to the concerned workman. Having typed the proceedings of enquiry in English and 
the signatures of the concerned workman were obtained without even explaining the contents. As a result of the 
above, the concerned workman was put to great prejudice which resulted in imposing of penalty. It is submitted that 
during the enquiry, the workman was neither provided the opportunity to lead the evidence nor he was permitted to 
cross examine the management witnesses. In fact, the concerned workman was not made aware of the fact that he can 
avail the services of a defense assistant. As a result of above improper conduct of enquiry, the concerned workman 
put to great prejudice which resulted in issuance of order of punishment. It is submitted that the charge alleged against 
the workman itself is not correct, as, the management witness cited the name of the concerned workman, he was 
implicated in this false case. In fact, the concerned workman need not be present at the common distribution point 
being conducted by the management witness. Without application of mind, though the concerned workman was not 
responsible for the charge, he was treated as delinquent and by conducting a farce enquiry he was imposed with a 
penalty, which is incorrect. As a matter of fact, leaving the other workman, who did not respond to his call, the 
management witness quoted workman’s name and the disciplinary proceedings were initiated against the concerned 
workman on a pick and choose method. The above action on the part of the Respondents is not only unjustifiable but 
also discriminatory in nature. As a matter of fact, the concerned workman was directed to work as Jig Trammer at 24 
Dip District of 3 Seam. The concerned workman along with other Trammers went underground to carry out their 
duties as Trammer. The Trammers were not at all on strike whereas the coal fillers were on strike. Therefore, the 


workman, though narrated all the above factual aspects, which were remained unrebutted during the course of 


550 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
enquiry, despite, enquiry officer came to a perverse conclusion that, the charge alleged against the concerned 
workman was held proved. It is no more a res integra that, the enquiry officer should act like a judge and he cannot 
act like a representative of the management. Further, it is the bounden duty of the enquiry officer to analyze the 
evidence on record and come to an independent conclusion without being influenced by either the presenting officer 
or by any other management representatives. | However, in the instant case, the enquiry officer acted like a 
representative of the management and proceeded with a preconceived notion, as if, the concerned workman is guilty 
of charge. A bare perusal of findings of the enquiry officer clearly establish that, they are the outside the scope of 
proceedings of enquiry. It is submitted that though the presenting officer relied upon several documents to hold the 
concerned workman as guilty, none of those documents were shown or furnished to the concerned workman before or 
during the course of enquiry. Even the presenting officer has submitted those documents after the conclusion of 
evidence of the workman and exclusively relying upon those documents, enquiry officer came to a perverse 
conclusion that the concerned workman is guilty of charge. Further, it is submitted that though the strict rules of 
evidence are not applicable to the departmental enquiries, but the departmental enquiries cannot be treated as mere 
sham, more specifically when the civil rights of the delinquent employees are being deprived, such as reduction of 
pay. It is submitted that the charge alleged against the concerned workman was the he instigated other workman to 
go on strike on 27.6.2005. However, there is no evidence to support the charge alleged. Simply because, the 
management witness stated that the concerned workman did not stop the other workman of 3 Seam from going on 
illegal strike, it cannot be treated as misconduct. With great respect, the appreciation of evidence of record by the 
enquiry officer is absolutely hopeless and perverse in nature. Instigating the workmen to go on strike is different 
than not stopping the workmen from going on illegal strike. It is submitted that while deposing before the enquiry 
officer, the management witness never stated that he gave a complaint to the management on 27.6.2005. Even in the 
charge sheet, there was no indication about the alleged complaint. No such document was either pleaded or filed 
before the enquiry officer till the deposition of the workman. As the enquiry officer has relied upon the said 
document and the impugned order was issued basing on such perverse findings of the enquiry officer, penalty 
imposed on the concerned workman is liable to be set aside and all the increments deferred are liable to be restored 
back along with all consequential benefits. It is submitted that the enquiry officer refers to loss of 340 tonnes of coal 
worth Rs.3,39,520/- on 27.6.2005 in 3 shifts, there was no evidence to that extent during the enquiry. Having 
permitted the presenting officer to submit the documents after conclusion of deposition by the concerned workman, 
the enquiry officer erroneously concludes that "since there is no denial from the workman in this regard and the 
document submitted by the Presenting Officer to the effect that SCCL Company was declared as Public Utility 


service and participation in the strike is prohibited vide Cir. No. CRP/Per/IR/E/ISI/357 dt. 05/07.03.05, and also the 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 55] 
Telugu version, which was also displayed on Notice board of the Mine on 3.03.05 and also Form 'N' and Form C' 
regarding commencement and termination of the strike held from 27.06.05 to 29.06.05 have also been sent to the 
authorities concerned and also a charge sheeted employee confirmed he had also participated in the strike". The above 
findings of the enquiry officer are wholly perverse in nature. The penalty imposed on the concerned workman 
basing on such perverse findings is liable to be set aside. Be that as it may, the impugned office order 
No.BHP/PER/20-D/4053 dated 3.0.2007 issued by the Respondent imposing the punishment of reversion to a 
lower stage by reducing four increments/SPRAS in the present scale of pay with effect from |..2007 with 
cumulative effect is without any basis. The impugned order passed by the Respondent is cryptic and does not 
disclose any reasons, much less valid in nature. It is to humbly submit that any order which affects the civil the 
rights of the employees should contain the reasons. In the instant case, impugned order does not contain any reasons. 
Therefore, the impugned order is liable to be set aside on this ground alone. It is submitted that aggrieved by the 
above penalty imposed by the Respondent, the workman approached the Respondent requesting to restore the 
increments as he was implicated in the above false case. However, when nothing resulted positively, he has 
approached the Petitioner union to which the concerned workman was a member. In these circumstances, the 
concerned workman has no other alternative remedy, except to invoke the jurisdiction of this Hon'ble Court. Hence 
prayed to set aside the impugned order dated 3l.0.2007 duly restoring all the increments deferred pursuant to 


impugned order. 


3. Respondent filed counter denying the averments of the Petitioner_as under: 


It is submitted that the charge sheeted workman was initially appointed as Badli filler on 5.06.983, and later he was 
promoted as Trammer. While he was working as Trammer at KTK-! Incline, Bhupalapalli Area he was issued with a 
chargesheet vide Lr.No.KTK.I/BHP/R-007/2782, dated 27.06.2005 under Company's Standing Orders No.25.], 


25.6 and 25.24 for the misconduct committed by him which reads as:- 


235(74)- Going on illegal Strike either singly or with other workman without giving /4 days previous notice. 


23([476). Any breach of the Mines Act, /9352 or any other Act or any Rules or Regulations or by laws there 


under. 

25(24): Sabotage or causing willful damage to work in progress or property of the company. 
Further, it is submitted that on 27.6.2005 the workman booked his IN muster and did not go to distribution point for 
common distribution of No. | Seam and No.3 Seam at manway, even though a notice was displayed on the notice 
board well in advance informing of common distribution from I Shift of 27.6.2005, and demanded for distribution of 
No.l Seam and No.3 Seam separately. Further, he has instigated all other workman not to go to distribution point 


which has resulted in an illegal strike in I shift of 27.6.2005 which continued up to the end of the I shift of 29.6.2005. 


552 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
The company lost around 343 tones of production output worth Rs.3,39,570/- due to this illegal strike. The above 
Charge Sheet was acknowledged by him on 5.7.2005 and he has submitted his explanation dated.30.6.2005 which 
has been found to be not satisfactory. He was issued notice of enquiry advising him to attend the enquiry along with 
his witnesses. The Enquiry was commenced on 8.0.2005 and continued on 04.0.2006 and finally the enquiry was 
closed on 28.02.2006. The charge sheeted workman in the case attended and fully participated in the enquiry. _ It is 
submitted that the Enquiry Officer after appreciating and analyzing each evidence adduced in the enquiry held that the 
charges against the workman were duly proved against him under Company's Standing Order 
No.25(I]),25(6),25(24). ‘It is pertinent to mention here that mere participation in a illegal strike itself is a 
misconduct under Company's Standing Order No.25(/), and the charge sheeted workman himself has deposed in the 
enquiry that all the trammers including himself returned their Cap Lamps to Lamp Room and gone home in I shift of 
27.6.2005 thereby admitting that he has participated in the strike. The workman was supplied with all the documents 
including enquiry proceedings and enquiry report vide Second Show Cause notice dated 23.7.2007 giving him 7 days 
time to represent if any against the findings of the enquiry report, the charge sheeted workman acknowledged the said 
notice and submitted his representation dated 30.7.2007 in English which has been examined and as there were no 
new points to consider in his representation the Disciplinary Authority after going through the entire enquiry 
proceedings and after evaluating all the evidence on record concurred with the findings of the enquiry officer, and as 
the charges framed and proved in the enquiry were grave and serious in nature warranting punishment with that of 
dismissal, the management has taken a lenient view to give an opportunity to the delinquent workman to improve 
himself, passed the impugned order No.BHP/PER/20-D/4053, 3i.0.2007 imposing the penalty of 'Reversion to a 
lower stage by reducing four increments/SPRAs in the present scale of pay w.e.f.0..2007 with cumulative effect.’ 
in the strike. It is submitted that at the outset the Enquiry Officer explained the enquiry procedure in Telugu and in 
fact the enquiry proceedings were held in Telugu following all the principle of natural justice. The charge sheeted 
workman has given his consent to record the proceedings in English while the enquiry proceedings were actually held 
in Telugu and further explained to him in Telugu before obtaining his signature. Further, the contention of the 
Petitioner that the workman is illiterate is far from truth. In fact the workman put his signature in all the enquiry 
proceedings and all other evidences in English, and submitted his explanation dated 30.07.2007 to the show cause 
notice No. KTKI/BHP/R-008/3776 dated 23.07.2007 in English. The allegation that the concerned workman was an 
illiterate is denied. It is submitted that all the principles of Natural Justice were followed in the enquiry giving every 
opportunity to the charge sheeted workman to defend his case, including extending the opportunity of availing a 
defense assistance which he declined. Further, he refused to cross examine the management witness in the enquiry, 


as such the contention of the Petitioner that the opportunity of defense assistance and cross examination of 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 553 
management witness was not given to the workman is denied. Further, it is submitted that workman has instigated 
all the other workman not to go to distribution point which has resulted in an illegal strike in I shift of 27.6.2005 
which continued up to the end of the I shift of 29.6.2005 as such the charge sheet was issued to him. Further, it is to 
submit that Sri Govind Chandra Murmu, Incharge Overman of 3 Seam has given a written complaint dated 
27.06.2005 to Dy.General Manager, KTK.I 6 Incline stating that Sri Panthangi Rajam and one other workman refused 
to come and took all the workman away from the common deposed by him as distribution point, and the same proof 
of the same. was a management witness in the enquiry. The charge sheeted workman did not cross examine him in the 
enquiry; thereby the nexus between the workman and the illegal strike has been clearly established. As such the 
contention of the Petitioner that the workman was implicated falsely is not correct, hence denied. It is submitted that 
documentary and oral evidence was produced before the enquiry officer and the copies of documents were supplied to 
him. Further, the Presenting Officer and the management witness deposed in the presence of the workman but he did 
not cross examine them. The enquiry officer after appreciating all the evidence adduced in the enquiry came to the 
conclusion that the charges leveled against the workman as proved in his enquiry report. Further, it is pertinent to 
mention here that the Petitioner has taken a contradictory and inconsistent plea that the charge sheeted workman has 
not instigated the strike by stating in the claim statement filed by the Petitioner in this Hon'ble Court that the 
concerned workman along with other Trammers went underground to carry out their duties and were not at all on 
strike whereas the Coal fillers were on strike, while the charge sheeted workman in his reply dated 30.06.2005 to the 
charge sheet and in his statement in the enquiry proceedings stated that he along with other Trammers did not go 
down the mine, which clearly shows that the statement is false. Therefore, the contention of the Petitioner that the 
enquiry officer acted like a management representative is denied. It is submitted that the charge sheeted workman 
was not able to rebut the charges leveled against him nor did he produce any evidence or witness in his defense in the 
enquiry. It is submitted that the Presenting Officer while deposing his evidence in the enquiry stated that under 
Public Utility Services the Strike was prohibited in the Company by notification G.O.Rt.No.52 dated 0.03.2005 by 
Government of Andhra Pradesh, which was displayed on the notice board in Telugu at the mine on 3.03.2005 and 
that due to the illegal strike production of 343 tonnes was lost amounting to Rs,3,39,570/-, but the workman did not 
cross examine. Further, Sri Panthangi Rajam the charge sheeted workman himself has deposed in the enquiry that all 
the trammers including himself returned their Cap Lamps to Lamp Room and gone home thereby it is clear that he has 
instigated and participated in the illegal strike. As such the contention of the Petitioner that the findings of the enquiry 
officer are wholly perverse in nature is far from the truth, hence denied. It is submitted that the Disciplinary 
Authority after going through the entire enquiry proceedings and enquiry report came to the conclusion that the 


charge leveled against the delinquent workman was proved and concurred with the findings of the Enquiry Officer 


554 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
holding him guilty of misconduct under Company Standing Orders No.25.lI, 25.6, and 25.24. Further, though in the 
ordinary course dismissal from service would have been the appropriate punishment on the basis of the grave and 
serious charges proved against the charge sheeted workman, the management has taken a lenient view with an 
intention to give opportunity to the delinquent workman to improve himself, and instead of awarding the extreme 
penalty has imposed on him the penalty of reversion to a lower stage by reducing four increments/SPRAs in the 
present scale of pay w.e.f. 0..2007 with cumulative effect’ Vide Office Order No.BHP/PER/20-D/4053.dated 


3.0.2007. Therefore, prayed to dismiss the claim petition. 
4. Perused written arguments of Respondent as well as claim of the Petitioner. 


5. On the basis of rival pleadings of both the parties, following issues arise for determination in the 


present case:- 


I Whether the departmental enquiry held against the Workman is legal and valid? 


I. Whether the action of General Manager SCCL, Bhupalpally Warangal district in awarding the punishment of 
reversion to a lower stage by reducing 4 increments/SPRAs in the present scale of pay with effect frame 


..2007 with cumulative effect in respect of Sri Pantangi Rajam, Trammer is justified or not? 
I. To what relief the Petitioner is entitled? 
Findings:- 
6. Issue _No.I: This issue has been decided vide order dated 2.4.2024 whereas the Petitioner Workman has 


conceded the legality and validity of the departmental enquiry. 


This issue is answered accordingly. 


7. Issue No. If:- Petitioner-Workman has challenged the imposition of punishment vide order dated 3!.0.2007 
passed by the Disciplinary Authority, General Manager, Bhupalpally, whereby Workman has been awarded penalty 
of reversion to a lower stage by reducing four increments/SPRAs in the present scale of pay, with cumulative effect as 
the Workman has been held guilty of committing misconduct under the Company’s Standing Order Nos. 25.], 
25.6, and 25.24 for participating in illegal strike in breach of the Mines Act and for sabotage or causing willful 
damage to work in progress or to the property of the Company. The Petitioner-Workman, has submitted that the 
charge alleged against him was that he instigated other workmen not to go to the distribution point, which resulted in 
the illegal strike on 27.06.2005. However, there is no evidence to support the alleged charge. Simply because the 
management witness stated that the concerned Workman did not stop the other workmen of 3 Seam from going on an 
illegal strike, it cannot be treated as misconduct. Therefore, the appreciation of the evidence on record by the Enquiry 
Officer is absolutely hopeless and perverse in nature. Instigating the workmen to go on strike is different from not 


stopping the workmen from going on an illegal strike. 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 555 
8. Further, the Petitioner submitted that the management witness never disclosed during the enquiry that he had 
given a complaint to the management on 27.06.2005. Even in the charge sheet, there was no indication of the alleged 
complaint, and no such documents were filed before the Enquiry Officer until the deposition of the Workman. 


Therefore, the Enquiry Officer erroneously relied upon the documents dated 27.06.2005 to hold the Workman guilty. 


9. Further, it is submitted that the Enquiry Officer, in his findings, referred to the loss of 340 tons of coal worth 
Rs. 3,39,520/- on 27.06.2005 in three shifts. However, there was no evidence to that effect during the enquiry. The 
Enquiry Officer erroneously arrived at his conclusions, and the findings are wholly perverse in nature. Moreover, 
the punishment imposed by the Disciplinary Authority—reversion to a lower stage by reducing four 
increments/SPRAs in the present scale of pay with effect from 0..2007, with cumulative effect—is without any 
basis. The impugned order passed by the Disciplinary Authority is cryptic and does not disclose any reasons, much 


less valid ones. Therefore, the impugned order is liable to be set aside on this ground alone. 


0. On the other hand, the Respondent Counsel contended that the Workman had booked his IN muster and did 
not go to the distribution point, which resulted in an illegal strike on 27.06.2005 that continued up to the end of the 
first shift on 29.06.2005. As such, a chargesheet was issued to him. Further, it is contended that Sri Govind Chandra 
Murmu, In-charge Overman of 3 Seam, had given a written complaint dated 27.06.2005 to the General Manager, KK- 
| Incline, stating that Sri Pantangi Rajam and another workman refused to come and took all the workmen away from 
the common distribution point and management witness has also deposed during the enquiry. Further, it is 
contended that the chargesheeted Workman did not cross-examine witness during the enquiry, thereby the nexus 
between the Workman and the illegal strike has been clearly established. It is further contended that the departmental 
enquiry against the Workman has been conducted in accordance with the principles of natural justice, and both 
documentary and oral evidence were produced before the Enquiry Officer. The management witness deposed in the 
presence of the Workman, but he did not cross-examine them. The Enquiry Officer, after appreciating all the 
evidence adduced in the enquiry, came to the conclusion that the charges levelled against the Workman were proved. 
It is further contended that in the instant case, the Enquiry Officer, following the principles of natural justice, gave 
every opportunity to the delinquent Workman to defend his case, and after analysing all the evidence adduced in the 
enquiry, came to the conclusion that the charges levelled against the delinquent Workman were proved. As such, the 
contention of the Petitioner that the Enquiry Officer acted in a pre-determined manner is denied, and the Petitioner is 
put to strict proof of the same. The charges were proved in the enquiry based on the evidence produced, and the 
chargesheeted Workman was not able to rebut the charges levelled against him, nor did he produce any evidence or 


witness in his defence during the enquiry. Further, it is contended that the Presenting Officer, while deposing during 


556 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
the enquiry, stated that under public utility services, the strike was prohibited in the company by Notification dated 
0.03.2005 issued by the Government of Andhra Pradesh, which was displayed on the notice board in Telugu at the 
Mine on 3.03.2005. It is further stated that due to the illegal strike, there was a production loss of 343 tons 
amounting to Rs. 3,39,570/-, but the Workman did not cross-examine the Presenting Officer. Further, the Workman 
himself deposed during the enquiry that all the trammers, including himself, returned their cap lamps to the lamp 
room and went home. Therefore, it is clear that he instigated and participated in the illegal strike. As such, the 
contention of the Petitioner that the findings of the Enquiry Officer are wholly perverse in nature is far from the truth 
and hence denied. It is further submitted that the management has taken a lenient view with an intention to give an 
opportunity to the delinquent Workman to prove himself, and instead of awarding the extreme penalty, has imposed 
the penalty of reversion to a lower stage by reducing 4 increments/SPRAs in the present scale of pay with effect from 


0..2007, with cumulative effect. 


il. In view of the oral submissions made by either party and on going through the record of the enquiry 
proceedings, it manifests that the chargesheeted Workman was issued with the chargesheet dated 27.06.2005 


containing the charges under the Company’s Standing Orders No. 25.I], 25.5, and 25.24, which read as hereunder: 


४ ॥. You are here by charged with the offence noted here under, 


on 27.6.2005 in I shift you booked your IN muster and did not go to distribution point for common 
distribution of १०.47 seam and No.3 seam at manway. Even though a notice board was displayed at manway well in 
advance for common distribution from I shift of 27.6.2005, you have demanded for distribution at No.] Seam and 
No.3 seam separately. You have instigated all the workmen not to go to distribution point which resulted in an 
illegal strike in which you have also participated. 


You are also aware that The Singareni Collieries Company Ltd., is declared as public utility service vide notification 
No.CRP PER IR E I5] 357 dated 05 07-03-2005 of Government of India which has been displayed on notice board. 
In public utility service, the strike without giving required notice is an illegal strike. Therefore, you have violated ID 
Act, 4947., Due to above strike incited and participated by you, due to which the company lost an output to a tune of 
343 tonnes worth Rs.3,39,570/-. 


2. Therefore, your above acts amounts to misconduct under Company’s Standing Order 25(//), 25(76) and 
25(24) which read as follows, 


C.S.O 25(I]):-Going on illegal strike either singly or with other workmen without giving (4 days previous notice 
C.S.O 25(76).- Any breach of the Mines Act, /9352 or any other Act of any rules or regulations or by laws thereunder 
C.S.O 25(24):-Sabotage or causing willful damage to work in progress or to property of the company.” 


2. As regard first charge against the CSE Workman it is alleged that on 27.06.2005, Petitioner booked his IN 
muster and but he did not go to the distribution point for common distribution of No.l Seam and No.3 Seam at the 
manway. Further, it is alleged that CSE workman along with other workmen demanded distribution at No.! Seam 


and No.3 Seam separately, and in support of his demand, he instigated all the workers not to go to the distribution 


[भाग व--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 557 
point, which resulted in an illegal strike in which he also participated. In support of the aforesaid charge 
management has examined witnesses Sri K. Srinivas, Under Manager, Presenting Officer, and Sri Govind Chandra 


Murmu, Overman, KK-! Incline. 


3. It is settled law that the Presenting Officer cannot be a competent witness during the enquiry in support the 
charge against the CSE. Therefore, the evidence of the Presenting Officer, Sri K. Srinivas cannot be read in support of 
charge against the CSE Workman. The other management witness, Sri Govind Chandra Murmu, Overman, who is 
stated to be present at the spot has deposed that, on 27.6.2005 in first shift he was incharge for 3 Seam and shift Under 
Manager instructed him that common distribution of both the seams | seam and 3 seam are to be conducted at | Seam 
manway. Further, witness states that as per instructions of shift Under Manager he has gone to 3 Seam pit head to 
inform all the workmen of 3 Seam to come for common distribution to be held at | Seam manway and the same was 
informed to the shift Under Manager Sri K Srinivas. Further, management witness deposed that Manager KTK] 
incline informed him to give two names of the workmen working at 3 Seam who are not willing to come for common 
distribution to be held at | Seam manway and as per the instruction of manager the witness has given the name of the 
CSE Sri P Rajam, Trammer and Sri M.Ramesh coal filler 8A gang. Thus, from the above statement of this witness 
it reflects that as per instruction of the Manager of KTK | incline, he has reported the name of the CSE Workman Sri 
Pantangi Rajam along with the other Workman Sri M.Ramesh participating in the alleged strike. This witness has 
nowhere stated that the CSE Workman was involve in instigating other Workman to conduct a strike for demanding 
distribution at Seam |. Further, witness states that all the workmen of 3 Seam did not come to the common 
distribution point at | Seam manway and went on illegal strike but the above two persons namely Sri Pantangi Rajam 
and Sri M. Ramesh did not stop other workmen for going on strike. But the witness did not state under what 
authority CSE Workman was duty bound to stop workmen from proceeding on strike. Further, management failed 
to examine any witness in order to prove the fact that the CSE was involved in instigating the Workmen to commit 
the strike on 27.6.2005. Merely, on the basis of statement of witness that CSE workman did not stop workmen to 


proceed on strike does not amount any misconduct under Company’s Standing Order No. 25.I]. 


4. Moreover, perusal of the record of enquiry proceedings goes to reveal that the alleged incident of the illegal 
strike occurred on 27.06.2005 in the first shift and, on the same day, the chargesheet dated 27.06.2005 was served 
upon the CSE. Whereas, as per record of enquiry, the strike commenced from 27.06.2005 in the first shift and 
continued upto 29.06.2005 in the first shift. The allegation of the management against the CSE in the chargesheet 
dated 27.6.2005 that due to said illegal strike CSE was solely responsible for the loss of output in the Company to 


the tune of 343 tons worth Rs. 3,39,570/- is a vague allegation without any basis and rationale. However, witness Sri 


558 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 
Govind Chandra Murmu has not stated in his statement anything regarding loss of Rs. 3,39,570/- to the company 
during the strike period attributed to the CSE Workman. Therefore, imputation loss occurred due to CSE Workman 
is a vague allegation without any justification. None the less, CSE Workman has been held guilty of the charge 
under Company’s Standing Order No. 25.24: for causing willful damage to the work in progress of the company to 
the tune of Rs. 3,39,570/-, without any basis or evidence. Thus, the finding of the Enquiry Officer in respect of 
charges framed against the CSE is without any reasoning or evidence on record no suffers from perversity. 
Moreover, management witness, Sri Govind Chandra Murmu, stated that he had given the names of two persons of 
Sri Pantangi Rajam and Sri M. Ramesh, Coal Filler as they were not willing to come to the common distribution 
point at No.! Seam on 27.06.2005, and these names has been given by him on the instruction of the Manager of KTK- 
l Incline. He did not state that he ever gave any complaint against the CSE Workman regarding his alleged 
participation in the strike. Therefore, in the absence of evidence of said complaint, the charge against the CSE 
Workman that he participated in alleged strike and caused loss to respondent company as alleged in chargesheet, is 
not found to be proved. Thus, the finding recorded by the Enquiry Officer that CSE Workman participated in the 
strike and instigated the other Workmen not to go to work and, he is also responsible for the loss of production of 343 
tons of coal worth Rs. 3,39,570/- in the respondent company, is not sustainable for want of relevant evidence. 
Therefore, the finding given by the Enquiry Officer in his enquiry report holding CSE Workman guilty of charges is 
illegal and perverse. Consequently, order of punishment passed by the Disciplinary Authority is not sustainable and 


liable to be set aside. 


5. Whereas, it is settled law that, to establish a Workman's participation in an illegal strike, evidence may 
consist of eyewitnesses, video or photographic proof, attendance records, or admission by the Workman himself. 
There can also be testimony from co-workmen or supervisors who observed the Workman participating in the strike. 
Further, absence records of workman can be used to show that the Workman participated in the illegal strike. The 
Respondent management failed to produce any of the above evidence in the enquiry to prove the charge against the 
CSE. However, record of the enquiry goes to show that on 27.6.2005 the underground Workmen were on strike and 
workmen trammers were not on strike on that strike. The CSE Workman during the enquiry, he has no where stated 
that he was a part of the illegal strike on 27.06.2005, rather, he stated that, as informed by the Munshi, the trammers 
were directed to go down the mine after all the fillers went underground. Thus, from the perusal of material on 
record of enquiry, it is clear that there is no evidence to prove the charge against CSE Workman that he participated 


in the illegal strike or instigated the Workmen to commit the strike in the Respondent’s premises. 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 559 
As regards jurisdiction of Tribunal to interfere in the finding of the Enquiry Officer, Hon'ble Supreme Court in the 


case of State of Rajasthan vs. Heem Singh, Civil Appeal No.3340 of 2020 dt.29.0.202 have held as hereunder:- 


“33. In exercising judicial review in disciplinary matters, there are two ends of the spectrum. The first embodies a 
rule of restraint. The second defines when interference is permissible. The rule of restraint constricts the ambit of 
judicial review. This is for a valid reason. The determination of whether a misconduct has been committed lies 
primarily within the domain of the disciplinary authority. The judge does not assume the mantle of the disciplinary 
authority. Nor does the judge wear the hat of an employer. Deference to a finding of fact by the disciplinary authority 
is a recognition of the idea that it is the employer who is responsible for the efficient conduct of their service. 
Disciplinary enquiries have to abide by the rules of natural justice. But they are not governed by strict rules of 
evidence which apply to judicial proceedings. The standard of proof is hence not the strict standard which governs a 
criminal trial, of proof beyond reasonable doubt, but a civil standard governed by a preponderance of probabilities. 
Within the rule of preponderance, there are varying approaches based on context and subject. The first end of the 
spectrum is founded on deference and autonomy — deference to the position of the disciplinary authority as a fact 
finding authority and autonomy of the employer in maintaining discipline and efficiency of the service. At the other 
end of the spectrum is the principle that the court has the jurisdiction to interfere when the findings in the enquiry are 
based on no evidence or when they suffer from perversity. A failure to consider vital evidence is an incident of what 
the law regards as a perverse 


PART J determination of fact. Proportionality is an entrenched feature of our jurisprudence. Service jurisprudence 
has recognized it for long years in allowing for the authority of the court to interfere when the finding or the penalty 
are disproportionate to the weight of the evidence or misconduct. Judicial craft lies in maintaining a steady sail 
between the banks of these two shores which have been termed as the two ends of the spectrum. Judges do not rest 
with a mere recitation of the hands-off mantra when they exercise judicial review. To determine whether the finding 
in a disciplinary enquiry is based on some evidence an initial or threshold level of scrutiny is undertaken. That is to 
satisfy the conscience of the court that there is some evidence to support the charge of misconduct and to guard 
against perversity. But this does not allow the court to re-appreciate evidentiary findings in a disciplinary enquiry or 
to substitute a view which appears to the judge to be more appropriate. To do so would offend the first principle 
which has been outlined above. The ultimate guide is the exercise of robust common sense without which the judges’ 
craft is in vain.” 


Further in the case of Roop Singh Negi vs. Punjab National Bank & Ors. On 9.2.2008, AIR 2008 SC92I, 


Hon'ble Supreme Court have held:- 


“॥2, In Moni Shankar v. Union of India and Anr. [(2008) 3 SCC 484], this Court held: 


7. The departmental proceeding is a quasi judicial one. Although the provisions of the Evidence Act are not 
applicable in the said proceeding, principles of natural justice are required to be complied with. The Court exercising 
power of judicial review are entitled to consider as to whether while inferring commission of misconduct on the part 
of a delinquent officer relevant piece of evidence has been taken into consideration and irrelevant facts have been 
excluded therefrom. Inference on facts must be based on evidence which meet the requirements of legal principles. 
The Tribunal was, thus, entitled to arrive at its own conclusion on the premise that the evidence adduced by the 
department, even if it is taken on its face value to be correct in its entirety, meet the requirements of burden of proof, 
namely - 


preponderance of probability. If on such evidences, the test of the doctrine of proportionality has not been satisfied, 
the Tribunal was within its domain to interfere. We must place on record that the doctrine of unreasonableness is 
giving way to the doctrine of proportionality." 


Thus, in view of the law laid down by the Hon'ble Supreme Court as discussed above, this court has got jurisdiction 


to interfere in the perverse finding of the Enquiry Officer as well as the Disciplinary Authority. 


Further, in the case of Workmen of Food Corporation of India vs. Union of India 997 II LLJ 462 Calcutta High 
Court Hon'ble High Court have held:- 


“II. In the Gujarat Steel Tubes Mazdoor Sabha (supra), the Supreme Court held that mere participation in a strike 
which has been declared illegal cannot by itself be said to be a misconduct to justify an order of dismissal. In this 


560 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


decision the Supreme Court quoted with approval its earlier decision in Indian General Navigation and Railway Co. 
Ltd. v. Their Workmen, (7969-7-7./.08 3) wherein it was held that even in case where the strike was declared illegal 
affecting a public utility service, the "only question of practical importance which may arise would be the kind or 
quantum of punishment and that, of course, has to be modulated in accordance with the facts and circumstances of 
each case ...... there may be reasons for distinguishing of the case of those who may have acted as mere dumb-driven 
cattle from those who have taken an active part in fomenting the trouble and instigating the workmen to join such a 
strike, or have taken recourse to violence." 


42. To determine the question of punishment a clear distinction has to be made between those workmen who not only 
joined in such a strike, but also took part in obstructing the loyal workmen from carrying on their work, or took part 
in violent demonstration, or acted in defiance of law and order, on the one hand and those workmen who are more or 
less silent participators in such a strike, on the other. It is not in the interest of industry that there should be a 
wholesale dismissal of all the workmen who merely participated in such a strike. It is certainly not in the interest of 
the workmen themselves. An Industrial Tribunal, therefore, has to consider the question of punishment keeping in 
view the overriding consideration of the full and efficient working of the industry as a whole. Section IIA of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 947 lays down that where an Industrial Dispute relating to discharge or dismissal has been 
referred to a Tribunal for adjudication and in the course of the adjudication proceeding the Tribunal is satisfied that 
the order of the discharge or dismissal was not justified, it may by its award set aside the order of the discharge or 
dismissal and direct reinstatement of the workman on certain terms, if any as it may think or may give such other 
relief to the workman including awarding a lesser punishment in lieu of discharge or dismissal as the circumstances 
may require.” 


6. Therefore, in view of fore gone discussion and law laid down by Hon'ble Court as discussed above, the 
finding of the Enquiry Officer that the CSE has committed misconduct of participating in illegal strike or instigated 
the other Workmen to commit the strike is illegal and perverse for want of relevant evidence and same is not 
sustainable. Further, the findings of the Enquiry Officer regarding the loss of output to the tune of 343 tonns due to 
the alleged participation of the CSE Workman in the strike is also without any basis and rationale, and the same is 
not sustainable in the eye of law. Therefore, I am of the view that the punishment order passed by the Disciplinary 
Authority in respect of the Petitioner Workman for imposition of reversion by 4 increments/SPRAs with cumulative 
effect to him is liable to be set aside. Hence, the action of the Respondent management in awarding punishment of 
reversion to a lower stage by reducing four increments/SPRAs in the present scale of pay w.e.f. l.!.2007 is not 
justified. 


This point is answered in favour of the Workman and against the Respondent. 


7. Issue No.II: In view of the fore gone discussion and finding arrived at Issue No.I, this Court is of considered 
view that the finding of the enquiry officer holding the Workman guilty of the charges under Company’s Standing 
Order No. 25. l, 25.6, 25.24 is illegal and not justifiable. Therefore, the finding of the Enquiry Officer holding 
the petitioner as guilty of the aforesaid charges is liable to be set aside and consequently the order dated |..2007 
passed by Disciplinary Authority passed against the petitioner Sri Pantangi Rajam, Trammer of imposition of 
punishment of reversion to a lower stage by reducing four increments/ SPRAS in the present scale of pay with 
cumulative effect is liable to be set aside. 


This Issue is decided in favour of the Workman and against the Respondent. 
AWARD 


In the result, the claim petition is allowed. The punishment order No. BHP/PER/20-d/4053 dated 3.0.2007 
passed by General Manager, M/s. Singareni Collieries Co. Ltd., whereby punishment of reversion to a lower stage by 
reducing four increments/ SPRAS in the present scale of pay with cumulative effect has been imposed upon 
Petitioner Sri Panthangi Rajam, Trammer w.e.f. .!.2007 is hereby set aside. The Respondent management is 
directed to restore the increments of Sri Panthangi Rajam, Trammer, from the date of the order of punishment, 
i.e.,l..2007, within one month from the receipt of this award. The Reference is answered accordingly. 


Award passed accordingly. Transmit.

562 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


(JUDGMENT) 
(Passed on this 5"" day of May - 2025) 


The applicant workman has filed this petition under Section 2-A (2&3) of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
947, hereinafter referred by the word ‘Act’, with a case that he was engaged by the Branch Manager of Khachrod 
Branch Ujjain in July, 998 for different intervals as a Daily Wager for cleaning of the Branch and was engaged on 
5.07.2006 by the Branch Manager of the Branch on the post of Office Peon and cleaning under his orders. He 
worked eight hours a day continuously till the day of termination on 22.03.206 by the Management Bank. He further 
alleged that the Bank started recruitment process for the post of cleaner/peon. He also submitted an application being 
a Daily Wager in the process. He was called for interview by Management Bank, vide its letter dated 3!.0.20l4 
which was scheduled on 4..204. He did appear in the interview, but was not selected. 


According to the Applicant Workman, he was paid any compensation or notice by those in his 
disengagement is in violation of Section 25F and 25G of the Act. The workman has thus prayed that, holding his 
disengagement against law, he be held entitled to be reinstated with back wages and benefits. 


In its written reply to the petition, the Management has taken a case that he was not a regularly appointed 
employee following recruitment process rather he was a Daily Wager who was called for work on as and when 
required basis. He never worked continuously for 240 days in any year. Being a daily wager who had worked with the 
Bank for few days, he was called for interview for the post of Peon cum Cleaner in the recruitment process held in 
203-4, but could not be selected. Thereafter, he was never engaged by Management Bank. Management has prayed 
the dismissal of his petition. 


In evidence, the Workman has filed Photocopy documents which he has proved as Exhibit W-l to W-22. 
These documents will be referred to as and when required. 


The Workman has further filed his affidavit as his examination-in-chief; he has been cross-examined by 
the Management. 


The Management has filed affidavit of its witness Vijay Kumar Maheswari as his examination-in-chief. He 
was not produced for cross-examination. Management filed affidavit of another witness Prakash Laxman Kewal 
Ramani, as his examination-in-chief. He was cross-examined by Workman. Management has filed and proved 
documents relating to recruitment in 203.4, to be referred to as and when require. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for Applicant Workman Shri Arun Patel and Learned Counsel for 
Management Bank Shri Neeraj Kewat. I have gone through the record as well. 


From the perusal of record in the light of rival arguments it comes out that on the basis of being engaged 
by Management Bank as a daily wager he was given an opportunity in the recruitment process held in 203-4, and 
he did appeared in that process, but cannot be selected. There is no dispute between the parties with regard to this 
factual position. According to the Applicant Workman, the recruitment process was vitiated as it was mala fide or 
there where irregularities but this process is not challenged before this Tribunal, hence it is not a subject matter of 
dispute. 


Case of the Workman is that, he continued to work even after he was not selected in the recruitment 
process, and he worked till 22.03.20l6 when his services were terminated by Management without notice or 
compensation as alleged by the Applicant Workman. Management has denied this allegation; hence the burden to 
proof his continuous appointment from date of closer of recruitment process till date of his alleged disengagement is 
on Workman. 


Section 25B of the Act requires to be reproduced here and is being reproduced as follows:- 


25B. Definition of continuous service — 


(7) a workman shall be said to be in continuous service for a period if he is, for that period, in 
uninterrupted service, including service which may be interrupted on account of sickness or 
authorised leave or an accident or a strike which is not illegal, or a lock-out or a cessation of 
work which is not due to any fault on the part of the workman; 


(2) where a workman is not in continuous service within the meaning of clause () for a period 
of one year or six months, he shall be deemed to be in continuous service under an 
employer— 


(a) for a period of one year, if the workman, during a period of twelve calendar months 
preceding the date with reference to which calculation is to be made, has actually 
worked under the employer for not less than — 


(i) one hundred and ninety days in the case of a workman employed below ground 
in a mine; and 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 563 


(ii) two hundred and forty days, in any other case; 


(b) for a period of six months, if the workman, during a period of six calendar months 
preceding the date with reference to which calculation is to be made, has actually 
worked under the employer for not less than— 


(i) ninety-five days, in the case of a workman employed below ground in a mine; 
and 
(ii) one hundred and twenty days, in any other case. 


The Photocopy documents filed and proved by the Workman relate to his engagement are only up to the 
period of 204, there is no document filed or proved by the Applicant Workman which indicates that he continued to 
Work till 23.03.206 as alleged by him. Hence, only his statement on oath in support of his this allegation will not be 
sufficient because the Management has also rebutted these allegations in the statement on oath of its witness. Hence, 
in the light of above discussion, the Workman is held to have failed in proving his continuous appointment for 240 
days, in the year preceding the date of his disengagement. 


In the light of above findings, the disengagement of the Workman cannot be held to be unjust or illegal or 
in violation of Section 25F or 25G of the Act. 


On the basis of above discussion and findings, the petition is liable to be dismissed. 


ORDER 
Petition dismissed. 
No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 5/05/2025 
P. K.SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer

Government hereby publishes the Award (Ref. 06/208) of the Cent.Govt.Indus.Tribunal-cum-Labour Court

[No. L-20]/23/208- IR(B-ID]

THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL CUM LABOUR COURT, JABALPUR 
NO. CGIT/LC/R/06/208 
Present: P.K.Srivastava 
H.J.S..(Retd) 
The General Secretary, 
Dainik Vetan Bhogi Bank Karmchari Sangathan, 
F-I, Tripti Vihar, Opp. Engg. College, 
Ujjain (M.P.) 


Workman 


564 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


Vs 
The Zonal Manager, 
Bank of India, 
Kaitha Ujjain (MP) 
Management 
(JUDGMENT) 


(Passed on this 9" day of May- 2025) 


As per letter dated 30//208 by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New Delhi, the reference is 
made to this Tribunal under Section-l0 of Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (in short the ‘Act’)as per Notification No. L- 
20/23/208 (IR(B-ID) dt. 30/ /208. The dispute under reference relates to: 


“Whether the allegations of the Union that the action of the Management of Bank of India by (i) not 
giving appointment letter/termination letter (ii) payment was not made as per pay scale/skill wage (iii) 
Minimum wages not paid (iv) Muster Roll has not been maintained as per Section 25 D of the ID Act (v) 
Applicants have worked 240 days in a year (vi) After working for 06 days, the wages of weekly off and 
National Holidays, 26th January, 75॥#7 August were deducted, etc. amounts to alleged Unfair Labour 
Practice? If so, what relief they are entitled to?” 


Case of the Workmen as taken by them in the statement of claim filed by them is that, the Management 
had appointed Workman Jagdish Parihar in the Kathya Branch of the Bank of India, Indore from 23..983 to 990 
and from 23..994 to 04.09.2009 and has practiced unfair labour practice in his case by way of violating the 
provisions of Desai Award of 966, Minimum Wages Act, 948, Payment of Wages Act, 938, Bonus Act, 965, and 
Bonded Labour Abolition Act, 976. The Workman Union has sought the relief of criminal prosecution as well 
benefits to the Workman Jagdish Chauhan as mentioned above. 


Case of the Workman is mainly that the dispute is not maintainable as it has been heard and decided between 
the parties in the earlier references details mentioned as follows: 


l. Case R/54/20]] vide notification dated 3.06.20l], 
Schedule - 


“Whether Shri Jagdish Parihar is a Workman within the provisions of Industrial Disputes Act? 
Whether his services were terminated without following provisions of law in illegal manner? What relief 
he is entitled to?” 


2. Case R/I2/202 vide notification dated 4.02.20]], 
Schedule - 


“Whether the Applicant is a Workman, within the provisions of Industrial Dispute Act? Whether, his 
services were terminated in violation of Section 33 of ILD. Act, /94772 If yes, when what relief he is 
entitled for?” 


“Whether the Applicant is entitled for regularization in services of non-applicant, if yes, then the 
benefits if any he is entitled for?” 


3. Case R/00/202, vide notification dated 8.09.202, 
Schedule — 


“Whether demand of Union for payment of bonus for the period /995-96 to 2008-09 with respect to 
Shri Jagdish is just and proper? If yes, what relief the Workman is entitled to?” 


It is further the case of the Management that, case No. R/54/20l] which was against termination of 
services of the Workman Jagdish Chauhan raised on similar grounds in the said dispute has been vide Award dated 
0.07.207. Case R/I2/20ll with the allegation of violation of Section 33A of the Act, has also been decided. 
Management has further pleaded that, the Workman Jagdish Parihar was a daily wager, he was engaged on as and 
when require basis, for which he was paid his wages. He was neither of permanent nor temporary staff appointed 
following recruitment procedure, hence, he is not entitled to equal pay or benefits admissible to regular staff. 


Both the sides have filed affidavits as their examination-in-chief. They have been cross-examined by their 
opposite party. 


I have heard argument of Learned Counsel for Workman Union Shri Arun Patel, and Shri Neeraj Kewat 
for Management. I have gone through the record as well. 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 565 


The reference itself is the issue for determination. 


There is Award of R/54/20] in which the termination of the Workman was counter dispute. This Tribunal 
recorded a finding that the Workman could not successful prove his continuous engagement with the Bank for 240 
days in any year, hence his services were not terminated in violation of the Act. 


The Case regarding payment of Bonus is subject matter of different case which is R/l00/202. 
Section 47 of the Civil Procedure Code is being reproduced as follows: 


Il. Res judicata.—No Court shall try any suit or issue in which the matter directly and substantially in 
issue has been directly and substantially in issue in a former suit between the same parties, or between parties 
under whom they or any of them claim, litigating under the same title, in a Court competent to try such subsequent 
suit or the suit in which such issue has been subsequently raised, and has been heard and finally decided by such 
Court. 


Explanation I.—The expression “former suit” shall denote a suit which has been decided prior to a suit in 
question whether or not it was instituted prior thereto. 


Explanation II.—For the purposes of this section, the competence of a Court shall be determined irrespective of 
any provisions as to a right of appeal from the decision of such Court. 


Explanation IlI.—The matter above referred to must in the former suit have been alleged by one party and either 
denied or admitted, expressly or impliedly, by the other. 


Explanation IV.—Any matter which might and ought to have been made ground of defence or attack in such 
former suit shall be deemed to have been a matter directly and substantially in issue in such suit. 


Explanation V.—Any relief claimed in the plaint, which is not expressly granted by the decree, shall for the 
purposes of this section, be deemed to have been refused. 


Explanation VI.—Where persons litigate bona fide in respect of a public right or of a private right claimed in 
common for themselves and others, all persons interested in such right shall, for the purposes of this section, be 
deemed to claim under the persons so litigating . 


Explanation VII.—The provisions of this section shall apply to a proceeding for the execution of a decree and 
references in this section to any suit, issue or former suit shall be construed as references, respectively, to a 
proceeding for the execution of the decree, question arising in such proceeding and a former proceeding for the 
execution of that decree. 


Explanation VIII. —An issue heard and finally decided by a Court of limited jurisdiction, competent to decide 
such issue, shall operate as res judicata in a subsequent suit, notwithstanding that such Court of limited 
jurisdiction was not competent to try such subsequent suit or the suit in which such issue has been subsequently 
raised. 


Since, the disputes like non-issuing of appointment/termination letter or non preparation of Muster Roll, 
were ancillary, issue connected with the dispute of termination which ought to have been raised in the previous case 
mentioned above. The Workman Union cannot be permitted to raise because it is barred by constructive res judicata. 
The payment of minimum wages is not supported by any evidence from the side of Workman Union, hence, this 
allegation is also held not proved. 


In the light of above discussion and findings, the reference stands answered accordingly. 
No order as to cost. 
DATE:- 9/05/2025 
P. K.SRIVASTAVA, Presiding Officer
