AWARD 


Sri B. Venkati, Ex. Sub-staff who was an employee of 4" Respondent has filed this Petition invoking Sec.2 
A (2) of the LD. Act, 947, seeking for declaring the order dated 26.6.2009 “compulsory retirement with 
superannuation benefits and consequential order passed by the Appellate Authority dated .3.200/2.3.20l0 and 
reviewing authority order dated l0.6.20l0 issued by the Respondent Bank as _ illegal, arbitrary and to set aside the 
same and to grant consequential relief of reinstatement to the Petitioner into service duly granting of other 


[भाग व--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 567 


consequential benefits such as, continuity of service, back wages, along with all other attendant benefits. 
Respondents are contesting this petition by filing their counter. 


2. As can be seen from the contentions of the Petitioner the claim of the Petitioner is briefly as follows: 


It is submitted that the Petitioner joined in service on .6.984 as a Sub-Staff at Jyothinagar Branch of Respondent 
bank, Ramagundam, Karimnagar district. Subsequently, his services were confirmed in the said post. While so, on 
0.6.2006 the Petitioner borrowed an amount of Rs.!0,000/- from one Sri Puli Rajaiah who is an employee of M/s. 
Singareni Collieries Company Ltd.,, Godavarikhani, and could not repay the same in time. Forcibly, the Petitioner 
gave a cheque for Rs.l2,000/- to Sri Puli Rajaiah without mentioning the date. Subsequently, in the month of 
November, 2006, the Petitioner refunded Rs.l0000/- to Sri Puli Rajaiah, but Sri Puli Rajaiah demanded Rs.2,000/- 
towards interest. The Petitioner informed to Sri Puli Rajaiah that he will pay the interest amount afterwards and 
collect his cheque. But Sri Puli Rajaiah has presented the cheque on .7.2007 to his account at S.B.H., Singareni 
Collieries branch, Godavarikhani. On the date of presentation of cheque, the Petitioner has no sufficient funds in the 
account. It is submitted that as the Branch Manager was on leave on | .7.2007, the Petitioner informed and requested 
the Assistant Manager regarding the cheque, to settle the matter with Sri Puli Rajaiah by paying the interest amount 
of Rs.2000/- and he will collect the bounced cheque issued by him to Sri Puli Rajaiah. The Assistant Manager 
assured the Petitioner that the matter will be placed before the concerned authorities and it will be settled amicably 
without any complications. While the Petitioner was working as a Sub-staff at Jyothinager branch of the Respondent, 
he submitted a quotation for getting two wheeler vehicle loan (for purchasing two wheeler vehicle [Hero Honda]) on 
0.8.2006 for Rs.48,420/- and he got sanction for a sum of Rs.4,l50/- vide cheque dated 5.9.2006. While so, the 
Respondent bank has issued suspension order dt. 24.7.2007/6.8.2007 to the Petitioner suspending him from service 
alleging that a cheque dated .7.2007 for a sum of ६.। 2000/- issued by the Petitioner on his clean OD Account in 
favour of Sri Puli Rajaiah and it was returned by the branch due to insufficient funds. The Petitioner has submitted 
a detailed explanation on |2.9.2007 denying the charges and further requested to revoke the suspension order. After 
taking permission from the Branch Manager to settle the matter amicably, the Petitioner met Sri Puli Rajaiah, paid the 
interest amount of Rs.2000/- and got the bounced cheque. There is no malafide in intention on the part of the 
Petitioner to give a cheque to Sri Puli Rajaiah without sufficient funds. The Petitioner approached Shreya 
Consumer Welefare Association, Godavrikhani on !7.5.2007 and gave complaint against M/s Raja Rajeswara Motors, 
Hero Honda Show Room, NRPC, Krishna Nagar, Jyothi Nagar, Karimnagar District seeking relief form the 
Respondent therein and arrange to pay the amount of Rs.97,I50/- or deliver the vehicle by exempting the cheque 
amount at the earliest date. The said association, after hearing the parties directed the Respondents therein to pay a 
sum of Rs.97,50/- jointly and severally to the complainant therein i.e., the Petitioner herein within a period of [5 
days from the date of receipt of the order and a reminder was also issued by the association in this regard. It is further 
submitted that, while so, the Respondents bank have issued charge sheet dated 29.7.2008 alleging that the Petitioner 
has committed gross misconduct within the meaning of para 2(j) of the bipartite settlement as under: 


“S(j) :“doing an act prejudicial to the interest of the bank or gross negligence or negligence involving or likely to 
involve the bank in serious loss.” 


The Petitioner submitted his explanation to the charge sheet. But, without considering the explanation submitted by 
the Petitioner, an enquiry was ordered. The Petitioner participated in the enquiry and requested to drop all further 
proceedings in the matter. The deposition submitted by the Respondent’s witness are false and incorrect. No 
evidence was adduced before the Enquiry Officer to show that the Petitioner has committed irregularities as alleged in 
the charge sheet. During the pendency of the enquiry proceeding, Vemula Kanaka Raju, customer of the bank has 
submitted his affidavit before the Respondents’ bank, stating that he is the agent of M/s. Raja Rajeswar Motors, 
Jyothinagar branch, Ramgundam, he has been entrusted with the invoice of purchase of the vehicle of the Petitioner, 
he met the Petitioner, he obtained the advanced stamp receipt on plain paper on 20.9.2006 duly signed by the 
Petitioner authorizing him to receive the vehicle on 2.9.2006. He further stated in his affidavit that he kept silent 
over that receipt and on suggestion made by the friends of the Petitioner to create another problem to the Petitioner, 
they took out the revenue stamp receipts and prepared a promissory note worth Rs.50,000/- which is a false one and 
vague, and the above statement is given with his free will and without coercion and also addressed a letter dt. 
9.2.2008 to the Enquiry Officer. The Raja Rajeswar Motors, addressed a letter to the Shreya Consumer Welfare 
Association, stating that they have received cheque through Sri Vemula Kanaka Raju and they delivered the vehicle to 
Sri Vemula Kanaka Raju instead of the Petitioner, and further stated that they have rectified the mistake and settled 
the issue on 20.2.2009 amicably and requested to drop all further proceedings in the matter. The Petitioner lodged a 
complaint against the show room owner before the Police regarding purchase of vehicle and also submitted 
representation to the Police after getting his grievance solved. Without considering the facts and circumstances of 
the case the Enquiry Officer has submitted his report holding the charges levelled against the Petitioner stands proved. 
No evidence was produced before the Enquiry Officer showing that the Petitioner has committed irregularities as 
alleged in the charge sheet. Hence, the findings of the Enquiry Officer are perverse. It is submitted that Petitioner has 
also lodged a complaint before the Circle Inspector of Police, Karimnagar against the Hero Honda Showroom, 
Karimnagar, stating that said show room has not delivered the vehicle within stipulated time, inspite of receiving the 
cheque for a sum of Rs.4I,50/- and requested the police officials to take necessary action against the proprietor of 


568 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


the said showroom. After receipt of the same, Petitioner submitted detailed explanation denying the charges leveled 
against him in the show cause notice. Instead of considering the explanation submitted by the Petitioner to the show 
cause notice, the Respondents bank have passed punishment order Dt. 26/06/2009 imposing major penalty 
punishment of compulsory retirement from service with superannuation benefits as would be due otherwise and 
without disqualification from future employment and the same was received by the Petitioner on 30-6-2009. The 
punishment imposed on the Petitioner is disproportionate to the gravity of the charges. Hence, the action on part of 
the Respondents in imposing the major penalty Compulsorily retirement from service is illegal, arbitrary and unjust. 
It is submitted that aggrieved by the punishment orders Petitioner preferred an Appeal dated 3-7-2009 and _ the 
Appellate Authority has not passed any orders in the Appeal submitted by the Petitioner. Again Petitioner has 
submitted reminder on 7-2-200 reiterating the facts and the Appellate Authority passed an order dated !2-3-200 
mechanically stating that the Appellate Authority already rejected the appeal vide order dated l!-3-20l0. The action 
on part of the appellate authority in not considering submissions made by the Petitioner for reconsideration of his case 
for reinstatement into service is illegal, arbitrary and unjust. Again on !/03/20l0 the Zonal Manager & review 
authority passed an order confirming the punishment order Dt. 26/06/2009. Thereafter Petitioner preferred a mercy 
application before the Zonal Manager & Appellate Authority explaining the facts and circumstances of the case and 
requested with the Zonal Manager & Appellate Authority to reconsider his case and drop all further proceedings in 
the matter, but in vain. It is submitted further that Petitioner approached the National Commission for Scheduled 
Castes with a request to intervene in the matter, after receipt of Petitioner's representation the Director National 
Commission for Scheduled Castes addressed a letter to the General Manager Bank of India, Hyderabad. After receipt 
of said letter the Respondents bank have furnished all the details pertaining to the Petitioner case. On !6/!/200 the 
General Manager (HR) addressed a letter to the Director National Commission for Scheduled Castes they are not in a 
position to reconsider Petitioner's case. It is submitted that again Petitioner has submitted another representation to 
the Director National Commission for Scheduled Castes with same request to intervene in the matter for his 
reinstatement into service, after receipt of his representation the Director National Commission for Scheduled Castes 
addressed a letter to the Chairman & Managing Director, Bank of India, Mumbai enclosing all relevant documents 
pertaining to the Petitioner's case, and requested to consider the matter. It is submitted that the Disciplinary Authority 
and Enquiry Officer failed to appreciate the fact that the Petitioner never involved in any such kind of activities. It is 
submitted that since from the date of punishment order, Petitioner facing severe financial difficulties, Petitioner alone 
earning member in his family. Petitioner entire family is depending on his salary only. Unless, this Hon'ble Court set 
aside the punishment orders dated. 26.6.2009 and consequential orders passed by the appellate authority Dt. l-03- 
200/2 03-20i0 and reviewing authority Dt. l0-06-200, direct the Respondents to reinstate the Petitioner into 
service with all consequential benefits, otherwise he will be put to serious and irreparable loss. It is submitted that 
Petitioner made an application on 29-03-20i2 before the Assistant Commissioner of Labour (Central). Vidyanagar, 
Hyderabad to intervene in the matter and settle the dispute by reinstating him into service and it ended in failure. 
Hence, prayed to set aside the impugned order dt.26.6.2009 directing the Respondents to reinstate the Petitioner into 
service with all consequential benefits. 


3.___— The Respondents filed counter statement as follows: 


The Respondents filed counter denying the averments of the claim petition, that it was brought to the notice of the 
management that the applicant had committed an act of mis-utilization of staff vehicle loan to cause wrongful 
pecuniary gain to himself and issued cheque to the bank without keeping sufficient funds, including pilferage of 
bank’s documents. He was placed under suspension on 6.8.2007 by Competent authority. A Departmental enquiry 
has been initiated against the Petitioner as per Bi-partite settlement applicable to sub-staff of the bank. A charge sheet 
was issued to the Petitioner on 29.7.2008. He submitted his explanation, wherein he denied charge No.l and 
admitted charge No.2. Hence, Departmental enquiry was held from 4.8.2008 to .9.2008. The Departmental 
witnesses were examined and the Petitioner was allowed to be represented by Defence Representative who cross 
examined the management witnesses. After completion of examination of witnesses and marking of documents as 
exhibits, the Presenting Officer and the Defence representative submitted their written briefs, the Enquiry Officer 
submitted his report holding the charges proved. The Petitioner acknowledged the receipt of the enquiry report. As 
such, the Departmental enquiry has been conducted following the principles of natural justice. The applicant was 
given full opportunity in disciplinary enquiry and he cross-examined the witnesses. The applicant was given 
opportunity of personal hearing by Disciplinary Authority before imposing punishment. The Respondents followed 
the procedure prescribed and the applicant was not denied opportunity to present his case. Hence the applicant cannot 
have any grievance regarding the procedure. The punishment imposed is commensurate with the gravity of the 
charges and there is no ground for interference by this Hon'ble Tribunal. Hence it is prayed that the claim of the 
applicant be rejected and LC be dismissed with costs. 


4. On the basis of the rival pleadings of either party following issues emerge for determination:- 
I. Whether the departmental enquiry conducted against the Workman is legal and valid? 
Il. Whether the action of the Respondent management in imposing the punishment of compulsorily retirement 


from service with superannuation benefits as would be due otherwise and without disqualification from 
future employment under class 6(c) of the memorandum of settlement dated 0" April 2002, to the Petitioner 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 569 


Sri B. Venkati is legal and justified? 
I. To what relief if any the Petitioner is entitled? 
Findings:- 


5. Issue No.I:- This issue pertains to the legality and validity of the departmental enquiry. This issue has been 
decided against the workman vide order dated 4.9.207 and departmental enquiry has been held legal and valid. 


Thus, Issue No.I is answered accordingly. 


6. Issue_No.II: - Now let us examine whether the action of Respondent in imposition of punishment of 
compulsory retirement of Petitioner from service with superannuation benefits as would be due otherwise and without 
disqualification from future employment under clause 6(c) of the memorandum of settlement dated 0.4.2002 is 
legal and justified. In this context, the Learned Counsel for Respondent submitted that the Petitioner has been found 
guilty of the charges of encash of loan amount granted to him for a specific purpose i.e., purchasing the vehicle but 
instead of paying to dealer, he encashed the loan amount for himself. Further, it is contended that the Petitioner has 
also been found guilty of the charge of pilfering official documents and issuing cheque towards repayment of loan 
which was bounced. Further, Respondent submitted that a memorandum containing the charges was issued to the 
Workman and enquiry was conducted against the charge sheeted employee and witnesses were examined by the 
management. Further, it is contended that applicant was allowed to be represented by defence representative, also 
has cross examined the witness, Presenting Officer and Defence representative submitted written brief and thereafter 
Enquiry Officer on appreciating the evidence submitted his report dated l3.4.2009 holding the charges proved. 
Further, it is contended that the Petitioner was given fair opportunity of hearing in domestic enquiry and enquiry 
report was also served to the charge sheeted employee. The Disciplinary Authority after examining the evidence 
on record and proofs submitted by the CSE, issued show cause notice to charge sheeted employee, which he 
submitted reply. CSE through his defence representative was given personal hearing on 23.6.2009 and Disciplinary 
Authority passed the order of punishment of compulsory retirement of CSE with superannuation benefits and without 
disqualification from future employment giving cogent reasons for passing such an order. T Respondent submitted 
that order of punishment awarded to CSE is commensurate with the gravity of the charges, and not liable to 
interference. 


7. Perused the record. Undisputedly, Petitioner Workman was in the service of Respondent bank as sub-staff 
and he has applied for loan to purchase two Wheeler vehicle and Respondent bank sanctioned the said loan to the 
Petitioner for a sum of Rs.4I,50/- vide cheque No.57966 dated 5.9.2006 drawn on Bank of India. But the 
evidence recorded during the enquiry reveals that Petitioner did not utilise the said vehicle loan amount for 
purchasing the vehicle and he withdrawn the said loan amount with collusion of the dealer of the vehicle and thereby 
committed misconduct under class 5(j) of the bi-partite settlement dated 0.4.2002. The record of enquiry 
proceeding goes to reveal that the Petitioner was served with chargesheet dated 29.7.2008 containing two heads of 
the charges reproduced as hereunder: - 


“CHARGE-I:- 


As per your application dated 37.96,.2096, interest free Staff Vehicle Loan of Rs. 47, 9/35/- was sanctioned to you on 
43.99.2006 for purchasing a Hero Honda Motorcycle. A quotation dated 79.96.2006 of M/s. Raja Rajeshwari Dealers 
was submitted by you. The loan was disbursed by issuing Pay Order bearing No. 377966 for Rs. 47,650/-क6 
43.99.2006 in favour of above dealer for delivering the Hero Honda Motorcycle and registering Bank's lien thereon. 
You were required to submit registration and invoice to the Bank as required under the extant rules of the Bank. 
However, you failed to do so despite several reminders. When the matter was taken up with the dealer M/s. Raja 
Rajeshwari Dealers he informed that you have encashed the Pay Order from their subdealer without taking delivery 
of the Hero-Honda Motorcycle. In the aforesaid manner, you have misutilised Bank's interest free Staff Vehicle Loan 
facility in collusion with the aforesaid dealer to derive undue pecuniary advantage to yourself. 


CHARGE II:- 


You have issued a cheque for Rs. 72,0006 dated 7/.07.2007 from your Clean Overdraft A/c favouring Shri Puli 
Rajaiah. Due to insufficient funds, the said cheque was returned unpaid. The Branch Manager, therefore, reported 
the matter to the Zonal Office about your issuing of cheques without maintaining sufficient balance. The said 
communication i.e., IOM dated /4.07.2090/, was handed over by Despatch Section of Jyothinagar Branch to a courier 
agency viz. M/s. ANL Parcel Service along with other mail meant for Zonal Office. When your request for not 
reporting the matter to Zonal Office was not acceded to by the Branch Manager you approached the said courier 
agency with ulterior motives, by representing to them that one letter was not meant to be sent/dispatched to Zonal 
Office and you opened the said closed cover and delivered it back to the courier agency after removing the said IOM 
dated 4.07.2097 about reporting of your cheque returned to Zonal Office. Thereafter, you have pilfered official 
documents of the Branch and retained those documents with you unauthorisedly and in the process halted the 
Branch's attempt to report the matter to Zonal Office of your acts of issuing cheques without maintaining sufficient 
balance.” 


570 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


The charge No.II has been admitted by the CSE hence required no further discussion. 


8. Further, record of enquiry proceeding goes to reveal thatduring the enquiry Respondent management has 
examined the witness and produced the documents exhibits MEx.] to MEx. 29. Thereafter, on conclusion of the 
enquiry, Enquiry Officer on going the evidence recorded during the enquiry, submitted his written report to the 
Disciplinary Authority holding the charges proved against CSE. The Disciplinary Authority issued show cause notice 
to the charge sheeted employee and extended personal hearing opportunity to the CSE and after hearing passed 
punishment order imposing the punishment of compulsory retirement of Petitioner from service with superannuation 
benefits and without disqualification for future employment. 


9. Therefore, it manifest from record that charge sheeted employee was given fair opportunity of hearing at 
every stage of the proceeding which he has availed and he never raised any objection complaining any prejudice 
caused to him before the Enquiry Officer. Further, he received all the documents filed and relied upon by the 
Respondent in support of the chargesheet and he also filed reply, cross examined the management witnesses. 
Further, the Enquiry Officer on appreciating the evidence, on record submitted his reasoned report holding the 
charges proved against CSE. Thus, in view of the above, no case is made out to hold that departmental enquiry us 
suffering from any procedural lapses or was conducted in violation of principles of natural justice. Since both the 
charges were held proved in domestic enquiry against the Petitioner and one cannot possibly hold that charges were 
simple in nature, in other words that both the charges were of serious nature and no employer would ever allow or 
tolerate of such offence of an employee while on duty. 


0. Petitioner vehemently argued that after sanction of vehicle loan the bank gave him pay order drawn on M/s. 
Rajarajeshwara Motors, Petitioner went to dealer M/s. Rajarajeshwara Motors and handed over the pay order. As 
Petitioner was busy in his work in the bank, he requested one customer to follow up for getting specific coloured 
vehicle with the said dealer, then the dealer informed the Petitioner that specified colour vehicle was not available 
and they would inform after receipt of stock of desired colour. Petitioner has stated in his explanation that after 
sometime Petitioner enquired from dealer M/s. Raja Rajeswara Motors, then he came to know that the bank 
customer has encashed the pay order. Petitioner approached Shreya Consumers Welfare Association Godavarikhani 
and lodged a complaint against M/s. Raja Rajeswara Motors for mischief played by the Rajarajeshwara Motors. 
Consumer Welfare Association issued notices to the Respondent dealer and after hearing the parties directed the 
Respondent dealer vide order dated 7.5.2008 to pay a sum of Rs.97,!50/- jointly and severally to the complainant. 
Inspite of receiving the said order M/s. Rajarajeshwara Motors Hero Honda showroom, NRPC, Krishna Nagar, Jyoti 
Nagar, Karimnagar district did not take any step for payment of the said amount, Respondent bank ordered an 
enquiry into the alleged incident. In the enquiry Petitioner submitted reiterated version, that has been stated in his 
explanation to the charge and requested the Enquiry Officer and Disciplinary Authority to drop all further 
proceedings in the matter. The record of enquiry proceeding reveals that Presenting Officer Mr UV Ramana 
presented the case on 30.8.2008 and examined Sri T Venkateswarlu, Manager, LB Nagar branch and marked 
documents as exhibits MI to M29. Petitioner argued that The deposition submitted by the Respondent management 
witness are contrary to the record available in the case and the Respondents have not filed any documents to prove 
their contentions submitted in the depositions adduced before the Enquiry Officer to show that Petitioner has 
committed irregularities as alleged in the chargesheet. Petitioner contended that he has also lodged a complaint 
before the Circle Inspector of Police, Karimnagar against the Hero Honda showroom, Karimnagar stating that the 
said showroom has not delivered the vehicle within stipulated time inspite of receiving the cheque for a sum of 
Rs.4I,!50/- and requested the police officials to take necessary action against the proprietor of the said showroom. 
Further, Petitioner submitted that Sri Vemula Kanakaraju had obtained advance receipt on plain paper on 20.9.2006 
earlier from Petitioner with a promise to handover the vehicle to Petitioner on 2.9.2006, and that advance receipt 
was kept by Sri Vemula Kanakaraju with him and Petitioner requested several times to hand over the same but he 
prolonged the matter. Further, the Petitioner submitted that Sri Vemula Kana Raju has prepared false promissory 
note on that receipt, the Enquiry Officer without considering the facts and circumstances of the case has submitted his 
report holding the charges levelled against the Petitioner stands proved. No evidence was produced before the 
Enquiry Officer that Petitioner has committed irregularities as alleged in the chargesheet. 

Il. The perusal of the documents on record would reveal that the Petitioner has obtained a proforma invoice/ 
quotation dated 0.8.2006, from M/s. Rajarajeshwara Motors, Hero Honda showroom, authorised dealer for 
purchasing two wheeler Splender+ motorcycle in his name and the price of the vehicle therein has been quoted as 
Rs.4,50/-. Further, record reveals that the Bank of India after sanctioning the vehicle loan has issued the cheque 
dated 5.9.2006 in the name of authorised dealer Hero Honda showroom, M/s. Rajarajeshwara Motors for 
Rs.4,!50/- and the said cheque has been encashed on 4.!0.2006. But Petitioner failed to submit the registration 
papers of vehicle purchase in terms of loan amount within stipulated time to the Respondent bank. Further, record 
goes to show that vehicle loan was sanctioned by the bank in the year 2006 for purchasing the Hero Honda Motor 
Cycle to the Petitioner but he has submitted the registration papers showing that Petitioner has purchased vehicle in 
the year 2009, after three years of sanctioning the loan and has not submitted any explanation to justify his conduct in 
view of terms and conditions of sanctioned loan. Therefore, it can safely be concluded that Petitioner has 
misappropriated the loan amount sanctioned in the yar 2006 for purchasing the two Wheeler motorcycle by 
encashing it. It is a settled law that the bank employee taking a loan for vehicle purchase, but not using the fund for 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 57] 


that purpose and misappropriating them is considered as misconduct. The bank can take disciplinary action against 
such employee and he could face criminal action u/s. Indian Penal Code and other relevant laws. Moreover the 
investigation officer finds employee guilty of committing such misconduct of misappropriation the Disciplinary 
Authority may impose the penalty of termination from employment. 


2. The record of enquiry proceeding goes to reveal that there is ample evidence on record to prove the charges 
of misappropriation of fund of sanctioned loan against the charge sheeted employee.. As regard the jurisdiction of the 
Tribunal to interfere in the order of the Disciplinary Authority, Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of State Bank of 
Bikaner and Jaipur versus Nemi Chand Nalwaya, in Civil Appeal No. 586 of 2007, 20il (4) SCC 584 
decided on March I, 20i, Hon’ble Apex Court have held:- 


“6. It is now well settled that the courts will not act as an appellate court and reassess the evidence led in the 
domestic enquiry, nor interfere on the ground that another view is possible on the material on record. If the 
enquiry has been fairly and properly held and the findings are based on evidence, the question of adequacy of 
the evidence or the reliable nature of the evidence will not be grounds for interfering with the findings in 
departmental enquiries. Therefore, courts will not interfere with findings of fact recorded in departmental 
enquiries, except where such findings are based on no evidence or where they are clearly perverse. The test to 
find out perversity is to see whether a tribunal acting reasonably could have arrived at such conclusion or 
finding, on the material on record. Courts will however interfere with the findings in disciplinary matters, if 
principles of natural justice or statutory regulations have been violated or if the order is found to be arbitrary, 
capricious, mala fide or based on extraneous considerations.” 
In view of law laid down by Hon'ble Apex Court it safely be concluded that finding recorded by Enquiry Officer 
holding charges of misappropriation of loan amount is on the basis of evidence and admission of Petitioner on record. 
Thus, the punishment order does not suffer from any illegality, impropriety or perversity hence, liable to be 
confirmed. 


3. Moreover, as regard seriousness and gravity of charges levelled and proved against Petitioner it cannot be 
said that the charges of misappropriation of funds by the Petitioner is simple in nature. In other words, it is serious 
and grave in nature. In this context, the reference of following decisions of Hon’ble Supreme Court are relevant as 
discussed hereunder:- 


Further, in the case of Disciplinary Authority vs. Nikunj Bihari Patnaik JT 996 (4) page 457 Hon’ble Supreme 
Court have held:- 


“In the case of a Bank - for that matter, in the case of any other organization - every officer/employee is 
supposed to act within the limits of his authority. If each officer/ employee is allowed to act beyond his 
authority, the discipline of the organisation/bank will disappear; the functioning of the Bank would become 
chaotic and unmanageable. Each officer of the Bank cannot be allowed to carve out his own little empire 
wherein he dispenses favours and largesse. No organization, more particularly, a Bank can function properly 
and effectively if its officers and employees do not observe the prescribed norms and discipline. Such 
indiscipline cannot be condoned on the specious ground that it was not actuated by ulterior motives or by 
extraneous considerations. The very act of acting beyond authority - that too a course of conduct spread over 
a sufficiently long period and involving innumerable instances - is by itself a misconduct. Such acts, if 
permitted, may bring in profit in some cases but they may also lead to huge losses. Such adventures are not 
given to the employees of Banks which deal with public funds. If what we hear about the reasons for the 
collapse of Barings Bank is true, it is attributable to the acts of one of its employees, Nick Leeson, a minor 
officer stationed at Singapore, who was allowed by his superiors to act far beyond his authority. As mentioned 
hereinbefore, the very discipline of an organization and more particularly, a Bank is dependent upon each of 
its employees and officers acting and operating within their allotted sphere. Acting beyond one's authority is 
by itself a breach of discipline and a breach of Regulation 3. It constitutes misconduct within the meaning of 
Regulation 24. No further proof of loss is really necessary though as a matter of fact, in this case there are 
findings that several advances and over-drawals allowed by the Respondent beyond his authority have become 
sticky and irrecoverable. Just because, similar acts have fetched some profit - huge profit, as the High Court 
characterizes it - they are no less blameworthy. It is wrong to characterize them as errors of judgment. It is 
not suggested that the Respondent being a Class-I officer was not aware of the limits of his authority or of his 
powers. Indeed, Charge No.9, which has been held established in full is to the effect that inspite of instructions 
by the Regional Office to stop such practice, the Respondent continued to indulge in such acts. The Enquiry 
Officer has recorded a clear finding that the Respondent did flout the said instructions and has thereby 
committed an act of disobedience of lawful orders. Similarly, Charge No.8, which has also been established in 
full is to the effect that inspite of reminders, the Respondent did not submit "Control Returns" 


to the Regional Office. We fail to understand how could all this be characterized as errors of judgment and not 
as misconduct as defined by the regulations. We are of the opinion that the High Court has committed a clear 
error in holding that the aforesaid conduct of the Respondent does not amount to misconduct or that it does 
not constitute violation of Regulations 3 and 24.” 


572 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


जा the case of Divisional Controller, NEKRTC vs. Amaresh, AIR 2006, SC page 2730, wherein Hon'ble 
Supreme Court of India have held, 


“amount misappropriated is not relevant to decide the quantum of punishment and it is always a question of loss of 
confidence by the management in the employee that matters.”’ 


Further, in the case of Chairman and Managing Director, United Commercial Bank vs. PC Kakkar AIR 2003 
SC page 357], Hon'ble Supreme Court of India have held, 


“Bank officer is required to exercise higher standards of honesty and integrity -Defence that there was no loss of 
profit resulting — not available when delinquent employee acted without authority- High Court setting aside as 
shockingly disproportionate without indicating reasons — Amounts to denial of justice- fact that co-delinquent is 
given lesser punishment — can also be no ground for interference.” 


Further, in JP Jain Vs. Management of State Bank of India 982 AIR page 673, Hon’ble Supreme Court have 
held:- 


“ the facts of the case are that the Appellant was working as cashier in the Meerut State branch of State Bank 
of India, the complainant, came to bank to receive his pass book. On receipt of pass book accountholder 
complained that he had withdrawn only Rs.500/- but there is entry of Rs.I500/- as shown in the passbook. 
The complainant reported matter to the Supervisor R K Gupta, and necessary documents pertaining to such 
withdrawal were examined and it was found that the complainant has given a letter of authority to the 
Appellant workman and authorizing to withdraw the amount Rs.500/-from his account. The said authority 
letter was for withdrawal of Rs.500/-, but, workman acting as cashier manipulated figure in the letter from 
Rs.500/- to 77. /300/- and withdrawn the amount retained 75, /099/- with him. 


In that case enquiry was held and the casual Appellant was dismissed from the job. In the case the alleged 
misconduct of the Appellant to produce forged documents and withdrew 75./300/- instead Rs.500/- and 
Rs.l000/- in excess of the amount which he was not authorized misappropriated it. In that case Appellant 
had submitted his confession letter and on the basis of the confession letter and substantial evidence he was 
terminated from the service and the Hon’ble Apex Court upheld the termination of the Appellant.” Therefore, 
plea of Petitioner that his letter of admission of guilt is not admissible evidence is not tenable.” 


In the case of Darshan Singh Vs. Canara Bank CWP .४०.।०0458/ 2003 D.O.D. 6.4.207 High Court held: 


“the Court has rightly observed that oral evidence contrary to documentary evidence and entries has not to be 
believed this was the case defendant for proof of misconduct on documentary evidence and entries in the Saving 
Bank Account pass book and ledger folios etc... The workman admitted that these entries made by him saying 
that it was made under bonafide mistake. Such act have serious propensities which when made public a bank 
might loss its reputation and confidence in investing public, not continue employment. It was the case of 
misappropriation. Even if it is classified as temporary misappropriation or temporary embezzlement both are 
bad of law. Workman therefore, cannot be absolved simply, by saying that entries were some bonafide 
mistake. In the opinion of Labour Court no lenience can be shown to the workman. Once the misconduct is 
proved in the enquiry conducted by employer, punishment imposed cannot be interfered with by the Tribunal 
except in cases where the punishment is so harsh as to suggest victimization.” 


Similarly, SBI vs Hemant Kumar 20]! () SC 890, Hon’ble Supreme Court have held:- 


# 7, The second reason assigned by the Tribunal that the Enquiry Officer should have allowed the Respondent 
the opportunity to lead evidence in rebuttal is also without substance in the overall facts of the case. The 
Respondent had already tendered two admissions of guilt in writing and one orally before 79.7 and there was 
hardly anything that could be said on his behalf to repel the charges. 


2, We are, therefore, satisfied that the Tribunal's findings are wholly unreasonable and perverse and fit to be 
set aside. The High Court, unfortunately, did not consider the matter as it should have, in light of the 
discussions made above. The High Court's order is equally unsustainable. We, accordingly, set aside the order 
passed by the High Court and the award made by the Tribunal. The appeal is allowed but with no order 
as to costs.” 


4. Further, reference of the case of the United Bank of India vs. Bachan Prasad Lal Civil Appeal No. 2949 
of 20i! date of decision .2.2022, is relevant in this context. The facts of the case are that workman CSE was the 
bank employee and he fraudulently prepared 9 credit transfer vouchers on various dates on the pretext of payment of 
interest towards fixed deposits and crediting the whole amount to one saving account opened in the name of one 
Smt.Asha Devi (admittedly the fake account prepared by Respondent employee). In order to adjust the said amount 
he manipulated the other book records of the bank using forged signatures. After such nature of allegations stood 
proved, the disciplinary authority after taking into consideration the record of inquiry and the post held by the 
Respondent employee, imposed the punishment to employee with the penalty of dismissal from service. In that case 
Hon’ble Supreme Court have held:- 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाहढ़ 2, 947 573 


#7, In our considered view, looking into seriousness of the nature of allegations levelled against the 
Respondent employee, the punishment of dismissal inflicted upon him in no manner could be said to be 
shockingly disproportionate which would have required to be interfered with by the Tribunal in exercise of its 
power under Section IIA of the Act 7947. At the same time, merely because the employee stood superannuated 
in the meanwhile, will not absolve him from the misconduct which he had committed in discharge of his duties 
and looking into the nature of misconduct which he had committed, he was not entitled for any indulgence. The 
Bank employee always holds the position of trust where honesty and integrity are the sine qua non but it would 
never be advisable to deal with such matters leniently. 


2, Consequently, the appeal succeeds and is allowed. The interference made by the Tribunal and the High 
Court in the impugned judgment is hereby set aside. No costs.” 


Thus, in view of the law laid down by the Hon'ble Apex Court even a temporary misappropriation of fund of bank by 
the employee amounts to serious and grave misconduct and once proved such charges against employee deserve the 
termination of employment. Further, charges can be held proved on the basis of admission made by CSE. In this 
case, Petitioner has already admitted second charge, hence there is no need for further to discuss here. 


5. Thus, in view of the fore gone discussion and law laid down by the Hon'ble Apex Court as discussed above, 
both the charges misappropriation and pilfering the official documents of bank being serious in nature therefore, the 
order of punishment of compulsory retirement of the Petitioner passed by Respondent Disciplinary Authority cannot 
be faulted with neither it can be said to be in anyway disproportionate to the gravity of the charges. _ Disciplinary 
Authority has already taken a lenient view in awarding the punishment of compulsory retirement with benefits of 
superannuation. Therefore, the imposition of said punishment cannot be termed as disproportionate to the gravity of 
charges and deserve to be upheld. Thus, the punishment order passed by Disciplinary Authority deserves to be 
upheld and claim petition filed by the Petitioner is devoid of merit he is liable to be dismissed. 


This point is decided against the Petitioner and in favour of the Respondent management. 


6. Issue No.II:- In view of the discussion and finding at Issues No.I & II, the Petitioner is not entitled for 
any relief and his petition is liable to be dismissed. 


This Issue is answered against the Petitioner. 
AWARD 


In view of the fore gone discussion and finding given at Issues No. I, If & I, I hold that the action of the 
Respondent of imposition of punishment of compulsorily retirement from service to the Petitioner Sri B. Venkati, 
with superannuation benefits as would be due otherwise and without disqualification from future employment under 
class 6(c) of the memorandum of settlement dated 0" April 2002, is hereby held legal and justified. The petition filed 
by the Petitioner sans merit, hence, dismissed. Therefore, the petition is dismissed. 


Award is passed accordingly. Transmit.

the 97 day of June, 2025. 


IRFAN QAMAR, Presiding Officer

In exercise of powers conferred under clause (d) of sub-section (l) and sub —section (2A) of Section l0 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 947 (4 of 947),Government Of India through the Ministry of Labour, vide its Order 
No.L-20/98/20l!-(IR(B-I)) dated 26/09/20l2 has been pleased to refer the following dispute between the 
employer i.e. management of Punjab National Bank and their workman through Dy. General Secretary, Punjab 
National Bank Staff Union (Bihar), Patna for adjudication by this Tribunal: 

“Whether the action the management of Punjab National Bank in forefitting the special functional 

allowance on temporary transfer of workmen, is legal and justified? What relief the workmen are 

entitled to?” 
2. On receiving order no. L-!20/98/20l-(R(B-ID) dated 26/09/20!2 Government of India, Ministry of Labour, 
New Delhi for adjudication of the dispute, Reference case no. 47 of 202 was registered on 30.!0.202 and thereafter 
the notices were sent to the parties with a direction to appear and submit their written statements along with relevant 
documents in support of their claims and the witnesses. 
3; After issuance of notice, the workmen never appeared before the Tribunal nor any step was taken by him 
whereas management has appeared previously but later on, failed to appear before the Tribunal. The workmen never 
appeared before the Tribunal since 30.]0.202. 
4. On perusal of the entire case record it is transpires that the workmen never appeared before this Tribunal for a 
period of !3 years which shows that the workmen have no interest in this case and therefore, for the ends of justice, 
this case deserves to be dismissed for non prosecution. 
5. Hence, 
ORDERED 

that this case is hereby dismissed and a “No Dispute Award” be drawn up in respect of the above reference case. 
Let the copies of Award in duplicate be sent to the Ministry of Labour & Employment, Government of India, New 
Delhi for information and notification.

AWARD 


Smt. A. Lakshmi, who worked as Sweeper (who will be referred to as the workman) has filed this petition 
under Sec. 2A(2) of the Industrial Disputes Act, !947 against the Respondents M/s. Oriental Bank of Commerce 
seeking for declaring the oral termination dated 4.5.203 issued by Respondent as illegal, arbitrary and to set aside 
the same consequently directing the Respondents to reinstate the Petitioner into service duly granting all the 
consequential benefits such as continuity of service, back wages and all other attendant benefits etc., and such other 
reliefs as this court may deems fit. 


2 The averments made in the petition in brief are as follows: 


It has been alleged by the Petitioner Workman that she was appointed by M/s. Spark Securities & Maintenance 
Services on 27.8.2005 as sweeper at Respondent No.2 office and was posted to work at Respondent No.! office on 
the initial salary of Rs.2000/- per month. Further, it is alleged that the Petitioner was assigned and allotted the day to 
day duties by the Manager of first Respondent branch bank OBC, Secunderabad from time to time and from the date 


576 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


of her posting in the first Respondent branch bank Petitioner was discharging her work with very sincerity and 
efficiently with due diligence and devotion. Further, it is alleged that on 4.5.20I3 Petitioner went to the 
Respondent No.! branch to report for her duties at 9:00 AM but the Branch Manager of the first Respondent bank 
stopped and prevented her from entering and discharging her duties in the bank premises by informing the Petitioner 
that her services has been orally terminated. Further, the Petitioner was told that she is not required to come for 
discharging her duties in the branch of the bank. Further, it is alleged that the verbal termination order dated 
4.5.203 of the Petitioner’s service is illegal, unjustified and invalid on the ground that there is no written order of 
termination served on the Petitioner and the Petitioner was not served one month notice nor was paid one month 
salary in lieu of that notice nor paid retrenchment compensation as per section 25 F of the I.D. Act, 947. Further, it 
is alleged that the termination order dated 4.5.203 is in contravention of the provision contained under section 25 F 
of the I.D. Act, !947 and also in contravention of the provision of section 25 H of the Act. Further, it is alleged that 
the Petitioner had worked continuously for 9 years under the first Respondent and moreover first Respondent used to 
allot day to day duties to the Petitioner as such Respondent No.2 is only the name sake employer as such alleged 
contract between the Respondent No.! and Respondent No.2 is camouflage and sham contract which is not valid in 
the eye of law. It is further alleged that First Respondent Bank by seeing the sincerity and dedicated and unblemished 
record of service of the Petitioner, had duly informed and issued the Petitioner with interview letter dated .2.2007. 
The said interview letter issued pertaining is "interview letter for the post of part time sweeper" in First Respondent 
Bank, Secunderabad. The said interview letter issued to Petitioner is only after carefully perusing his entire service 
record, satisfaction, performance, character, attitude, honesty, approach and attitude towards co-workers and bank 
officials and staff and sincerely obeying all the instruction and orders and discharging the duties as directed by the 
First Respondent branch bank. In the said interview letter, First Respondent bank instructed Petitioner to attend the 
said interview at Regional Office, Banjara Hills, Hyderabad. It is submitted that, after receipt of the said interview 
letter, the Petitioner personally attended the said interview which had been conducted for the post of "Part Time 
Sweeper" on the prescribed date along with relevant records i.e. school certificate, caste certificate, service certificate, 
employment exchange card. The persons who were having the requisite qualifications and who have studied up to 5 
class only the interview letter were sent to them and those persons who have received the interview letter are only 
eligible for attending the interview conducted for the afore mentioned post by the Regional Office of the First 
Respondent bank. As per the conditions notified in the interview letter, the concerned officials who conducted the 
said interview has enquired all the details about the day to day conducting / discharging of the normal duties in the 
First Respondent bank, on that, the Petitioner have given all the answers correctly and also clearly stated that, the 
Petitioner was doing all type of duties whatever has been entrusted to her by the said bank officials without any 
protest. The Petitioner further states that, those persons who have attended to the said interview along with Petitioner 
for the same post are Kavitha, Masthanamma, Meena, Danamma, Aruna, Sridevi, Rashida etc., all these women are 
the co-workers of Petitioner as well as they were been deputed and posted by the said Second Respondent 
Management, whereas, their services were regularized / permanent 'but surprisingly the Petitioner’s services were not 
regularized for the reasons best known to the First Respondent bank. It is submitted that, after completion of 
interview till the date of termination of service of the Petitioner, the Petitioner did not receive any communication / 
letter about the regularization of her service by the Regional Office of the First Respondent Bank nor not known 
about the status of the interview which was been attended by the Petitioner, whether the Petitioner had been selected 
or rejected for the said post. The other persons whose names mentioned above had attended the said interview, have 
been selected by making their services permanent and those persons are presently working in the various branches of 
the First Respondent bank in /around of Hyderabad and at present they are drawing salary of Rs.!2,000/- per month. 
It is submitted that, after waiting for considerable time and under fond hope that the First Respondent Regional Bank 
and Branch Bank would regularize the service of the Petitioner duly confirming for the post of part time Sweeper in 
the First Respondent bank but to no use as there is no other go, on that, the Petitioner gave a representation notice to 
the First Respondent Regional Bank (OBC), Banjara Hills, Hyderabad vide notice, dated 07-03-203 by demanding 
the said Regional Office to regularize the Petitioner’s service, the said notice was received by the First Respondent 
Regional Bank but did not consider the demand at all nor gave any reply to the said notice. It is submitted that, First 
Respondent bank hatched a preplanned conspiracy to throw the Petitioner out of her service somehow or the other as 
the First Respondent bore a grudge against the Petitioner, as the Petitioner was always demanding for her rightful 
benefits ie., regularization of her service by way of personal notice as well as legal notice on various dates. The First 
Respondent Branch Manager had illegally and verbally terminated the service of the Petitioner on 4-05-203 without 
any prior intimation or notice. The Petitioner got shocked and surprised by the drastic decision of the First 
Respondent Branch Manager in terminating her service in an arbitrary manner, the Petitioner is having spot less 
character and does not have any bad remark nor any allegations against the Petitioner whatsoever through her service 
from the First Respondent bank, There is no written termination order given by First Respondent Branch Manager nor 
the First Respondent issued the Petitioner with one month prior notice nor in lieu of notice one month salary before 
terminating the Petitioner from service. The same amounts to unfair trade practices, violation of principles of natural 
justice, against the provision of I.D.Act, 947. The said illegal acts adopted against the Petitioner by the first 
Respondent is nothing but instant termination without following the due process of law. Moreover, preventing and 
stopping the Petitioner from discharging the duties amounts to termination of service. Therefore, it is prayed that 
the oral termination order dated 4.5.203 of the Respondent No.l, Oriental Bank of Commerce -Branch Manager, 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 7: 


Sarojini Devi Road, Secunderabad be set aside and the Respondent २०.] be directed to reinstate the Petitioner into 
the service with all benefits. 


3. Notice served upon both the Respondents. But Respondent No.! filed counter. Despite the service of notice 
R2 did not file any counter and thus, case was set ex-parte against R2 vide order dated 7..204. 


4. The Respondents filed counter denying the averments made in the petition, with the averments in brief 
which runs as follows: 


The Respondent No.! has contended in his counter that the present petition is not maintainable in law or on the facts 
of the case and the same is liable to be dismissed. It is submitted that the Petitioner has admitted in her petition that 
she was appointed by M/s. Sparks Security and Maintenance Services on 27.8.2005. Admittedly, she was paid wages 
by M/s. Sparks Security and Maintenance Services. She was accorded the benefit of ESI and Provident Fund by 
M/s. Sparks Security and Maintenance Services. Her attendance record was maintained by them. Her statutory 
deductions were made and remitted to statutory agencies by M/s. Sparks Security and Maintenance Services. She has 
admitted that she was under the supervision and control of M/s. Sparks Security and Maintenance Services. They used 
to direct duties to her and take disciplinary action, if any. No "industrial dispute" exists between the Petitioner and the 
Oriental Bank of Commerce. The application is thus liable to be dismissed. The Central Govt. cum Industrial Tribunal 
does not have the jurisdiction to deal with the present application. The dispute, if any, exists between the Petitioner 
and her employer, M/s. Sparks Security and Maintenance Services, which is a private agency. They do not come 
within the purview of Central Govt. The Petitioner was considered for the regular; post of part time sweeper in the 
Bank in 2007, subject to criteria of age, educational qualifications etc. The Petitioner was found overage and thus 
rejected. She has not challenged the said rejection and thus it attained finality. The application is thus liable to be 
dismissed. That the Petitioner submitted forged documents to obtain employment in Bank. Thus she is not entitled to 
appointment in Bank. The duly attested documents showed her date of birth as 24.05.966 and as 976 in her Aadhar 
card issued on 25.03.20l!. She also submitted dubious documents pertaining to her qualification, which were not 
relied upon. The application is thus liable to be dismissed. That no regularization can be claimed under Sec 2A of the 
Act. The Petitioner was sometimes deputed to work by M/s. Sparks Security and Maintenance Services in Oriental 
Bank of Commerce Hyderabad, for cleaning the tiles in toilets of the branch, before the start of the Branch, till the 
time the regular recruitment takes place, during the period of 2005 to March 20]3, ie. during the period of subsistence 
of contract between the Oriental Bank of Commerce and M/s. Sparks Security and Maintenance Services. Thus, the 
present application is not maintainable in law. It is submitted that the Petitioner was sent the interview letter as she 
had applied for the post of part time sweeper in the Bank and not because of her purported dedication or unblemished 
record, as alleged. She was never allotted any duty by the Bank. The Petitioner never worked full time in the Bank, as 
alleged. She always worked under the exclusive supervision and control of the Respondent No.2. Further, it is 
submitted that she was never allotted any duty by the Bank. The Petitioner was considered for the regular post of 
part time sweeper in the Bank in 2007, subject to criteria of age, educational qualifications etc. The credentials of the 
documents are verified after the interview. The Petitioner was found overage and thus rejected. She has not 
challenged the said rejection order and thus it attained finality. The application is thus liable to be dismissed. She has 
also submitted dubious documents pertaining to her qualification, which were not relied upon. The candidates with 
authentic credentials were appointed. The Petitioner has herself stated that Kavitha, Masthanamma, Meena, Aruna 
etc. were appointed. She was duly apprised that she was not eligible for appointment as part time sweeper in the 
Bank and was aware of the same. No assurance was ever given by the Bank to the Petitioner that her services would 
be regularized, as alleged. The Petitioner has no right to regular appointment in the Bank, sans her eligibility for the 
same. Laxmi is the regular appointee of the Bank and the Petitioner cannot claim right on the said appointment. It is 
unfathomable as which provision of law has been violated by the Bank by making regular recruitments. Laxmi was 
eligible as well as sent the interview letter. The Petitioner is seeking a back door entry in the Bank. The Petitioner is 
not eligible for appointment in the Bank. The Petitioner has no merits, as alleged nor is eligible for any benefit, as 
alleged. Regular recruitments, wherein she has been considered and rejected is neither an unfair trade practice nor any 
injustice to anyone. The Petitioner has not performed any work in the Bank after the regular recruitments have taken 
place. The services of the Petitioner were terminated by Respondent No.2. Since she was never appointed by the 
replying Respondent, no question arises for termination of her services by them. The Petitioner has no right to regular 
appointment in the Bank, sans her eligibility for the same. There can be no regularization/appointment de hors the 
policy of the Bank wherein the Petitioner was considered and rejected. The issue was set to rest upon her rejection 
under the policy of the Bank. The Petitioner is "workman" of the Respondent No.2. The dispute is between 
Petitioner and Respondent No.2 as they had appointed the workman. Admittedly, she was paid wages by them. She 
availed the benefit of ESI and Provident Fund from them. Her attendance record was maintained by them. Her 
statutory deductions were made and remitted to statutory agencies by them. She admitted that she was under their 
supervision and control and that they used to direct duties to her. Hon'ble CGIT has no jurisdiction to deal with the 
present case. Since she was never appointed by the Bank, there arises no question of termination of her services by 
the Bank by one month's notice or any retrenchment compensation. Cleaning tiles of toilets is neither ancillary to 
Banking nor a perennial nature of job. Intermittently she used to clean the tiles. The contract is thus legal and 
justified. The dispute is between Petitioner and Respondent No.2 as they had appointed/terminated the workman. 
Therefore, prayed that the applicant is not entitled for any relief and to dismiss the application with costs. 


578 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


2, In support of the claim petition Petitioner has examined witness WWI in oral evidence and also filed 
documents in evidence ४5.0७] to WI9 through the list. Whereas Respondent in oral evidence has filed the chief 
statement affidavit of ०७७] and also filed the documents exhibited 75.७] Ex.M3. 


6. On the basis of the rival pleadings of both parties and arguments of Learned Counsel for both the 
parties, the following issues_emerge for determination:- 
I. Whether the action of the Respondent No.| in terminating the services of the Petitioner vide order 
with effect from 4.5.20]3 is illegal and unjustified? 
Il. Whether the Petitioner is entitled for appointment in the employment of Respondent No.! on the 
ground as alleged in the claim petition? 
Ill. To what relief if any the Petitioner is entitled for? 
Findings:- 
7. Issue__No.I:- It is undisputed that the Petitioner was appointed by the M/s. Spark Securities and 


Maintenance Services, contractor, Respondent No.2 on 27.8.2005 as a sweeper. The Petitioner witness WWI 
herself admitted in her testimony that as per allegation of the Petitioner she was posted for doing the work at the 
branch of the Bank of the Respondent bank at Sarojini Devi Road, Secunderabad with effect from 27.8.2005 and she 
has worked continuously upto !4.5.203 without any break of about 9 years and Respondent No.| has terminated her 
from services with effect from 4.5.203 in contravention of the provision contained under section 25F of the LD. 
Act, !947. In order to corroborate her claim petition witness WW| in chief affidavit statement has stated that she 
was appointed by M/s. Spark Securities and Maintenance Services, Contractor, Respondent No.2 on 27.8.2005 as a 
sweeper and she worked at the branch Bank of the Respondent No.l M/s. Oriental Bank of Commerce at Sarojini 
Devi Road, Secunderabad, since 27.8.2005 till her termination with effect from 4.5.20]3. 


8. Further, this witness was cross examined by the Respondent counsel and in the cross examination ऐै०७। 
states that +5. ४४| and W2 have been issued by the Respondent No.2 to her. Further, witness states that she has not 
filed any document to show that Respondent No.| was giving salary towards her work. Further, witness states that 
she has not filed any document to show that she was regularly working at the office of the Respondent. The witness in 
the cross examination stated that, 


“T have not filed any document to show that Respondent 2४०, / was giving me the salary towards my work. I have 
also not filed any document to show that I was regularly working in the office of the Respondent No.l. I have 
not filed any documents in this case to show that Respondent १०0, / has instructed me to work in the bank and the 
bank had paid the wages daily to me till the date of my termination. It is not true to suggest that the averments 
made by me in my chief evidence are not correct.” 


Thus, from the statement of Petitioner WWI it manifest that she has not produced any documents in support of her 
claim that she had worked in the branch Bank of the Respondent No.| continuously for the alleged period of 9 years. 
Witness herself admitted that she has not produced any document pertaining to her appointment by the Respondent 
No.! or payment of salary. Whatever document has been produced by the Petitioner in support of the claim, +5.४७। 
is the alleged posting order which has been issued by the Respondent No.2, the contractor. The contents of this 
document Ex.WI goes to reveal that the Petitioner Smt. Akula Lakshmi has been posted by the Manager 
Recruitment, Sparks Security & Maintenance Services at the office of the Respondent No.! with effect from 
27.8.2005, but the issuing authority of Ex.WI letter has not been examined by the Petitioner to prove it in support of 
her claim. Further Ex.W4 is the certificate issued by the Respondent No.2 and Petitioner’s claim doesn't find any 
support from it. Further, the documents Ex.W5 to Ex.WI0 do not pertain to the employment with the Respondent, 
hence, are not relevant to the claim of the Petitioner that she had worked at the Respondent No.! branch for the 
alleged period. 


9. However, Respondent No.! in evidence has filed the chief statement affidavit of ०७] and witness in his 
testimony has specifically stated that Petitioner was appointed by Sparks Securities & Maintenance Services from 
27.8.2005 and she was paid by the Respondent No.2 i.e., M/s. Sparks Security & Maintenance Services. Further, 
५७७४] states that workman was accorded the benefit of ESI and Provident fund by M/s. Sparks Security & 
Maintenance Services and her attendance record was maintained by them. Further, MW states that workman stated 
that she was under the supervision and control of M/s. Sparks Security & Maintenance Services and they used to 
direct duties to her and also take disciplinary action against her. ७४] categorically stated that there was no employer 
and employee relationship between the Respondent No.! and Petitioner workman and hence, there exists no 
industrial dispute. | Respondent witness ०७४] has specifically denied the allegation made by the Petitioner. 
However, despite the sufficient opportunity accorded Petitioner did not cross examine MWI. Therefore, in the 
absence of the cross examination, the statement in chief of MW remained uncontraverted. 


0. As regards the allegation of Petitioner that she had worked continuously for 9 years in the branch of 
Respondent bank and she has been terminated in contravention of the provision of section 25 F of the I.D. Act, 947, 
the provision contained under section 25F reads out as follows:- 


[भाग Il—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 579 


Section 2577 in The Industrial Disputes Act, 7947 


25F. Conditions precedent to retrenchment of workmen.- No workman employed in any industry who has been 
in continuous service for not less than one year under an employer shall be retrenched by that employer until— 


(a) the workman has been given one month' s notice in writing indicating the reasons for retrenchment and the 
period of notice has expired, or the workman has been paid in lieu of such notice, wages for the period of the 
notice: 


(b) the workman has been paid, at the time of retrenchment, compensation which shall be equivalent to fifteen 
days' average pay for every completed year of continuous service] or any part thereof in excess of six months; 
and 


(c) notice in the prescribed manner is served on the appropriate Government or such authority as may be 
specified by the appropriate Government by notification in the Official Gazette]. 


Compensation to workmen in case of transfer of undertakings. 


l]. Further, the burden of proof lies on the Petitioner to prove the fact that she has been terminated by the 
Respondent in contravention of provision contained under section 25 F of I.D. Act, 947. In this context, the 
following decisions of Hon’ble Supreme Court are relevant as hereunder:- 


In the case of Manager, R.B.I., Bangalore Vs. S. Mani & others AIR 2005 SC 279 the Hon’ble Supreme Court 
have held:- 


“BURDEN OF PROOF: 


The initial burden of proof was on the workmen to show that they had completed 240 days of service. 


In Range Forest Officer Vs. S.T. Hadimani [(2002) 3 SCC 25], it was stated: 


"3In our opinion the Tribunal was not right in placing the onus on the management without first determining on 
the basis of cogent evidence that the Respondent had worked for more than 240 days in the year preceding his 
termination. It was the case of the claimant that he had so worked but this claim was denied by the appellant. It 
was then for the claimant to lead evidence to show that he had in fact worked for 240 days in the year preceding 
his termination. Filing of an affidavit is only his own statement in his favour and that cannot be regarded as 
sufficient evidence for any court or tribunal to come to the conclusion that a workman had, in fact, worked for 
240 days in a year. No proof of receipt of salary or wages for 240 days or order or record of appointment or 
engagement for this period was produced by the workman. On this ground alone, the award is liable to be set 
aside. 


[See also Essen Deinki Vs. Rajiv Kumar, (2002) 8 SCC 400] In Siri Niwas (supra), this Court held: 


"The provisions of the Indian Evidence Act per se are not applicable in an industrial adjudication. The general 
principles of it are, however applicable. It is also imperative for the Industrial Tribunal to see that the 
principles of natural justice are complied with. The burden of proof was on the Respondent herein to show that 
he had worked for 240 days in preceding twelve months prior to his alleged retrenchment. In terms of Section 
25-F of the Industrial Disputes Act, /947, an order retrenching a workman would not be effective unless the 
conditions precedent therefor are satisfied. Section 25-F postulates the following conditions to be fulfilled by 
employer for effecting a valid retrenchment : 


(i) one month's notice in writing indicating the reasons for retrenchment or wages in lieu thereof; 


(ii) payment of compensation equivalent to fifteen days, average pay for every completed year of continuous 
service or any part thereof in excess of six months." 


Further, in Rajasthan State Ganganagar S. Mills Ltd. v. State of Rajasthan and Anr. (2004) Apex Court 
held: - 


“It was the case of the workman that he had worked for more than 240 days in the year concerned. This claim 
was denied by the appellant. It was for the claimant to lead evidence to show that he had worked for 240 days 
in the year preceding the date of his termination. He has filed an affidavit. It is statement which is in his 
favor and that cannot be regarded as sufficient evidence for any Court or Tribunal to come to the conclusion 
that in fact the claimant had worked for 240 days in a year.” 


In the case of Mohan Lal v. Management, BEL 98 SCC P. 225, Hon'ble Apex Court held, " 


Before a workman can claim retrenchment, not being in consonance of Section 25 of the ID act. he has to show 
that he has been in continuous service of not less than I year with the employer who had retrenched him from 
service." 


580 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


The Hon'ble Apex Court in this case also laid down the principle of how to count 240 days of service 
within one year and held: 


"Clause (2)(a) provides for a fiction to treat a workman in continuous service for a period of one year despite 
the fact that he has not rendered uninterrupted service for a period of one year but he has rendered service 
period of 240 days during the period of 72 calendar months for counting backwards and just preceding the 
relevant date being the date of retrenchment. In other words, in order to invoke the fiction enacted in clause 
(2)(a) it is necessary to determine first the relevant date, ie the date of termination of service which is 
complained of as retrenchment. After that date is ascertained. move backward to a period of 72 months just 
preceding the date of retrenchment and then ascertain whether within the period of /2 months, the workman 
has rendered service for a period of 240 days. If these three facts are affirmatively answered in favor of the 
workman pursuant to the deeming fiction enacted in clause (2)(a) it will have to be assumed that the workman 
is in continuous service for a period of one year and he will satisfy the eligibility qualification enacted in 
Section 25-F". 


In the case of GM., BSNL and others V. Mahesh Chand AIR 2008 SC (Supp) 328, wherein the Hon’ble Apex 
Court have held, 


“Tt was the case of the claimant that he had so worked but this claim was denied by the appellant. It was then for the 
claimant to lead evidence to show that he had in fact worked for 240 days in the year preceding his termination. 
Filing of an affidavit is only his own statement in his favour and that cannot be regarded as sufficient evidence for 
any Court or Tribunal to come to the conclusion that a workman had, in fact, worked for 240 days in a year. No proof 
of receipt of salary or wages for 240 days or order or record of appointment or engagement for this period was 
produced by the workman. On this ground alone, the award is liable to be set aside.” 


Thus, in view of law laid down by Hon'ble Apex Court as discussed above the initial burden lies upon the Petitioner 
Workman to prove that she had worked for 240 days continuously in a year just preceding the date of her termination. 
But Petitioner has not produced any documentary evidence i.e., any appointment letter issued by Respondent १०.। or 
any salary slip or wage payment slip or attendance register to establish the fact that she had worked in the branch of 
Respondent bank for the alleged period. Mere filing of the chief statement affidavit of witness is not sufficient to 
corroborate the claim of the Petitioner as she has alleged in the petition. 


2. It is settled the law that the documentary evidence of appointment letter or salary slip or attendance register 
are required to prove the fact of 240 days in continuous service of the Respondent employment just preceding from 
the date of termination. Therefore, for the want of aforementioned documentary evidence, the claim of the Petitioner 
that she was under the employment of Respondent ४०. continuously and had worked for 9 years in the Bank of the 
Respondent, is not proved and established. Therefore, the claim of the Petitioner that she has been terminated in 
contravention of the provision contained under section 25 F is not acceptable as she failed to prove the condition 
precedent as provided under section 25 F for alleged retrenchment. Moreover, MW] states that Petitioner was never 
appointed by the Respondent No.! as such there is no question of termination of her services by the bank by one 
month’s notice nor any retrenchment compensation as she was never appointed by the bank, hence, there arises no 
question of termination of her services by the bank. Therefore, in view of the fore gone discussion and law laid 
down by the Hon'ble Apex Court as discussed above, this issue is decided against the Workman Petitioner. 


I3. Issue No.II:- Petitioner has claimed that the Respondent No.| bank after seeing the sincerity and 
dedication and unblemished record of service of Petitioner had issued her interview letter dated !.2.2007 and she 
appeared in the interview for the post of part time sweeper in the Respondent No.! bank, Secunderabad, along with 
her relevant records i.e., school certificate, caste certificate, service certificate, employment exchange card. But the 
Respondent No.! did not select her on the basis of interview whereas for the same post other applicants i.e., Kavitha, 
Mastanamma, Meena, Danamma, Aruna, Sridevi and Rashida etc., have been selected and posted by the Respondent 
management and their services have been regularised on permanent basis by the first Respondent bank. Petitioner 
has not received any communication or letter about her regularisation in the services from Respondent No.! and she 
has no knowledge of result of her said interview. 


4. In this context, Petitioner has examined herself as WWI and she has reiterated the allegations made in the 
claim petition. However, in the cross examination WW has stated that Ex.W3 has been issued by Respondent ९०.। 
calling her to attend the interview for the post of part time sweeper. It is not true to suggest that she was not selected 
for the post of part time sweeper in the interview. It is not true to suggest that as because she was not selected she 
has not produced any documents in that context. Further, witness states that she has been verbally offered to work in 
the Respondent branch. 


5. Per contra, Respondent in this context has filed the chief statement affidavit of MWI and witness ०७। 
stated that the Petitioner was considered for regular post of the part time sweeper in the bank in 2007 subject to 
criteria of age, educational qualifications etc., and on scrutiny of document Petitioner was found overage and thus her 
candidature was rejected. Further, MWI states that credentials of documents were verified after the interview and 
Petitioner was found overaged and thus her candidature was rejected. Further ०७४] states that Petitioner has not 


[भाग वा--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 5i8] 


challenged the said rejection and thus it has attained finality. Further, MWI states that the Petitioner has submitted 
forged documents to obtain employment in the bank and she is not entitled to appointment in bank. Witness further 
states that the duly attested documents showed her date of birth as 24.3,[966 and in her aadhar card issued on 
25.3.20l it has been shown as 97l. Further, witness states that she has also submitted dubious documents 
pertaining to her qualification which were not relied upon. MW states that candidates with authentic credentials were 
appointed. The Petitioner has cheated the bank. Further, ४७४] stated that Kavitha, Mastanamma, Meena, Aruna 
etc., were appointed. Petitioner was duly apprised that she was not eligible for appointment as part time sweeper in 
the bank and she was aware of the same. Further, ५४] states that no assurance was ever given by the bank to the 
Petitioner that her services would be regularised. Petitioner has no right to regular appointment in the bank sans_her 
eligibility for the same. Further, श०७] states that no right has accrued to the Petitioner to seek appointment 
/regularization in the bank sans her eligibility for the same. MW states that for regular recruitment she has been 
considered and rejected and it is neither an unfair trade practice nor any injustice to anyone. The Petitioner has not 
performed any work in the bank after the regular recruitments have taken place. Further witness MW states that a 
notification dated 24.6.202 was issued in July, 202 and she has applied for appointment giving her date of birth as 
0..983. In the process of 2007, the eligibility education qualification was the 7" fail and during 20l2 
recruitment process it was 0" fail, and during 20l2 recruitment process was 0" pass and 2" fail. Looking into the 
eligibility, the Petitioner had filed fake documents giving her age differently at different times and dubious 
declarations of her educational qualifications. Thus, she was not found entitled for regular employment /regularization 
in the bank. Thus, the statement of this witness goes to reveal that the Petitioner was issued interview letter and she 
was interviewed but due to disqualification she was not selected for regular post. The perusal of document filed on 
record reveals that in application date of birth of Petitioner has been shown as 0..983 and in the aadhar card her 
date of birth has been shown as 24.6.[976. However, the details registered with ESI- date of birth of the Petitioner is 
shown as |.7.974. Thus, there is a discrepancy in the details of date of birth of the Petitioner. The documents filed 
by the Respondent regarding recruitment of part time sweeper in December 2007 goes to show that date of birth of the 
Petitioner has been shown as l0.!.977 whereas in the aadhar card her date of birth was shown as 24.6.976. 
However, despite sufficient opportunity granted Petitioner did not cross examine ५७४]. Therefore, the statement in 
chief affidavit of the witness MWI as stated above, remained uncontroverted. Petitioner has not filed any documents 
in evidence in order to controvert the contention of Respondent regarding discrepancy in the date of birth of 
Petitioner. 


6. Therefore, in view of the above, the claim of the Petitioner that she was eligible for regular appointment in 
the branch of the Respondent No.| is not established by cogent and reliable evidence. 


This issue is decided against the Petitioner and in favour of Respondent. 


7. Issue_No. III: - In view of the fore gone discussion and finding at Issues No.I & II, the Petitioner is not entitled 
to any relief and present petition is liable to be dismissed. 


This Point is answered accordingly. 
AWARD 


The claim petition filed by the Petitioner Smt. A. Lakshmi, u/s 2A(2) of I.D. Act, 947 challenging the 
alleged termination order dated !4.5.203 is devoid of merit, hence, dismissed. 


Award is passed accordingly. Transmit.
