AWARD 


The Petitioner who was an employee of |** Respondent company has filed this Petition invoking Sec.2 A (2) 
of the I.D. Act, 947, seeking for declaring the order dated 30..20I0 issued by the “ Respondent as_ illegal, 
arbitrary and to set aside the same and to grant consequential relief of reinstatement to Petitioner into service duly 
granting of other consequential benefits such as, continuity of service, back wages, all other attendant benefits. 
Respondents are contesting this petition by filing their counter. 


2: The averments made in the petition in brief are as follows: 


It is submitted that Petitioner joined in service as a Clerk/Cashier on 8.4.99! at Nizamabad Branch Nizamabad 
District, he worked in various branches in the Respondent bank. Since the date of his appointment Petitioner have 
been discharging his duties to the entire satisfaction of his superiors. It is submitted that while Petitioner have been 
discharging his duties as a clerk/cashier at Miyapur Branch R.R. District, on 25//0/2008, the Respondent bank 
officials entrusted the work of Computers Terminal Operator. It is submitted that on 02..2009 the 2™ Respondent 
i.e. Chief Manager has addressed a letter to the Branch Manager alleging that Petitioner has committed certain 
irregularities and advised the Branch Manager to take necessary action, and further advised to issue suspension order 
to the Petitioner. On the same day the Branch Manager has issued suspension order Dt. 2.l.2009 suspending the 
Petitioner from service on allegation that Petitioner has committed irregularities while functioning as Clerk/Cashier in 
the Branch which may result in tarnishing the image of the bank and shortage/loss of the cash to the Respondent bank. 
After receipt of the same Petitioner has submitted a letter to the Respondent bank stating that he has not committed 
any irregularities as alleged in the suspension order. The allegation made in the suspension order is false and 
incorrect. The allegation was made against the Petitioner pertaining to the transaction took place in the month of 
October, 2008. It is submitted that based on the suspension order Dt. 2..2009 after lapse of more than 7 months the 
Respondent bank has issued Charge Sheet. Dt. 5.8.2009 alleging that Petitioner has created/entered the transactions 
by misusing the user ID 33]50 KND of Sri. N. Krishnabrahmam Manager between 9:49.37 AM and 9:57 AM on 
25.0.2008, and further alleged that Petitioner has withdrawn Rs. 6,000/- from two ATM Hits of Rs. 5,000/- and

evidences adduced during the enquiry. The claimant was given adequate opportunity to rebut the charges by 
producing contra-evidence and by cross examination of witness. There is nothing to indicate any prejudice against the

4. The Petitioner has submitted written arguments u/s |lA of the Industrial Disputes Act, 947. 

5. On the basis of rival pleadings of both parties and arguments advanced, the following issues arise for 
determination: - 

I. Whether the departmental enquiry held against the Workman is legal and valid? 

Il. Whether the action of the Respondent management in awarding the punishment of compulsorily retired the 


Workman Sri P.J.C. Bhanudev with superannuation benefits that is pension, PF, gratuity, as would be due 
otherwise under the rules and regulations prevailing at the time and without disqualification for future 
employment is legal and justified? 


Ill. To what relief is the Petitioner entitled for? 
Findings:- 


6. Issue No.I:- This issue has been decided by the Tribunal vide order dated 9.6.2023 and the departmental 
enquiry has been held legal and valid. 


This issue is decided accordingly. 


7. Issue No.II: - This issue pertains to whether the imposition of punishment of compulsory retirement with 
superannuation benefits i.e., pension and /or provident fund and gratuity as would be due otherwise under the rules or 
regulations prevailing at the relevant time and without disqualification from future employment is legal and justified. 


8. In this context, the Learned Counsel for Workman has submitted that after receipt of chargesheet dated 
5.8.2009 Petitioner Workman had submitted a detailed explanation on 6.0.2009 denying the charges levelled against 
him. Petitioner clearly stated in his explanation that he had made some entries by mistake in usual /routine manner by 
using his ID and all entries were authorised by manager only. Further, Petitioner stated that, neither he used the 
user ID of the Manager nor authorised the entries as alleged in the chargesheet dated 5.8.2009. Further, it is stated 
that certain amount was there in his account and he availed the same, when it came to notice that there was a wrong 
credit in his account, he had adjusted the amount and _ thus, no loss was caused either to the Respondent bank or to 
any individual. Petitioner further submitted that in his explanation that he neither have any intention to 
misappropriate the Respondent bank funds nor defraud the bank and further requested the Respondent bank to drop 
all further proceedings in the matter. Further, it is submitted on behalf of workman that Sri Rajender, account holder 
bearing account No. 2!49000!0980700 submitted certificate dated 7.2.2009 to whomsoever it may concern stating 
that he has handed over the cash, a sum of Rs. 6,000/- in cash on 25.0.2008 to the Petitioner for depositing in his 
saving account. Hence, based on the above certificate dated 7.2.2009, it reveals that Petitioner has not committed 
any irregularities as alleged by the Respondent bank. Further, it is submitted that the Enquiry Officer has not given 
reasonable opportunity to the Petitioner to defend his case. The Enquiry Officer has not recorded the defence 
statement properly and he has acted as per the directions of the Respondent bank. Thus, he acted in a biased manner 
and without taking into consideration the material facts available on record. Further, it is submitted that there is no 
sufficient evidence to conclude that cash was withdrawn from ATM by the Workman Petitioner therefore, with 
regard to first charge there is no evidence before the Enquiry Officer to show that Petitioner has committed 
irregularities as alleged in the charge sheet hence the findings of the Enquiry Officer are perverse and liable to be 
vitiated. Further, it is submitted that statement of ५०७४] who inspected the record in the Respondent bank regarding 
alleged incident on 2.!.2009 cannot be relied on as he is neither an eyewitness nor a circumstantial witness nor a 
cause to the incident. Therefore, he cannot be a valid witness. Further, it is submitted that no document was filed by 
the witness ७४४७४] to substantiate his statement. Respondent bank deliberately did not provide the details of the 
system log in and logout records as evidence to prove the allegations made against the Petitioner. Thus, the action on 


588 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


the part of the Respondent bank in furnishing the details of systems log in and logout records amounts to denial of 
principles of natural justice. On this ground the entire inquiry proceedings are liable to be vitiated. Further, it is 
submitted that Petitioner requested several times the disciplinary authority as well as Enquiry Officer to furnish the 
details of the video footage of the ATM of the branch in the enquiry to verify the facts of the alleged incident. But 
same was not furnished to the Petitioner inspite of repeated requests. Therefore, on this ground the enquiry 
proceeding is liable to be vitiated as no opportunity was given to the Petitioner which amounts to violation of 
principles of natural justice. Further, it is submitted that the Petitioner has submitted a detailed representation on 
6.0.200 to the disciplinary authority explaining that facts circumstances of the case and requested to drop all 
further proceedings in the matter and permit him to perform his regular duties but without considering the facts 
circumstances of the case the Chief Manager (Disciplinary Authority) has issued the proceeding dated 30..200 
imposing the punishment of “compulsorily retired” with superannuation benefits i.e., pension and/or Provident fund 
and gratuity as would be due otherwise under the rules and regulations prevailing at the relevant time and without 
disqualification from future employment in terms of para 6(a) of settlement dated 0.4.2002. No valid reasons were 
assigned in the order in imposing the aforesaid punishment. Therefore, the action on the part of Chief Manager 
(Disciplinary Authority) dated 30..200 is illegal, arbitrary and unjust. Further, it is submitted that the Petitioner 
has submitted an appeal to Appellate Authority denying the averments made therein and requested to drop all further 
proceedings in the matter. However, without considering the facts and circumstances of the case Appellate Authority 
has passed orders dated 2!.3.20l] rejecting the appeal and confirming the punishment order dated 30..200. 
Therefore, order of Disciplinary Authority as well as Appellate Authority is disproportionate to the gravity of the 
charge levelled against the Petitioner as the Appellate Authority has not taken into consideration the past service of 
the Petitioner. Further, it is submitted that in the given case no relevant documents were produced by the 
Disciplinary Authority to prove the charge levelled against the Petitioner and Disciplinary Authority failed to consider 
the Petitioner’s statement and hence taken action by way of imposing major penalty of compulsory retirement with 
superannuation benefits etc., without considering that Petitioner had put in more than 49 years of service in the 
Respondent bank without any break and remarks whatsoever. 


Therefore, the punishment of compulsory retirement with superannuation benefits is disproportionate to the gravity of 
the charges levelled against the Petitioner.

he created /entered unauthorisedly the transactions which was verified by him by misusing the user ID 33l50NKB of 
Sri N. Krishnabramham, Manager. Further, the CSE Workman who was posted as cash clerk cum cashier at branch 
of Miyapur of Punjab National Bank on 25.0.2008 has deposited the amount of Rs.l9566/- from the account of the 
account holder TRR College of Engineering & Technology at 9.49.37 AM and that amount was transferred by the 
charge sheeted Workman into the non-customer account on the same date at 9:49.37AM. Further, that an amount of 
Rs.6,000/- through transfer from the non customer account by the Workman in his own account and that amount was 
withdrawn by the Workman through ATM withdrawal. Thus, he has misappropriated the fund of the bank by 
indulging in fraudulent transaction. The nature of allegation against the CSE Petitioner crediting the amount in his 
own account from the other account holder is serious in nature, after such nature of allegation stood proved, the 
Disciplinary Authority, after taking into consideration the record of enquiry has passed the punishment order of 
compulsory retirement with superannuation benefits. 


Il. The perusal of the record reveals that the CSE Workman was given fair opportunity of hearing at every 
stage of proceedings which he availed and he never raised any objection complaining of causing any nature of 
prejudice before Enquiry Officer. Further, he received the documents filed and relied upon by the Respondent in 
support of the charge and he filed reply and cross examined management witness and also examined witness in 
defence. Thus. he attended the proceeding at every stage. The Enquiry Officer after appreciating all the evidence 
recorded during the enquiry has submitted his reasoned report finding the CSE Workman guilty of the charges. 
Thus, in view of the above no case is made out showing that the domestic enquiry suffers any procedural lapses or 
violation of principles of natural justice, thereby causing any prejudice to the rights of the Workman. Further, record 
of the enquiry proceeding goes to reveal that the management has examined MWI who _ has conducted vigilance 
inquiry on the incident and he has deposed in oral testimony and also proved the documents in evidence in support of 
the charge. The witness states as hereunder:- 


“As per document P-I, it is seen that Sri Bhanudev has entered the debit transaction of Rs. /9566/- in the 
account number 46320902/099099657.० 7  77फर College of Engg. and Technology vide transaction No. M 3795 at 
9.49 a.m. on 235./9.2006.

account number 4632093777760 (Non Customer Inter Branch Fund) vide transaction number M 3795 at 9.49

Thus, there is ample documentary and oral evidence on record in support of the charge holding the CSE Workman 
guilty of the charges. 


2. 


As regards the plea of the Workman that there is no sufficient evidence on record, in this context it is settled 


law that question of adequacy of evidence or reliable nature of evidence will not be agreed for interfering with the 
finding in the Departmental Enquiry unless such findings are based on no evidence or where they are clearly 
perverse. 


In State of Bikaner & Jaipur Vs. Nemi Chand Nalwaya in Civil Appeal No.586/2007 AIR 20 SC 93 dated 
.3.200I is relevant wherein the Hon’ble Apex Court have held:- 


“6. It is now well settled that the courts will not act as an appellate court and reassess the evidence led in the 
domestic Enquiry, nor interfere on the ground that another view is possible on the material on record. If the 
Enquiry has been fairly and properly held and the findings are based on evidence, the question of adequacy of 
the evidence or the reliable nature of the evidence will not be grounds for interfering with the findings in 
departmental enquiries. Therefore, courts will not interfere with findings of fact recorded in departmental 
enquiries, except where such findings are based on no evidence or where they are clearly perverse. The test to 
find out perversity is to see whether a tribunal acting reasonably could have arrived at such conclusion or 
finding, on the material on record. Courts will however interfere with the findings in disciplinary matters, if 
principles of natural justice or statutory regulations have been violated or if the order is found to be arbitrary, 
capricious, mala fide or based on extraneous considerations.” 


Further, in the case of State of Rajasthan Vs. Heem Singh, Civil Appeal No.3340/2020, (supra) Hon’ble Apex 
Court have held:- 


33. In exercising judicial review in disciplinary matters, there are two ends of the spectrum. The first embodies 
a rule of restraint. The second defines when interference is permissible. The rule of restraint constricts the 
ambit of judicial review. This is for a valid reason. The determination of whether a misconduct has been 
committed lies primarily within the domain of the disciplinary authority. The judge does not assume the mantle 
of the disciplinary authority. Nor does the judge wear the hat of an employer. Deference to a finding of fact by 
the disciplinary authority is a recognition of the idea that it is the employer who is responsible for the efficient 
conduct of their service. Disciplinary enquiries have to abide by the rules of natural justice. But they are not 
governed by strict rules of evidence which apply to judicial proceedings. The standard of proof is hence not the 
strict standard which governs a criminal trial, of proof beyond reasonable doubt, but a civil standard governed 
by a preponderance of probabilities. Within the rule of preponderance, there are varying approaches based on 
context and subject... ... ... ..... That is to satisfy the conscience of the court that there is some evidence to support 
the charge of misconduct and to guard against perversity. But this does not allow the court to re-appreciate 
evidentiary findings in a disciplinary Enquiry or to substitute a view which appears to the judge to be more 
appropriate. To do so would offend the first principle which has been outlined above. The ultimate guide is the 
exercise of robust common sense without which the judges’ craft is in vain.” 


In the case of State of Haryana versus Rattan Singh 977 ACC page 49 while dealing with the standard of proof/ 
Evidence applicable in domestic enquiry Hon'ble Supreme Court have held:- 


“4. It is well settled that in a domestic enquiry the strict and sophisticated rules of evidence under the Indian 
Evidence Act may not apply. Ail materials which are logically probative for a prudent mind are permissible. 
There is no allergy to hearsay evidence provided it has reasonable nexus and credibility. It is true that 
departmental authorities and administrative tribunals must be careful in evaluating such material and should 
not glibly swallow what is strictly speaking not relevant under the Indian Evidence Act. For this proposition it 
is not necessary to cite decisions nor text books, although we have been taken through case law and other 
authorities by counsel on both sides. The essence of a judicial approach is objectivity, exclusion of extraneous 
materials or considerations and observance of rules of natural justice. Of course, fairplay is the basis and if 
perversity or arbitrariness, bias or surrender of independence of judgment vitiate the conclusions reached, such 
finding,even though of a domestic tribunal, cannot be held good. However, the courts below mis-directed 
themselves, perhaps, in insisting that passengers who had come in and gone out should be chased and brought 
before the tribunal before a valid finding could be recorded. The 'residuum' rule to which counsel for the 


[भाग व--खण्ड 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाह़ 2, 947 59] 


respondent referred, based upon certain passengers from American jurisprudence does not go to that extent nor 
does the passage from Halsbury insist on such rigid requirement. The simple point is, was there some evidence 
or was there no evidence not in the sense of the technical rules governing regular court proceedings but in a 
fair common-sense way as men of understanding and wordly wisdom will accept. Viewed in this way, 
sufficiency of evidence in proof of the finding by a domestic tribunal is beyond scrutiny. Absence of any 
evidence in support of a ending is certainty available for the court to look into because it amounts to an error of 
law apparent on the record. We find, in this case, that the evidence of Chamanlal, Inspector of the flying squad, 
is some evidence which has elevance to the charge leveled against the respondent. Therefore, we are unable to 
hold that the order is invalid on that ground. “ 


Thus, in view of the principle laid down by the Hon’ble Supreme Court as discussed above the Tribunal has got no 
jurisdiction to reassess the evidence lead in the domestic enquiry nor to interfere on the ground that another view is 
possible on the material on record. In the present case enquiry has been fairly and properly held and findings are 
based on evidence. It is not the case that the finding of the Enquiry Officer is based on no evidence or suffers from 
perversity. Therefore, there is no ground in the present matter to interfere in the finding recorded by the Enquiry 
Officer and also to set aside punishment order passed by Disciplinary Authority on the basis of that finding. 


3. Further, as regards the plea of the Petitioner that the imposition of punishment is disproportionate to the 
charge levelled against the Petitioner, in this context Hon’ble Supreme Court in the case of Om Kumar vs. Union 
Of India 200 (SCC) 2 386 have held:- 


“The question of quantum of punishment in disciplinary matters is primarily for the disciplinary authority to 
order and the jurisdiction of the High courts under Article 226 of the Constitution or of the Administrative 
Tribunals is limited and is confined to the applicability of one or other of the well known principles known as 
“Wednesbury principles”. Further, it is held in the Wednesbury case, ((9486)7 KB 223 it was said when a 
statute gave discretion to an administrator to take a decision, the scope of judicial review would remain limited. 
Lord Greene further said that interference was not permissible unless one or the other of the following 
conditions was satisfied namely, 7) the order was contrary law, or 2) relevant factors were not considered, or 
3) irrelevant factors were considered, or 4) the decision was one which no reasonable person could have 
taken.” 


Further in the case of BC Chaturvedi vs. Union of India 995(6) SCC 749 in paragraph |8 Supreme Court have 
held:- 


776, A review of the above legal position would establish that the disciplinary authority, and on appeal the 
appellate authority, being fact-finding authorities have exclusive power to consider the evidence with a view to 
maintain discipline. They are invested with the discretion to impose appropriate punishment keeping in view the 
magnitude or gravity of the misconduct. The High Court/Tribunal, while exercising the power of judicial 
review, cannot normally substitute its own conclusion on penalty and impose some other penalty. If the 
punishment imposed by the disciplinary authority or the appellate authority shocks the conscience of the High 
Court/Tribunal, it would appropriately mould the relief, either directing the disciplinary/appellate authority to 
reconsider the penalty imposed, or to shorten the litigation, it may itself, in exceptional and rare cases, impose 
appropriate punishment with cogent reasons in support thereof." 


Further, in the case of General Secretary, South Indian Cashew Factory Workers Union vs Kerala State 
Cashew Development, 2006 LLR page 657 Hon’ble Supreme Court have held that if the enquiry is fair and proper 
and in the absence of any allegation of victimization, the Labour Court had no jurisdiction to interfere in the order of 
punishment. 


Further, reference of the case of the United Bank of India vs. Bachan Prasad Lal Civil Appeal No. 2949 of 20 
date of decision .2.2022, is relevant in this context. The facts of the case are that workman CSE was the bank 
employee and he fraudulently prepared 9 credit transfer vouchers on various dates on the pretext of payment of 
interest towards fixed deposits and crediting the whole amount to one saving account opened in the name of one 
Smt.Asha Devi (admittedly the fake account prepared by Respondent employee). In order to adjust the said amount 
he manipulated the other book records of the bank using forged signatures. After such nature of allegations stood 
proved, the disciplinary authority after taking into consideration the record of inquiry and the post held by the 
Respondent employee, imposed the punishment to employee with the penalty of dismissal from service. In that case 
Hon’ble Supreme Court have held:- 


ढ#॥7, In our considered view, looking into seriousness of the nature of allegations levelled against the 
Respondent employee, the punishment of dismissal inflicted upon him in no manner could be said to be 
shockingly disproportionate which would have required to be interfered with by the Tribunal in exercise of its 
power under Section //4 of the Act 7947, At the same time, merely because the employee stood superannuated 
in the meanwhile, will not absolve him from the misconduct which he had committed in discharge of his duties 
and looking into the nature of misconduct which he had committed, he was not entitled for any indulgence. The 
Bank employee always holds the position of trust where honesty and integrity are the sine qua non but it would 


592 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : JULY 2, 2025/ASHADHA 2, 947 [PART II—SEC. 3(ii)] 


never be advisable to deal with such matters leniently. 


2, Consequently, the appeal succeeds and is allowed. The interference made by the Tribunal and the High 
Court in the impugned judgment is hereby set aside. No costs.” 


Further, the reference of the SBI vs Hemant Kumar 20]! (4) SCC 355, is relevant therein workmen worked in the 
appellant bank as cashier cum clerk and it was discovered that the Respondent had been indulging in misappropriation 
of money by making fictitious entries and manipulations in the bank’s ledgers. The departmental enquiry was 
conducted against the Workman and Enquiry Officer submitted his report holding the Respondent guilty of all the 
charges. The appeal was also dismissed. CSE workman preferred appeal against the order of Disciplinary Authority 
and during the pendency of the appeal CSE workman submitted a letter admitting his guilt in writing, presumably 
hoping that a lenient view would be taken in the appeal but the appeal was also dismissed. The matter was referred 
for adjudication to the Tribunal and Tribunal set aside the dismissal order of the bank employee and ordered for 
reinstatement with full back wages. In that case Supreme Court have held:- 


#॥॥, The second reason assigned by the Tribunal that the Enquiry Officer should have allowed the Respondent the 
opportunity to lead evidence in rebuttal is also without substance in the overall facts of the case. The Respondent had 
already tendered two admissions of guilt in writing and one orally before 77.7 and there was hardly anything that 
could be said on his behalf to repel the charges. 


42. We are, therefore, satisfied that the Tribunal's findings are wholly unreasonable and perverse and fit to be set 
aside. The High Court, unfortunately, did not consider the matter as it should have, in light of the discussions made 
above. The High Court's order is equally unsustainable. We, accordingly, set aside the order passed by the High 
Court and the award made by the Tribunal. The appeal is allowed but with no order as to costs.”’ 


4. At this juncture it would be pertinent to mention the para 5(j) of the bipartite settlement dated 0.4.2002 
which provides, 


“5. By the expression” gross misconduct” shall be meant any of the following acts and omissions and the part of 
an employee: 


Para(j):- doing any act prejudicial to the interest of the bank or gross negligence or negligence involving or likely 
to involve the bank in serious loss;” 


Para 6 of the bipartite settlement provides, 
“6, An employee found guilty of gross misconduct may: 
(a) be dismissed without notice; or 


(9) be removed from service with superannuation benefits i.e., pension and/or Provident fund and gratuity as 
would be due otherwise under the rules or regulations prevailing at the relevant time and without disqualification 
from future employment; or 


(c) be compulsorily retired with superannuation benefits i.e., pension and/or Provident fund and gratuity as would 
be due otherwise under the rules or regulations prevailing at the relevant time and without disqualification from 
future employment; or” 


Thus, the Petitioner Workman has been found guilty of committing misconduct and has been awarded the 
punishment as per the bipartite settlement para 6(c) and there is no illegality or infirmity and perversity in the 
impugned order of punishment. 


5. Moreover, the record of enquiry goes to reveal that the Petitioner has submitted his written reply dated nil in 
response to the chargesheet dated 5.8.2009 and in his reply he has admitted that he has made some entries by mistake 
in usual and routine manner by using his ID and all entries were authorised by Manager, he had neither used the user 
ID of the Manager nor authorised the entries as alleged. Further, he stated that as the balance was available in his 
account he has availed the amount and when it came to his notice that there was a wrong credit in his account he had 
adjusted the amount. Further, he stated that he had not done any fraudulent transaction and there is no loss either to 
the bank or to anybody as the amount was appropriated. Thus, in his reply to the chargesheet he has admitted that on 
25.0.2008 at a given time in the charge sheet he has created and entered the transaction unauthorisedly using ID of 
the Manager as detailed in the charge sheet. Thus, in view of the documentary and oral evidence on record and also 
admission of the Petitioner Workman, it is established that workman has committed gross misconduct as described in 
Para 5(j) of Bipartite Settlement and there is no illegality or infirmity in the punishment order passed by the 
Respondent bank. 


6. Thus, in view of the law laid down by the Hon'ble Apex Court as discussed above, in the present matter there 
is ample evidence on record apart from admission made by CSE in support of the charge against the Petitioner and 
disciplinary authority has rightly imposed the punishment of “compulsorily retired with superannuation benefits i.e., 
pension and /or provident fund and gratuity as would be due otherwise under the rules are regulations prevailing at 
the relevant time and without disqualifications from future employment” by taking lenient view. In the facts and 
circumstances of the case in my considered opinion the punishment imposed upon workman is not disproportionate 
and rather commensurate to the gravity of charges levelled against CSE workman. 


[भाग II—ave 3(ii)] भारत का राजपत्र : जुलाई 2, 2025/आषाढ़ 2, 947 593 


This issue is decided accordingly against the Petitioner workman and in favour of the Respondent. 


7. Issue No.III:- In view of the fore gone discussion and finding arrived at Issue No.II, the Petitioner has 
been found guilty of committing misconduct under Para 5(j) of Bipartite Settlement and has rightly been punished 
with order of “compulsorily retired with superannuation benefits i.e., pension and /or provident fund and gratuity as 
would be due otherwise under the rules are regulations prevailing at the relevant time and without disqualifications 
from future employment”. Therefore, he is not entitled for any relief and his petition is liable to be dismissed. 


This issue is answered accordingly. 


AWARD 


In view of the fore gone discussion and finding arrived at Issue Nos.I & IH, I am of the considered view 
that the action of the Respondent in imposing the punishment of “compulsorily retired with superannuation benefits 
i.e., pension and /or provident fund and gratuity as would be due otherwise under the rules are regulations prevailing 
at the relevant time and without disqualifications from future employment” to the Petitioner Sri PJC. Bhanudev is 
legal and justified. Hence, the Petitioner is not entitled to any relief as prayed for. As such, the petition filed by the 
Petitioner deserves to be dismissed as devoid of merits. Therefore, the petition is dismissed. 


Award is passed accordingly. Transmit.
